So why don’t you spare me your lecture about growing up? I worry about myself, just the way you taught me, because all you think about is you, and all she does is think about herself. So, there you have it, I’m just like you … DAD!”
“I’m through talking with you. You have no respect. You are not worth my time.”
“That’s right, I’m not profitable, so you have to go play with your company. Go on, I don’t need you. You’ve hated me ever since the accident.”
“Don’t ever bring up the accident! I have to go, but you’re grounded. Don’t even think about leaving this house tonight!”
Dorian didn’t answer. He didn’t have to. He was not going to allow any orders from his dad to get in the way of his plans.
Dorian Brendon was a senior at Memorial High School. He and his group of friends were constantly looking for their next thrill. Recently, he had introduced the group to the occult. He was fascinated by the idea of conjuring a demon. At first, he thought it was all just bull crap. Then he came across a book on demonology and the occult. He found it online on the dark web. His father would freak out if he knew he had purchased the software to access those sites. There was a whole world in this hidden portion of the web dedicated to the occult. In the chat rooms, he was referred to a site where he could get THE book. It had no name, just a modified inverted pentagram. As he understood it the inverted pentagram represented the devil or demons. The rituals described the methods for drawing an inhuman entity into the world of humans. Dorian was intent on attempting to call forth an entity to do his bidding. 
He had no real idea of the powers to which he was exposing himself and his friends. The whole thing was making Crystal nervous. She had just arrived after Dorian’s father left.  She was sitting with him in his room, talking.
“So, your dad’s pretty upset with you.”
“Who cares? He is just embarrassed he can’t bully me like he does his employees.”
“You have done some interesting things this year. I mean, who gets picked up for drunk driving in a stolen car?”
“If the car wasn’t available for anyone to take, they shouldn’t have left the keys in it.”
“What about the graffiti on Mr. Kendall's house?”
“If a teacher has the right to grade us, we should have the right to grade them. I gave him a D. I could have failed him. I think it’s time we take our pact up another notch. We have been playing with the stuff from the book. I am ready to get real with it.”
“Dorian, what are you going to do next? It seems there isn’t a thrill you won’t go after. That stuff really scares me.”
“I’ve been reading a lot about that kind of thing. I think we could actually call something forth if we try. Think about the power that would be. You can’t chicken out now, not after all we’ve already done. I think the rest are ready for the next step.”
“You know, I’m with you, I have been since before we started school. Do you remember that?”
“Sure, your Dad was the first agent in my mom’s real estate company. We used to play in the back room at her office.”
“That was fun. We’ve been through so much together. I can’t believe we graduate in May. So many changes over the years. We had those little houses in Eau Claire; now you are out here living in this huge place.”
“Yeah, I call it the guilt house. Dad had this built for Mom after his first affair. It is big and designed and decorated by the best. But it is cold; there is no warmth of home here. About the only good thing about it is that it is close to the Lockwood. I think if we do our ritual out there, the extra energy will help it work.”
“The guy out there has kicked us out every time we’ve tried stuff there.”
“I think we can take care of him.”
“What do you mean by that?”
“Some things are best unsaid.”
Crystal just looked at her friend. She didn’t know how far Dorian would go. He had been getting more and more rebellious over the last few years. He hated his parents. All the money in the world can’t replace love and time. His parents had offered precious little of either in a long time. Dorian was often left alone; it had been that way since he was about twelve. That was when the accident with his brother happened. Dorian never talked about it; it was like it never happened. But his parents didn’t want much to do with him after the incident occurred. He always had anything he wanted except time with his parents. They had come to believe that expensive toys were a perfectly good replacement for giving of themselves. Her thoughts were interrupted by the ringing of the doorbell. 
Dorian checked his phone to see who was at the door. It turned out to be the rest of his little group. Crystal’s brother Ben, Bobbie Murrey, and Dan Zinkie. Dorian’s friends were, for the most part, kids looking for attention or just hunting thrills. Crystal’s brother Ben wasn’t good in the classroom, but was a wizard on computers. Bobbie was the daughter of the State Bank president. She had sought attention in several ways throughout high school. She became the easy girl as a freshman. When she got tired of being used, she found her current group. Dan was a wild man. He spent more time in the detention room than he did in classrooms.
Crystal went to open the door.
“Hi, guys. Dorian is upstairs.”
“Is he working on the 'project’ we’ve been talking about?”
“Yeah, Dan, he wants to get back out to the Lockwood and try the full ritual.”
“Did he find the sigil for Balsadore?”
“Don’t say that name!”
“Ah, come on, Crystal. You always get so jumpy about this stuff. Can you imagine the wild time we would be part of if this works?”
“Yeah, Crystal, this is our time. Nobody cares about us. It’s time we made some noise.”
“Bobbie, do you really understand what we are messing with? I mean, we’ve played with the stuff. We’ve drawn symbols out at the Lockwood and other stuff, but actually trying to call forth a demon, that’s going too far, isn’t it?”
“Sis, you could be overreacting. Do you really think we are going to get this ritual right?”
“Ben, I think Dorian is committed. When he puts his mind to something, it happens. I don’t like where this is headed. The stuff with the animals is too creepy.”
“You are being too dramatic.”
“Tell me that when we can’t control what happens.” 
“I’m sorry, I don’t really think he can do it. He is just being Dorian. He has everything in life a guy needs, but it is never enough. He always has to be the victim. This isn’t going to work, and it will be your fault or mine or theirs. It is never Dorian’s fault.”
“I would have to agree with Ben. Lately, the stuff he is doing isn’t fun anymore. We aren’t just sticking it to the school or parents. He thinks we need to … I don’t know, destroy the world, I guess. I think the guy has visions of superpowers.”
Bobbie just wanted to get on with the night and have some fun.
“Come on, guys, let’s just go upstairs, this is all just for laughs anyway.”
Dorian was waiting for the group to get to his room. He had a large space with a full sitting area around his video equipment. On the table in front of the couch, he had the book of incantations he had bought off the dark web. Over the past eighteen months, he and his friends had gotten progressively deeper into the occult. Dorian loved the theatrics of rituals and the idea of conjuring a demon to exact his wrath on the world.

Chapter 3


        Deputy Sally Thomas pulled off County Trunk Z onto a gravel driveway that led back to a small cabin buried in the woods of north central Wisconsin. The cabin was the home of Dober Grumby. Dober had lived there for most of his seventy-five years on this earth. He had been born in a converted chicken coop on a farm about twenty miles to the northeast of his current cabin. Over the ensuing seventy-five years, he was never more than fifty miles from his home, except for the four years during his military service in Vietnam. Since he returned, he had no interest in being around or involved with other people.
“You don’t need to get out of your car.”
“Mr. Grumby, I’m Deputy Sally Thomas. I just have a few questions for you.”
“Right now, you are trespassing. Unless you have a warrant, you have no business here.”
“Please, Mr Grumby, there is no need for that. I just want to know about the raccoon at the Lockwood.”
Sally was looking at the cabin. She was particularly interested in the raccoon pelts that were hung on the exterior wall to dry. There were other animal pelts as well. The deputy knew trapping or hunting raccoons was legal for Wisconsin residents for another three months, so having them was not actually a problem. It seemed too coincidental. 
“Mr. Grumby, if I have to go get a warrant, I’ll be turning over every part of your place. I don’t think you want that. We can settle this right now if you just allow me to come closer and ask you a few questions.”
The man in the cabin was having no part in talking with the deputy and was not very happy about being threatened. Sally saw the barrels of a shotgun stick out of the cabin window. The person holding the gun cocked both barrels in the deputy’s full view.
“I told you, you are trespassing. You have now threatened me with an unlawful search. Under the Wisconsin Castle Doctrine, I have the right to defend my property, even if it takes deadly force. So I am offering you the opportunity to leave now. Otherwise, I am going to use my rights to defend my person and my property. Have I made myself clear?”
“You have made yourself clear. Let me make myself clear. I will be back with a warrant.”
“You need reasonable suspicion of a crime having been committed or in the process of being committed. I defy you to present evidence that I have done anything illegal. You know that you have nothing on me. This was a big fishing trip. Go on back to civilization and leave this old man alone!”
Sally had actually been bluffing. No judge would grant a search warrant on her gut feeling. There was no evidence or signs of brutality to animals. She would need more than the fact that he wanted to be left alone. 
“I’m leaving, but I will be back. If I find you had anything to do with mutilating that raccoon, I promise I will bring down the full force of the law.”
She took a photograph of the raccoon pelts on the cabin while seated in her vehicle. The man in the cabin fired one shot in the air. That was not illegal since it was on his land and endangered no one. She began to back out of the lane to his cabin. Once back on County Trunk Z, Thomas turned toward the Eau Claire County Sheriff’s Office.
It took nearly half an hour to get to the office. On her drive, she thought about Grumby. What was his problem with talking to her? Was he hiding something, or just that private? Barney had said he was not one for talking to people easily. She could not get the feeling that the man was weird out of her mind. Living alone at his age was risky; he could have dementia, and who would see the signs until it was too late? 
She understood loneliness. One can feel that anywhere. It is not a condition that depends on solitude. Being around people is not the same as being with people. She had not been with a person in years. She needed the feeling that someone cared about her. She was alone in the world and had been most of her life. 
Barney had been her person. Then he found someone else and created a life without her. Perhaps it would have been different if she had been more open about her feelings for him back then. She allowed him to think her interest was only as a friend. She had been focused on her career, and she had a problem expressing her feelings. One could call it being shy, but it was more. She had seen her parents grow to hate one another; she was afraid that would happen to her as well. Neither of them had been good at being in a relationship, so why would she be any better?
Her shift was ending as she pulled into the office. She spoke with a couple of the other deputies and then drove to her apartment. It was actually half of a duplex. Her landlord lived in the other half. They were a retired couple who were always very interested in what had happened each day at her work. She often tried to sneak into her home just so she wouldn’t have to have a conversation with them. They were nice folks, they were just a little bored and looking to hear something exciting. She parked on the street and walked to her side door. She was nearly inside when she heard her landlord, Mr. Bartles. 
“Hi, Sally, busy day at work?”
“Oh, hi, Mr. Bartles. Not much is going on today. I just came from Dober Grumby’s.”
“Really? We heard on the scanner that there was a wreck down 37. Do you know if anyone was hurt?”
“I wasn’t on that call. I’m not sure if anyone was injured or hurt.”
“It sounded pretty bad; they had two ambulances out there.”
“See, there, you know more than I do. I was on the other side of the county most of the day. Just talking to people about the raccoon someone abused.”
        “Dober abused a raccoon? What did he do?”
        “I didn’t say Dober did it. I’m still working on the investigation. I shouldn’t say anything more.”
        “I understand, but the reason I’m asking is that a few months back, there was a raccoon that showed up on my cousin’s farm that had been tortured. Its eyes had been gouged out of its head, but it was still alive. Whoever did it turned it loose to wander in the woods. It made its way to my cousin’s barn.”
“So, you never reported that?”
“No, I thought maybe my cousin would, we thought it was maybe some kids messing around playing nasty pranks. We saw a group of about five or six of them on the backside of the Lockwood Sanatorium. His farm shares a property line with that place.”
“Okay, let me get this all straight. Sometime around, what, like September, this happened?”
“Yes, I think so.”
Mrs. Bartles was wondering where her husband had gotten off to, so she came around the corner to check on him.
 “Hi, Sally dear, how was your day? What have you two been talking about?”
“Hi, Mrs. Bartles. Your husband was telling me about the raccoon at his cousin’s farm.”
“Oh, that poor thing. It was simply brutal what someone did to that animal. That is actually MY first cousin.”
“Well, that makes him my cousin as well.”
“I guess so, but not a blood cousin.”
“Okay, okay, I’ll let the two of you define the relationship later. Right now, I need all of the facts about the raccoon. You see, the one I was looking into had the same thing happen to it; the eyes were gouged out.”
“Oh my god, there was a second one that was mutilated like that?”
“Yes, Mrs. Bartles. That is why I need to know everything about the one on your cousin’s farm. So it took place in September, and the farm is adjacent to the Lockwood. Is that right so far?”
“Yes, that is absolutely right. He put it in a rabbit hutch he was no longer using. He fed it and cared for it for nearly a month. Then it got out of the cage somehow, and the next time he saw it, the poor thing was dead and half-eaten. Who do you think is doing this?”
“I couldn’t tell you that if I knew. It is an ongoing investigation.”
“Ooo, an investigation, that sounds exciting. Is there any way we could help? Do you want me to call my cousin?”
“I may need his address and phone number, but that will be the extent of any assistance I would want you to get involved in. I am concerned that someone who is doing something like that may graduate into much worse types of cruelty.”
“Do you mean to animals, or are you saying there is someone out there that might be ready to do something like that to people?”
“No, no, there is no need to start talking about that. I just want to find whoever is doing this to raccoons and get it stopped, okay?”
“Well, I’ve seen shows on the television that were doc … documents isn’t the right word. Ahh … what were they called?”
Mrs. Bartles was addressing the question to her husband.
“I don’t know for sure where you were going with that. Did you mean documentaries?”
“That’s it! Documentaries are what I meant. I’ve seen some of those about killers, and some started out killing and mutilating small animals. I think that is what you’re worried about, isn’t it?”
“Mrs. Bartles, there is no point in getting all stirred up about something that probably has more to do with those kids your husband said have been seen out at the Lockwood than it does some would-be killer. Let’s not go getting rumors started.”
“Rumors! I would never do that, dear. What we talk about stays right here between us, that’s all there is to it. I think we'd better let Sally get into her house and get some rest. It sounds like she is going to have a lot of work to do tomorrow.”
The couple started walking to their side of the duplex.
“Goodnight, Sally.”
“Yes, dear, goodnight.”
“Goodnight.”
Sally walked into her apartment. She had hoped it would feel warmer, but she remembered she had turned the thermostat down to sixty-two degrees during the day. She immediately turned it up and went to put on her sweatpants and a hooded sweatshirt. As she changed, she thought about the fact that there had been a second raccoon mutilated in that fashion. It was really starting to bother her that someone was out there doing such things to animals. Though she had attempted to quell Mrs. Bartle's concerns that whoever did it might hurt a human, she was afraid that was exactly what could happen.


*****


        The dark cabin had a smell of sweat and the coppery scent of blood, which was mixed with the musty, rotting stench of an old building. In the center of the room was an old wooden table. The top had a large, deep red stain smeared across it. In the center of the top, a large cleaver was stuck into the wood. A shadowed figure inhabited the edges of the room, moving slowly, methodically. The figure reached into a cage on the floor and snatched up a small creature. The beast fought, biting and clawing at the gloved hands with no effect. It was slammed onto the table with such force as to stun the animal. Four ropes, each tied to a leg of the table, were flipped onto the table one at a time. Slowly, as if relishing the torment being inflicted on the creature, each leg was tied and stretched out until the animal was spread-eagled on the table top. Its head was rolling side to side, fighting its constraints, teeth gnashing, as it growled and hissed.  
The little beast was forced to stay there, perhaps as entertainment for the shadowy figure. Minutes passed as it fought hopelessly against the ropes. When the show grew wearisome, a gloved hand clamped down on the creature’s head. The other hand came forth with a heated knife, the blade glowing red. With deft movement, the knife plunged into the animal’s left eye, eliciting a screeching howl. The eye itself was popped onto the table as the heated blade cauterized the wound with a sickening sizzle. The act was repeated on the right eye, again drawing the anguished cry from the trapped prey.
The deed was completed, and the animal lay on the table writhing in pain, exhausted from the ordeal. One gloved hand held the victim down while the other untied the ropes. The beast was thrown back into the cage, and the figure grabbed a bucket of water and splashed the table to remove the remnants of his brutality.
The shadow moved to the corner of the room, taking a seat to observe the recent modification to his victim. He breathed a sigh as the rush was so short-lived. He was tired of the same outcome. 
“These little vermin are of no challenge for me. My work on them no longer offers the rush and reward it once did. I need more. I need a victim who can verbalize their feelings, who can plead for their eyesight before I take it. That would be the ultimate opportunity, the perfect control that I seek. It is time to make my next move toward gaining perfection. I need to show them all, don’t I, Father?
“Why do you never speak when I talk to you? Only when I try to sleep do you chatter about your disappointment in me. Every day you tear me down. You’ve done that since I can remember. You have always blamed me for Daryl. I didn’t hurt him. He lit that fire! I didn’t make it blow up in his face. I still remember his eyes melting as his face burned. I rolled on him to put it out. You should have done something, called the doctor, anything. He lay here for four days in agony. It was you who let him die. I remember, even though it was all those years ago.
“You chattered then about him getting tougher. He wasn’t man enough for you. Neither of us was. I still hear you. That chattering is always about wanting me to be stronger, tougher. You have never been happy with who I am. I will show you, you’ll see just how important I am.” 


*****


        Betty Bartles could not wait to get back to her apartment. As soon as she walked in, she pulled her phone out of her apron. She immediately called her friend Dorothy. Her friend ran the local beauty parlor and was noted as the news source for the whole area. Some may say she was a gossip, but that would be unfair. In a small town, the continuity of the community, the glue, is based on a shared worldview. That has to be perpetuated somewhere. The burden generally falls on locations where the interaction of members of all stations converges. Though times are changing, the adults in a community still frequent specialists for at least one common act. Hair parlors and barber shops still provide a common ground in most communities. Though the names have changed, hair stylists and grooming lounges, along with thousands of catchy names that replace the generic monikers, are still the conduit of information for many small-town inhabitants.
“Hello, this is Dorothy. Is this personal or for the shop?”
Dorothy worked out of her home. She had a front room set up as her shop and lived in the rest of the place. She only had one phone for the shop and her personal calls.
“Hi, Dorothy. This is Betty Bartles. Have I got news for you.”
“Oh, that is good. There hasn’t been much to talk about lately. I hope it’s juicy.”
“This isn’t gossip, mind you. This is actual police business.”
“Oh, this isn’t about the accident, is it? I have heard all of that story; no one was seriously hurt.”
Dorothy’s voice sounded a little disappointed with the lack of bodily injury.
“No, it has nothing to do with that. Someone is abusing raccoons!”
“Okay, so we have a sicko in the community who doesn’t like animals?”
“It’s not just that. The sheriff’s office is questioning Dober. Have you seen the shows about killers? They always look for when and how they were triggered into killing. It always starts with abusing small animals.”
“Are you saying Dober is a killer, or going to be one?”
“No, I didn’t say that. One could come up with that conclusion, though, I guess. Of course, there are those kids.”
“What kids?”
“There are some kids who have been messing around in the woods. I suppose they could be involved.”
“Well, now it is getting juicy. Dober, a killer. I just thought of him as sort of the town dunce, but not a killer. It takes all types, doesn’t it?”
“It sure does. Well, I thought you should know.”
        The role of these society-leveling establishments is still quite clear. They act as the conduit through which information is transmitted community-wide. The problem that arises, of course, is that once the word is passed along, the continued versions are altered in the hands of those less practiced at repeating the stories verbatim. Like a giant round of telephone, the children's game where a phrase is shared by whisper from one child to the next until it makes it around the room, resulting in an entirely different phrase, so is the spread of news, once out of the hands of the beautician.


*****


        Barney thought about his conversation with Sally. He realized he shouldn’t have been so upset with her. She was just trying to do her job, but it bothered him that he would be responsible for Dober being disturbed. He knew the older man eschewed contact with most people. He was a private man. He did not associate with anyone near his home. That was an off-limits location. Dober had made that very clear. Even Barney had never been inside his cabin, and he was the one person that Dober considered a friend. Sally was going to anger him just by wanting to ask questions on his property. He hoped Dober would not hold the intrusion against him.
        “Okay, it’s time I do a walk-through of the sanatorium. I feel silly that I talk to myself when I’m doing this. However, it makes me feel better, like I have someone with me. Sometimes I sing. That is pretty sad, because I cannot sing. I don’t believe in ghosts, but the creepiness of that building at night gets close to being more than I can handle. I would think I’d be used to it by now.”
        Barney walked out of his housing unit at the end of the facility and began his trip through the building, entering the basement tunnel.
        “I hate this tunnel. It smells down here, and the air is always damp feeling, even this time of the year when it dries out with the cold. This is where I see that damn shadow figure all the time.”
        The flashlight Barney was carrying started to flicker. He hit the side of it, trying to jar the batteries and reset the light. 
        “What is going on with this light? I just put new batteries in this thing. It can’t be running low. I brought more batteries, but do I stand here in the dark changing them?”
        He shook the flashlight a few more times, but it was flickering continuously. He had to change the batteries. He grabbed them in his pocket. His hands were shaking. Barney had never been this disturbed by the walk-through. He took a deep breath and rotated the base of the light, and it went out. He fumbled in the dark to take the three batteries out and replace them. It took about two minutes to complete. Just as he was about to twist the end back on the light, he felt a hand on his shoulder. That made him jump just as the light came on. He swung around, looking for who or what had just touched him. 
“Who is here? Show yourself! You need to get out of here right now. You are trespassing.”
        Barney was angry and searching in earnest for whoever he had felt. He walked quickly through the lower level of the building and then moved up to the main floor. 
        “If I find you, all I will do is ask you to leave. There is no reason to hide. This is private property. It is my job to keep people out, for their own safety. Just show yourself, and I will walk you out.”
        Barney stopped when he got to the “Grand Central” spot of the building. That was what he called it anyway. This was where the hallways fanned out in the three directions of the building. From that central spot, he could look down to the end of each hallway. He directed the beam of light down on each one. As he swung the light down the second hall, he caught the dark shadow of a figure running into the room at the end. After the initial shock of seeing the figure, he ran toward it, believing he would finally catch them.
        “There is no point in trying to hide in there. You have nowhere to go. Just come out, and we can end this game.”
        Barney stepped to the doorway of the room.
        “Alright, the game is over. It’s time for you to leave!”
        His light made its way around the room searching for the person he was chasing. There was no one there.
        “How is this possible?”
        Barney stood there, dumfounded by the shadow figure’s disappearance, when he heard a breathy, taunting voice.
        “Bar-ney … Bar-ney”
        Unsure of what was happening, Barney slowly stepped back out of the room. As he turned to retreat up the hallway, he saw the shadow figure standing in the middle of “Grand Central”, where the three hallways merged. It stood there as if he wanted to make certain Barney saw it and then moved down the right hall. Barney tried to wrap his mind around what had just happened. The figure had glided out of view rather than seeming to run away.
        “That is it. I’ve had enough of this for one night. I will check the building again in the morning, but this is not right. I’m done, and out of here.”
        He made his way as quickly as he could to the shelter of his apartment. Once there, he turned on the television and left it on all night. He didn’t want any voices or sounds that weren’t under his control. What had just happened? That was far more intense than the occasional glimpses of shadows he had experienced up to that point. If there wasn’t a person there, what was it? He had to be over tired. That was his only rational explanation for the events that had just unfolded. It would all be better in the morning.


*****


        Two days later, the story had made the rounds of the most diligent members of the community hotline. Betty was making coffee when the phone rang. It was Thelma Davies.
“Hello.”
        “Betty, you will never guess what I heard.”
“Hi, Thelma. What’s going on?”
“This is big. Do you know Dober Grumby?”
“Sure, he lives over by the Lockwood in a cabin.”
“He killed someone, I think it was a burglar, because they had a mask. That’s not all. He also molested some teens out in the woods.” 
Betty did not stop to think that what she was hearing was a bastardized version of her own tale, told to Dorothy.
“Oh my goodness, have the police arrested him?”
“No, they say they are still investigating. I don’t understand the law today. How could they let this maniac wander around? They don’t care enough about the safety of the community.”
“I agree, I’ll have to have a word with Sally Thomas. She is our tenant, you know.”
“Well, I hope you can light a fire under the deputy. We need this taken care of right away.”


*****
 
Sally had not slept well. The whole mutilated raccoon issue had her a little spooked, and the rough way her conversation ended with Barney bothered her. She was sitting at the small counter that faced the galley-style kitchen. She wasn’t a coffee drinker, so she preferred a glass of orange juice. As she sipped it, she thought of how many years she had done this exact act, alone. 
It wasn’t that she hadn’t had a good life. She had always held a steady job, doing work she felt was rewarding and for the good of her community. She had good health and had an abundance of material items based on her humble needs. Companionship was what she lacked. She had plenty of friends, but they were all at arm's length from her. She was not close with anyone, at least not the type of close she longed for.
Introspective thinking always seemed to get her down. She determined there was nothing good that would come of continued self-reflection. She shook it off and went on to her tasks at hand. It had been nearly a week since she had spoken to Barney. Perhaps he had more insight into the issue with the raccoon. It occurred to her that he would not be aware of the second raccoon that was found at Betty’s cousin’s place. She got ready for the day and called in to the office to inform them of her intentions for the morning.
“Dispatch, this is Thomas.”
“Go for dispatch.”
“I am headed out to the Lockwood to do a follow-up with Barney Doyle regarding the animal mutilation.”
“Hi Sally, Morris, here. You may want to swing past Dorothy Cooper’s. There have been several folks calling asking when Dober Grumby will be arrested for the child molestation.”
“Calling for what?”
“Somehow word has made it around the Corners that Dober molested a teenager who was in the woods by the Lockwood after he killed a masked burglar.”
“Where did a story like that come from?”
“You know how gossip spreads. I figure if you let Dorothy know there is nothing to it, we may nip it at the roots. Oh, hey, I kind of made a joke there. Did you get it, hair parlor, nip it at its roots?”
“Yeah, I got it. Good one. I’ll head over to Dorothy’s after the Lockwood. Out.”
“Clear.”
Sally hoped her neighbor was not responsible for the misinformation that was now flowing through the community. She did not trust Dober, but he didn’t deserve to be labeled a child molester or a killer. At least, not based on the information she had. How could she quell the rumors when it was obvious that they had already permeated the community? Perhaps Dorothy was the one best positioned to curb the onslaught of the grizzly false accusations. Sally would stop at her place as soon as she finished her visit to the Lockwood Sanatorium.


*****
        
        Sleep, it’s an odd phenomenon each of us experiences in our own way. For some, it may be a time of vivid dreams, filled with both fantastic fantasy or terrible horror. For others, that brain activity is immediately forgotten, lodged in the subconscious. The rest and revitalization that result from sleep are elements required for a healthy life. Sleep allows the body to rebuild its immunity. It supports normal brain functions, regulates emotions, and maintains hormonal balance. An ongoing issue of insomnia can lead to emotional disorders, irritability, anxiety, and depression. Extended occurrences can cause major mental harm, particularly for persons without strong support systems otherwise. Paranoia and hallucinations are not uncommon for persons suffering from severe sleep deprivation.
Barney Doyle had not slept for four days. Since his episode with the shadow figure, sleep was impossible. He feared the presence of something he could not understand. He did not allow himself to accept the premise that ghosts existed, so he either imagined it happened, in which case he was delusional, or there was a human who was purposely tormenting him. Neither of those conclusions lent him any comfort. He was on a jagged edge. Talking to himself had become a frequent reality for Barney.                                                  
“Why would someone be tormenting me? Who is doing it? I can’t be just imagining these things. Someone is whispering my name, taunting me, repeating it in a school kid’s sing-song method. Why would a ghost do that? Why am I forgetting things? I left the kettle I had started for tea sitting on the stove until the water boiled away. I could have started a fire. I’ve left my keys in several places, and I have trouble finding them. I’m spending time looking for them. It’s such a waste of my effort.”
 He looked the part of a man slowly losing grip with reality. Unshaven and unbathed, he wandered around aimlessly on the Lockwood property. Little to no work was being completed. He was in a constant time warp. Large segments of time often vanished without him realizing it. 
“Okay, Barney, you need to get something done. I need to spend an hour clearing the ceiling tiles that fell on the second floor, east. Let’s see, it's 2:30 pm. Wait, that can’t be right. I just came out here. It shouldn’t even be noon yet. My watch must be wrong. I’ll check my phone.”
Barney pulled his cell phone from his pocket. He shook his head as he saw the time matched his watch. 
“What have I been doing for nearly three hours? I was just standing on the second floor. How did I get in the tunnels? I’m losing it! I need sleep and a decent meal, or did I just wake up? I ate eggs at some point. Was that this morning, yesterday, or the day before? I don’t remember cooking them. Do I just think I have eaten?”
Tears of frustration began to stream down Barney’s cheeks. He had never felt this much out of control. Was it possible he had already lost his mind? Was he even at the Lockwood? He could be strapped to a bed somewhere, imagining all of this. 
“How does a person know if they are sane? Reality is not actually something one can prove. Anything I do right now to prove it could actually be part of the delusion. The veil between real and imagined is very thin. I don’t know if I am me, or if I only think I am me. I could be a made-up person in a made-up world, acting out the fantasy of someone else with no grip on reality. Perception is everything. Without believing in the existence of something, does that thing exist? If no one is looking for me or cares about me, do I exist?”
Barney's mind was a muddle of questions. He was uncertain of the time, unsure of his space, and lost in a confused perception of what was and wasn’t real. He literally sat down in the driveway of the Lockwood, feeling beaten and out of control. He was shaking, as tears ran freely. His vision was blurred, and the sunlight seemed to be bent as if shining through a prism. He felt broken.
“What the heck is going on? Barney?”
Sally Thomas was driving up the Lockwood driveway and just saw her friend Barney sitting in the middle of the road. He was rocking back and forth, rubbing his head as if it hurt. She parked and jumped out of her car. She ran to his side and knelt.
“Barney, Barney, what’s wrong?”
Doyle jumped back, his eyes wide and wild. He acted as though he did not recognize his long-time friend. 
“Look at me, Barney. It’s me, Sally. Come on, settle down and talk to me.”
“No, you’re not real. I won’t fall for your tricks anymore. Don’t pretend to be Sally.”
“Barney, what are you talking about? It is me, Sally. Here, feel me, I am real, it is me, Sally.”
She grabbed him and hugged him tightly. As she held on, Barney went from being tense and pulling away to calming down and realizing who was there.
“Sally, is that really you?”
“Yes, it's me. Now, tell me what is going on. You look terrible. Who was trying to trick you?”
“I … ahh … I don’t know. I found a shadow in the building. I went to chase whoever it was out of the place and … “
Barney’s voice just trailed off as he seemed to retreat into his bewildered mind.
“Come on, let’s get you inside.”
Sally helped him to his feet and guided him to the apartment. She got him situated on his couch and then looked around the place. It was a mess. There were papers strewn around the room. His work clothes were scattered on the floor. There were dishes in the sink that appeared to have been from several days earlier. The furniture was disheveled with blankets and pillows thrown about. On the table were scribbled drawings filled with the same image repeated several times. The sketch was of a man completely obscured in shadows. The drawings were increasingly distorted. It could have been due to the exhaustion diminishing his skills or impacting his perception, distorting his vision of the image itself.
Barney sat staring and mumbling.
“The shadow, I don’t think it is human. It’s not human.”
“Okay, Barney, you have me worried here. I need you to snap out of whatever this is. I need you to try to clear your mind and think. When was the last time you slept?”
“I … I don’t know.”
“When did you eat last?”
“I … don’t know that either. I am lost right now. I … I am so alone, and I don’t know what’s happening.”
Barney’s eyes welled up as he realized how confused he was. 
Sally recalled their last conversation. Barney had mentioned that he had heard voices and seen shadows when he was overtired. Perhaps this was just the result of Barney suffering from exhaustion.
“Barney, I’m going to make you some soup, and you are going to eat and go to bed.”
“I don’t think I can eat right now. I need to clear my head.”
“Barney, you don’t have a choice. You won’t be clearing your head when you are undernourished and overtired. We have to fix the physical problems first. When that is done, we will work on the confusion issues. So, I’m sorry, but you don’t have a choice. While I make the soup, you go to the bathroom and clean yourself up. Don’t think, just do.”
“But, I … ”
“No buts, go do as I say, right now.”
“Well … I … ahh … ”
Sally took him by the arm and led him to the bathroom. She handed him a washcloth and soap.
“Now, wash your face and hands.”
As if by instinct, Barney began to do as he was told. He got cleaned up and changed his shirt. When he looked in the mirror, he saw himself again. He had felt lost, like he wasn’t himself. He had begun to believe he wasn’t himself, that he had somehow morphed into someone or something new. At times, he wasn’t sure he really existed at all. He was not so certain of that now. He walked into the kitchen by the table where Sally had a bowl of tomato soup and some crackers waiting. He sat down and picked up the spoon.
“I don’t remember the last time I ate tomato soup.”
“I’m not sure you have eaten it today either. I’m not much of a cook.”
“It tastes good to me.”
“That would be a compliment if you weren’t starving. Just finish it and eat some crackers. You are looking too skinny, Mr.”
“Thank you for doing this. I … ahh … I don’t know what happened. I’m still confused, … I …”
“We aren’t going to talk about that yet. I fixed up your bed. You are going to sleep for a while.”
“I can’t sleep, I’m afraid that shadow will get in here. I can’t let it get in … I …”
“Shush! I’ll stay here and make sure nothing bothers you while you sleep.”
“You … you are going to stay?”
“Yes, now get in there, and I want you asleep by the time your head hits the pillow.”
There was something about knowing he wouldn’t be alone that made it easier to relax a little. By the time Barney was on the bed, he was ready to close his eyes. The nightmare was ending, and he could sleep.


*****


        “What are these for?”
        “We need to wear them for the ritual. I explained all that.”
        “I feel silly wearing a cloak. What are these symbols on the back and sleeves?”
        “Those are the inverted pentagrams with Balsadore’s image. I told you, we need to be wearing them when he appears. The symbols are what identify you as his minion on earth.”
        “So why is yours different?”
        “Of course it’s different. I am his hand in this world. I will be his high priest. I ordered each of the robes to identify our roles. It will be my job to direct his wrath upon our enemies.”
        “How exactly are we picking our enemies?”
        “We all agreed who they are already. I should think that would be clear. We can not have anyone stand in our way. The world will be ours. No more school, no parents trying to wield their authority. We shall be free to do as we please, take what we want.”
“Are you saying you want to unleash a demon’s wrath on our teachers and parents? What exactly does that mean? Will they be harmed or just frightened? If you are planning on hurting them, I don’t know if we are ready for all that.”
“Crystal, I want you by my side as you have always been. I can’t have you questioning our every act. You understand that, don’t you?”
“I am just concerned we may be going too far. You’ve been up here reading spells and incantations from that book for almost a week. You are hardly eating and never sleeping. I don’t think your head is clear at all. You need to think about what all this could lead to and just take a step back for a minute.” 
Dorian was getting angry with his friend.
“If you want out, just say so! I can’t be bothered with nurse-maiding you through our ascension. Just go, get out of here. I don’t need you!”
“Dorian, this isn’t you talking. You don’t even look like yourself. Just take a break from that book for one day. That’s all I ask, just one day.”
Dorian stepped forward and slapped her across the face.
“You are no longer welcome here. You are an unbeliever. Your spirit is defiling the sanctity of my union with Balsadore. Get out, and never return!”
Crystal looked at Dorian; his face was contorted and red. It didn’t look like her friend. He was getting totally out of control. She couldn’t believe the anger he was exhibiting. That wasn’t Dorian. He had always been so kind and warm with her. For him to turn on her so easily was scary. Tears filled her eyes as she turned and ran from Dorian’s room. She pulled the robe off as she ran down the stairs and out of the house
She was still crying as she got in her car. She felt Dorian was in over his head. If he would just listen to her. She started to drive out of the property. The lane that led to the road was quite long and had some twists and turns. 
Dorian stood at his window watching her drive away. He was reading a chant from the book. He repeated the words over and over as he stared at Crystal’s car. On the third rendition of the chant, he raised both arms and invoked the name of his demonic muse. 
Whether his plea to a dark lord or the fact that Crystal’s eyesight was impaired by the copious tears flowing, she lost control of the car. It swerved into the right ditch along the lane. When she over-adjusted, she drove straight off the road on the left side. With her next attempt to veer back onto the lane, she flipped the car. It rolled four times and settled on the side of a tree. It sat still for a moment as the fuel poured from the gas tank onto the tree and the car. With Crystal groggy and dazed, still buckled in the seatbelt, the car caught fire. She desperately fought to release herself, screaming for help. Dorian could hear the screams from his sentinel-like position, but he chose to remain still and watch as his friend was consumed in the flames.  
Once he was satisfied that his wrath had been carried out, he turned back to his preparation for the ritual he intended to enact within the week on the roof of the Lockwood. Since both of his parents were out of the area on business for the next three days, he didn't need to worry about Crystal’s body or her car. 
He needed to talk with the others and make sure he had no further dissent in their ranks. He thought it best they not see the wreckage, so he decided they should meet at an old warehouse his father owned. It was only used to house a couple of old cars, and the snowplow was used to clear the parking lot of the plastic firm and his mother's real estate office. There would be plenty of room there to rehearse the ritual. He wanted to be sure they got it just right. 
Dorian gathered his materials and the book, loaded them all into his car, and drove to the warehouse. It took less than half an hour to drive to the meeting spot. He wanted to be there early to set all of the material up before they arrived. He was a little concerned about Crystal’s brother, Ben. He was going to ask questions. He had an idea for keeping Crystal’s brother out of the way for a while.
Dorian placed five chairs around a chalk drawing of the inverted pentagram. In the center of the drawing, he placed the sigil of Balsadore, the towering, pale-skinned, bulging-eyed demon who sucks the life force from his victims and bestows power on his hand on earth. The high priest of Balsadore acts as the demon’s hand among the living. The demon’s powers are unleashed upon someone simply by receiving the touch of the high priest. With every touch, invoking the power of the demon, the priest’s being is replaced with Balsadore’s life force until the priest ceases to exist and becomes Balsadore himself. In this manner, one may not control the power of the demon without sacrificing oneself to become the demon.
Dorian knew he would cease to exist as a separate entity from Balsadore. He was willing to accept that fate in exchange for the power he would wield as the demon. It would mean eternal life and dominance over all who had wronged him in life. That was worth the price he would pay. 
The others viewed this all as something to do for kicks. They didn’t truly believe they would see an actual demon rise from the sacrifice they planned to offer. Truth be told, they all thought that part was going to be too gross. Killing a small mammal did not sound fun. They didn’t particularly care about the animal. They just thought it was going to be messy and possibly dangerous. Dorian was insistent that it had to be done. 
Dan and Bobbie were the first to arrive at the warehouse. Dorian greeted them.
“Are you ready for the greatest thrill of your lives? This is going to really be something. When the new moon occurs, we will be on the roof of the Lockwood, creating history.”
“I’m amped about it.”
“It should be lit.”
“Nobody says lit anymore.”
“Give me a break, Dan. I like the term. Okay, so it will be a BANGER.”
Bobbie said the last word in an exaggerated duh kind of voice.
“Guys, it will be amazing, alright? Soon, we won’t need any kind of slang. We will be the rulers of it all. What we say goes, right?”
Bobbie looked at Dan and rolled her eyes. Dorian was really into this whole scene. He seemed to really think he was going to summon a demon. She thought he sounded different, more wild and out of control. He was a little scary. She felt he needed to be more grounded. That was what Crystal always handled.
“Where’s Crystal? I thought she’d be here already.”
“She ran into a little trouble. She won’t be able to join us tonight, but she knows her part. Here, you two can put these on.”
Dorian handed them their robes.
“Wow, these look real. Oh my gosh, this is cool. Where did you find these?”
“I had them custom-made.”
“They are sweet!”
“Hi guys, sorry I’m late.”
Dorian walked over to meet Ben as he entered. He pulled him aside from the others.
“Hi Ben, listen, I have a very special job for you tonight. I need you to do some reconnaissance out at the Lockwood. I need to know the best way to get on the roof when we go out there. If we all go out to that place tonight, we would cause too much commotion. But if you go alone, you can figure it out and lead us on our glory day.”
“Oh, okay. Go out alone, huh. Yeah, sure, I can do that, I guess.”
“Here I got this just for you so you won’t get caught. It is an amulet with a spell of invisibility.”
“Are you kidding me? I won’t be invisible.”
“Of course you won’t, but it makes people confused, so they can’t see you. That way it works as if you are invisible.”
“I just wear it?”
“Yes, that’s all you do.”
“Okay, I’m not sure I fully buy it, but I guess it can’t hurt. Where’s Crystal? I just want to check with her before I go.”
“Oh, she is doing another task. She won’t be here til much later. I’ll let her know where you are. We talked about it this afternoon, so she knows about it anyway.”
“Alright then. I guess that’s okay then. I will be going out there … alone … now.”
“Yep, good luck, I know you will have no problems.”
Ben walked out. It was pretty obvious he did not like the idea of going there alone, but he knew better than to argue with Dorian. The guy had always been difficult to disagree with, but lately, he was close to impossible. 
________________


Chapter 4


        It had been close to seven hours since Sally had sent Barney to bed. The sun was setting, and she needed to take her cruiser back to the office. They weren’t that pleased with her when she called in to say she would not be patrolling or going over to talk with Dorothy. She said she had to take the day as a personal leave to handle something. She had occupied herself with cleaning up the apartment for Barney. In his state, of what could only be described as a breakdown, he had allowed the place to get out of hand. Sally had cleaned the dishes and wiped down all the surfaces. She picked up the trash and threw all of the clothes in the laundry. 
        Domestic work was not Sally’s strong suit, but she had done a better-than-presentable job. She even managed to cook a meatloaf, or at least what she hoped qualified as a meatloaf. It was the smell of the dinner that had finally brought Barney around from his exhaustion-induced slumber.
        “What’s going on? Who is there?”
        Sally heard him yelling from the front room.
        “What is all the racket back here?”
        “Sally? You really are here? I thought I had imagined that. I’m afraid I have been imagining a lot of things lately. I got myself pretty messed up, I guess.”
        “I can’t argue with you there. I was very worried about you earlier. What set you off so bad?”
        “I was doing a walk-through at night. I … ahh … I'm not sure how long ago. Anyway, I did the walk-through, and I started hearing that voice calling my name. That always sets me on edge.”
        “So, you’ve heard it before?”
        “I thought I told you that.”
        “You said you’ve heard sounds and a lady's scream. You didn’t tell me you had heard someone or something say your name.”
        “Oh, well, I have, but just a couple of times.”
        “And you stayed after that happened?”
        “I just thought it was all in my head. I don’t believe in ghosts.”
        “I think we should get that idea straight. The guy that I saw when I got here earlier, in my opinion, believed in ghosts. In fact, he believed they were tormenting him to the point of distraction. I think you would have been in some serious mental trouble if I hadn’t shown up when I did.”
        “I suppose it’s time for me to come clean to myself and say there is a real possibility that something is here.”
        “I think it may be time for you not to be here.”
        “That is silly. I just let my imagination get the better of me. I can handle it now. I just have to manage it better.”
“I’m serious here, I'm going to take a chance.”
Sally took a deep breath; she was not sure how Barney was going to handle what she was about to say. She just knew that if she was going to live with herself after that day, she had to be upfront and throw her cards on the table.
“I care about you.”
“I care about you, too. What does that have to do with me staying on working here?”
“I don’t think you get the picture. I CARE about you. I have for most of my life. I can’t stand by and see the man that I … ahh … CARE about allow this place to destroy him. There are other things you could do. Things that don’t put you in the middle of ghost central here.”
Barney looked into the eyes of the woman across from him. They were glossy and sharp. Her emotions were right there on her sleeve, waiting to be returned or blundered away. He wasn’t sure how to respond. She was his friend, and more. He could easily see himself spending the rest of his days with her. 
“I CARE for you and have for a long time. I guess I was never sure you thought of me in that way. I understand your concern for me. To be honest, I have worried about you in your job for as long as I can remember. You do know it is not a safe career. I know there isn’t much gang violence or hardened killer action out here, but that doesn’t take away the risks. I will consider trying something else, but I can’t leave Patty in a lurch. She is counting on me. I wouldn’t be someone you could … CARE about if I was not willing to hold up a commitment, would I?”
Sally looked at him. He had actually reciprocated her affections, in his own clumsy way. As she thought about it, he wasn’t any clumsier than she had been. Now he was appealing to her logic, and she hated that. She did not want to be logical; she just wanted him to understand her concerns and act on them.
“Okay, so how are you going to stay here and stay safe? I don’t want to see you in the condition I found you in today ever again.”
“I think I can avoid that. I allowed the place to get to me because I did not want to believe in ghosts. The fact that I couldn’t explain the shadow, combined with the voice saying my name, threw me off track. I’m just not going to let that happen again.”
“I hear you talking, but I’m not convinced.”
“I can’t just walk away. I owe Patty more than that. I will tell her I will be going, but I have to give her time.”
“I need to get my vehicle back to the office. I’ve made a meatloaf. Before you take a step out of this apartment, you eat again. I would recommend that you not deal with that building out there at all until tomorrow in the light of day.”
“I don’t think that will be a problem. I am still not very rested, and that meatloaf smells good.”
 “Just remember that smell when you are eating it. When it comes to working around a house, I’m a great law enforcement officer.”
Sally smiled as she spoke. She really would have liked to stay for the evening to see if the two of them could get over the clumsy stage. That was not to be, so she gave Barney a big hug.
“I’ll call you later, okay?”
“I will look forward to it.”
Sally left and walked to her car. As she was about to pull the door shut, she heard something. It seemed like it was coming from the central building of the complex. It sounded like a woman’s scream. She got back out of her vehicle and walked toward that portion of the building. She had her hand on her sidearm as she moved closer. She looked back at the apartment at the end and thought about getting Barney. He was just getting his head clear. If she brought this to him, she could be igniting his anxiety all over again. She decided it was best to check it out herself.
She entered what had been the main doorway of the original building. The doors were only partially in place. The entry opened to a large, two-story grand foyer. A large staircase was straight ahead that led to the second floor and then split to ascend to the third floor. Sally stood at the bottom of the staircase.
“Hello, is there someone up there? This is the sheriff’s deputy. Come out where I can see you.”
There was no response. Sally thought for a moment about what Barney had said. The woman’s scream he heard had to be the wind blowing through pipes or something like that. It could be he was right, except there wasn’t any wind outside. The air actually felt exceptionally dead.
“I’m coming up, don’t make any sudden moves. If you are up there, you would be well advised to come out now.”
She began her rise up the steps. Each one emitted a loud creak as her foot put pressure on it. It seemed like there were sounds all around her. She could attribute the scratching to mice or rats, the flutters to birds or bats, but the sounds of a person walking in the hall above her were difficult to explain away. Her flashlight was powerful and illuminated the entire foyer, creating shadows all around her. She flinched every time it seemed the shadows moved. She assumed it was just her overactive imagination. She spoke under her breath.
“No wonder Barney was in the state he was. This place could give anyone the heebie-jeebies.”
She stood at the top of the stairs and realized that it was where “Grand Central” was located. She walked straight ahead, and a hall extended to the left, one to the right, and a third continued in front of her. She realized this was the spot that Barney saw the shadow after he had raced down to the end of one of these halls. That meant that the shadow figure could have escaped down the stairs. It could have been human. How did it get from the end of the hall to this area without passing Barney? That was the trick. 
The scream had not occurred again since she entered the building. Since it was a phenomenon that Barney had also been hearing, she decided there was no reason to do further investigation. She had already spent nearly twenty minutes looking around and needed to get back to the office. When she talked to Barney on the phone later, she would have to tell him of her experience. She turned to descend the stairs, and something pushed her. She grabbed for her gun and spun around to confront whatever it was. Nothing was there. 
“Did that just happen? Are you here? I can still feel the touch on my shoulder. Show yourself.”
There was no response. Sally moved to the left of the stairs to use the railing. She did not want to get pushed down the steps. She was done. She had no interest in being in the building at all. No one was going to believe her story. She wouldn’t believe her own story. It did happen, though. She swiftly moved through the foyer to the front door and down the outer steps, and walked straight to her vehicle. She sat behind the wheel and tried to make sense of what had just happened. 
Taken as its individual pieces, she could not actually prove anything happened. All she could prove was that she walked into a dark, creepy building and thought she heard things. She had interpreted those sounds as words. Someone else may have only heard noises or entirely different words. She thought she had been pushed, but it could simply be that her mind was so thrown by the circumstances that she imagined it. There was no evidence to tell anyone; all they would do is make fun of her for getting herself spooked in a vacant building.
“Well, Sally girl, you have nothing to tell anyone. I guess I keep this all to myself. No wonder Barney talks to himself out here. This place could drive anyone to it.”
 She paused as she realized she was doing it. She shook her head and started the vehicle. She needed to get herself and Barney as far away from the insanity of this place as soon as possible.           


*****


Patty Robertson was at her office late. She was busy booking reservations for the several venues she managed for the CSPG. During the colder months in the north, her main booking came for the Brantley Hotel in South Carolina, the Bishop Garden Scythe Murder House in Arkansas, or the City of Angels Hospital in Louisiana. These venues were booked solid through the end of February. She was about to wrap it up for the night when the call came through. 
“Hello, this is Patty with Central States Paranormal Group.”
“Hi Patty, this is Don Musgrave.”
“Oh, Hello Don. It is great to finally get to talk with you. I love your show.”
Don was a well-known Internet ghost hunter. 
“Oh, well, thank you, Patty. Glad you watched. I hope you are subscribed to the channel. Remember to hit the like on each episode. I’m calling because my group, the Paranormal Posse, would like to record a forty-eight-hour segment for our internet channel at your Lockwood property in Wisconsin. It would be the three main hunters: myself, Spooky Boy Boo, and Mary the Medium. There will be two camera people as well. We’d arrive in a large camper and would require an electrical hookup. I don’t know how busy you are there this time of year, but we would want the place to ourselves for the whole time. Do you think that will work?”
“That should work out just fine. I will need to know when you are planning to do this. I do want to warn you, we are planning to enclose the building and add cameras over the next summer. Right now, it is definitely open to the elements in several places.”
“The timing was my reason for personally calling. Otherwise, I have people who set these things up. I need to get in there in three days. We are doing a five-stop tour across the country to generate enough content for our March through June segments. Our turnaround in post-production is going to be tight. We had a cancellation in Minnesota, and that left us with a big vacancy for our tour. When I researched spots, the Lockwood seemed like a very underinvestigated location.”
“Wow, that is a quick prep time for us, but I’m sure my guy at the location can get it ready for you. Just come dressed for a Wisconsin winter. We have snow in the forecast there over the next few days.”
“That actually should be a great video. The bleak landscape behind the building will look even more haunting on video. What are we talking about for cost?”
“Our normal rate would be one hundred per hour. Since we are talking about what is normally a slow period for us, what do you think about thirty-five-hundred for the time period?”
“That sounds great.”
“I’m assuming you are handling your food and whatever needs you will have. We don’t have bathroom facilities in the building yet. We do have a porta-potty, but you can imagine what that is like in the winter.”
“We will handle all of our needs, and we have a toilet in the camper. We just need the power line.”
“Okay, so we are talking the 14th through the 18th of February. My guy on premises is Barney Doyle. He will be staying there, but he has an apartment that is attached but not open to the sanatorium. You will be checking in with him. If you need anything, he will be available to help you.”
“Perfect, thanks for accommodating us. I guess there is one other thing. We like to interview people who know about the place we are investigating. Do you think your guy there … I wrote down his name … ahhh …”
“Barney Doyle.”
“Yes, Mr. Doyle. Do you think he would be able to tell us a little about the place on camera?”
“Oh, Barney has had some interesting experiences. He would be great.”
“Great, so I believe we are all set. Thank you, I look forward to seeing the Lockwood. I’ve read so much about it.”
“Okay then, thank you for thinking of us. Goodbye.”
“Goodbye.”
Patty was elated when she hung up the phone. Not only would she be getting income on the Lockwood during a time period that was difficult to book, but she was also going to be getting a ton of exposure for the Lockwood and for the Central States Paranormal Group’s other properties if Barney did a good job of mentioning them.
She needed to tell Barney as soon as possible, so she picked up the phone to call him, and it rang in her hand, startling her. Once she was over the shock, she answered the call.
“Hello?”
“Hi Patty, this is Barney.”
“Barney? I literally was about to call you. I have some great news for you.”
“Oh, well, I have some not-so-good news for you.”
“What? You have bad news? What’s wrong?”
“I’m afraid the Lockwood has gotten to me. I have had a couple of experiences that I could not handle well. I’m afraid I have to give you my notice.”
“Barney, I can’t believe this is happening. I need your expertise. Especially now. We have the Paranormal Posse coming.”
“The who?”
“The Paranormal Posse. They are one of the most popular online shows. They produce content about paranormal investigating. They have nearly a million followers. This is huge. They are coming to the Lockwood on the 14th through the 18th. This could be the start of building the Lockwood into one of the most popular paranormal investigation destinations. I need you until I can find someone who could be trained. Please tell me you can hang in there until after the Posse has been there. What happened that you are so set to leave?”
“I’m afraid I kind of went a little crazy. I was losing time, and I couldn’t sleep. There is something in that building that isn’t human. It shows itself as a shadow and leads me all over the building. I can’t catch it. It appears at one end, and when I get there, it shows up at the other end. It is taunting me by name. There is a woman who screams every night. I’ve never seen her. Something has touched me, too.”
“The last time we spoke, you didn’t believe in ghosts.”
“After my experiences this week, I am a believer. I just don’t know if I can handle much more, that’s all. But I will be here to get the Posse people in, and it should be fine while they’re here. I’ll let them play with whatever is in that building. I’ll just keep the property clear of anyone trying to sneak in.”
“Okay, I’ll work on getting your replacement. I’m sorry it has come to this. I have really been happy with how hard you’ve worked out there.”
“I’m sorry to have to leave. I enjoy the work and don’t mind the alone time, but I am not up for dealing with the haunting.”
“I understand, thanks for staying to help with the group that is coming. They are going to interview you. It would be good to share the experiences you just talked about. Believe it or not, that will actually be great for business.”
“I believe it, I’ve seen the people that come here. They want to be scared and see something they can’t explain. I don’t wish what I experienced on anyone, but if they want it, this place definitely has it.”
“Okay, thanks again for being there to count on. You’ll make sure the halls are cleared for them to do their investigation. They will also need a room that they can lock for all their equipment. Do we have a location that will work for that?”
“Yes, I can put a lock on the second-floor admin office. Then their stuff is not accessible through a window.”
“That will work. They are handling all of their food and other needs. They need an electrical connection in front of the central entry.”
“I have both a 110 and a 220 rigged for that spot. I think they should be set on electrical.”
“You are good. I can’t think of anything else. If something comes up, I’ll call. I’ll also text you their contact phone number just in case. Give me a call once they are there and getting set up. I’ll call Don Musgrave just to check in with him. Other than that, I believe we are set. Thanks, Barney.”
“No problem, I’ll be talking with you. Goodbye.”
“Be safe, goodbye.”
Patty got off the phone a bit less excited for the Paranormal Posse’s visit. She had never heard anxiety in Barney’s voice like that before. She hoped he could hold it together until this tour was over. If need be, she would just shut the Lockwood down after the group’s visit. It was possible that she could use the shutdown to her advantage. She could tell people, “The place was so haunted she was struggling to find someone willing to be out there”. That was a great marketing ploy. She was going to work on using it just right.
Barney got off the phone with a feeling of dread. He had realized that Sally was right. He needed to leave the Lockwood. He had offered his word salad of how he could handle it to Sally. He knew she wasn’t buying what he was saying, and the fact was, neither was he. He was going to steer clear of going inside the building at night. He would walk around the outside of the place to make sure he didn’t see anyone inside or sneaking around outside, but any work he did inside was going to be in broad daylight.


*****


        Sally was just a few minutes from the Sheriff’s office when she received a call from dispatch.
        “Dispatch to Thomas.”
        “Thomas, here.”
        “Sally, are you nearly back to the office?”
        “Yes, about fifteen minutes out.”
        “We just received a call from Doc Blanchard. Someone broke into his van. He needs a deputy to stop by. He is over near Lufkin. Can you take the call? Greg is off on the other side of the County.”
        “Will do. Did he say if anything was stolen or vandalized?”
        “He says he is missing some injectable meds. I didn’t get the full name of it. I figured that would be best handled on site, so we got it accurate.”
        “I should be there in twenty. Out.”
        “Thanks, clear.”
        Sally was hoping to get checked out and maybe head back to Barney’s place. She was not buying his story about being able to handle it now that he was accepting the idea of ghosts. She couldn’t wrap her head around the idea of such things, and she had played tag with something in that building that did not seem human. That building had a vibe that seemed to have her off-center from the moment she entered it. It was like there was a force that was in control, and you were in its grasp just by being there. That made no real sense in the world she was used to.
Her world didn’t have paranormal issues. It had living people who were in trouble. Some were in trouble with the law, and others who needed the law’s help. Those were tangible things. Yes, dealing with people was not always a logical process. No one who commits a crime is acting rationally. It is not rational to assume you will not be held accountable for your actions if they are not within the acceptable range of behavior. Some may get away with it for a while, but eventually most are brought to justice in some way.
Sally was nearly to Lufkin. It wasn’t much. It consisted of just a few houses, somewhat close together on the western portion of Eau Claire County. As she drove up, she could see Doc Blanchard’s van parked along the road. He was sitting inside. Sally got out as the veterinarian exited the front and walked to the rear of the van.
“Chilly out here this evening, isn’t it?”
“Always is this time of year, Doc. What happened? I heard the van was broken into.”
“I was over near the kennels on 37, close to the Lockwood. I had parked along the road to do some work in the whelping barn. When I came out, the door was open. Somebody had popped the lock off.”
“Was anything missing?”
“I could tell someone had looked at all of the injectables in the cage on this side. It looks like whoever was in here was searching for something specific.”
Sally looked at the lock on the back door. There were scratches around it, but she thought there should be more signs if a lock was broken off.
 “Did they find what they were looking for?”
“I’m not sure, but they took a vial of Azaperone.”
“They took one vial, that’s it?”
“Yes, I thought that was pretty odd. If they were specifically after one thing, that is not what I would have expected.”
“What does Azerpain do?
“The name is Azaperone. It is a widely used and effective sedative in pigs. I often use it as a premedicant or in combination with other drugs for minor procedures.”
“How do you spell it?”
“A… Z… A… P… E… R… O… N…. E. It would act as a sedative, but in large doses, it could be dangerous and cause difficulty in breathing.”
“Would a person steal that to get high?”
“No, they would either pass out or struggle breathing.”
        “So, what would someone do with that?”
        “That is my dilemma. I have no idea why they took that particular drug. If they knew what they were doing, and it seemed they did, then there were much better drugs available to steal.”
        “I need to see if there are any fingerprints on the drug cage other than yours, of course.”
        The inside of the van was organized, but there were some odd tools stored for a veterinarian. Why would he have so much rope? The van smelled pretty funky as well. It was a combination of sweat,  blood, and barnyard. The hooded sweatshirt on the back of the passenger seat was stained and stinky. She could tell it had been used for a long time without seeing a washing machine. She chalked it up to Doc being a bachelor; he may not be very good at keeping up on his cleaning. He always seemed a little eccentric anyway. He had such a weird habit of staring at your eyes when he talked to you. 
It took Sally about twenty minutes to check the cage and the door for prints. She found what she thought might be more than one set, so that was a good sign. She wouldn’t know until the lab took a look at her work. If it led to an identification, she would be surprised. The fingerprint kits the Sheriff’s department used rarely pointed at anyone specific. 
“Okay, I think I have what I need. I’ll get you a copy of the report. I assume you have to talk to your insurance carrier and make a claim. I’ll write up the report and get a copy emailed to you for turning in to your insurance. How much of the stuff is in that vial, like how many doses could someone potentially get from one vial?”
“It would be effective for as many as four doses.”
“Alright, we will keep our eyes open for what could be signs that someone used the drug.”
“Sorry you had to come over here this time of day. I would have called earlier, but Doug Olsen here in Lufkin had an emergency with one of his Holsteins. Those animals are too valuable to these guys who run the dairy farms to wait to postpone treatment.”
“I understand. I’ll let you know if anything comes of the investigation or the prints. Watch your email for the call report. Oh, and get the lock fixed on the van door. You're carrying too much stuff in there to leave it unlocked for long.”
“I’ll lock it in my warehouse for tonight and get it repaired tomorrow.”
“Sounds good, thanks, Doc, I’ll be seeing you.”
“Yeah, thanks, Sally. See you.”
Sally got back in the vehicle and turned around to head back to the office. This had been a long day. She figured she would go home rather than back out to the Lockwood. It seemed a little pushy to show up as late as she was going to that night. She knew she was perhaps overanxious to have the relationship formalized. This was the man she had always thought would be in her life. There had been a time when it was impossible. Fate had changed that, and now it appeared he felt they had a future. That made her happy, yet neither of them was very good at the next steps. Besides the issues of being shy and awkward at the whole relationship thing, Barney had the Lockwood problems.
She had to talk him out of staying out in that building. It was not a healthy situation for him. However, she did not want to start their relationship off by demanding that he do as she said. He was a grown man and needed to act as he saw fit. She had no idea how to proceed. It was going to take some thought to find the right words.


*****


Ben felt a shiver run through him as he stepped out of his car. He was parked in a field driveway along County Trunk Z. He would have to cross the farmer's field and enter the woods on the farm property. He would then follow the creek through the hollow. There were a couple of cabins he would need to avoid as he worked his way around to come up to the Lockwood from the backside. 
He had been out to the sanatorium several times, and he knew that the wing that faced the woods would be the least visible from the caretaker’s apartment. He had brought two flashlights. One to use until he got close, and then a smaller one so as not to cast too much light as he looked for a way to reach the roof. He wanted to find an external way to do it. Inside that building at night was too creepy. It was bad enough that he was out there alone; he had no intention of being inside that place without anyone else around. He started to mumble to himself as he walked the perimeter of the building.
“What am I doing here? This place is a nightmare. Why do I let Dorian tell me what to do? If it wasn’t for his relationship with Crystal, I would tell him where he can shove his orders.”
He was looking up at the side of the building, wondering what he was going to do. As he stood there, the hair on the back of his neck stood on end when he heard movement in the woods behind him.
“Who’s there? Leave me alone. I’m just standing here.”
There was no response. Ben stared at the shadows in the underbrush of the forest that started just a few feet from the walls of the building. Ben turned his flashlight onto the woods, but it seemed as though the light was swallowed up by the dark. He searched the woods trying to find where the noise came from. He stared for a long time, trying to decide if he was just going to leave.
“It must have just been a bird or something like that. I hate this. Come on, Ben, just get this done and get out of here.”
He got his focus back on getting on the roof. About halfway down the wing of the building, there was what used to be a fire escape. He walked slowly along the wall, climbing over and around the vegetation that was encroaching on the building. He kept checking the woods as he walked, the feeling of being hunted soaked into his psyche. He had not really talked himself into the noise of being a bird. His nerves were on edge. Just about anything would send him running out of there. Before that could happen, he found himself standing under the old fire escape. The ladder that reached the ground had fallen off the wall, but it was still lying on the ground. The twisted metal appeared to be buried in the dirt and brush. He reached for it, pulling at one end. It lifted a little bit, so he walked to the other end and worked on pulling it up. It took several minutes; finally, with great effort, he managed to pull it away from the tangle of grass and weeds. 
Getting the ladder out of the grass was just the first step; he needed to get it lifted onto the building. He dragged it toward the edge of the wall and started to lift it up. Ben was not a large person, and the weight of the ladder was close to his own. Even in the cold night air, he was beginning to perspire from the work. At one point,  he had it lifted nearly above his head, but it was too much for him. He looked at the iron heap with determination. Maybe if he had it at a right angle to the building and sort of walked it up the wall, he could do it.
He went to the end that would be the bottom and dragged it out perpendicular to the wall. He then lifted the end next to the wall up and toward the wall. That was a minor success.
