He actually had it leaning on the wall about two feet off the ground. He went back and lifted, then dragged and had it over waist high. The concentration on his task distracted him from the fear of whatever he had heard in the woods. He crouched under the ladder and used his back to move it to the next step up the wall. It was now over his head. He needed to go another five feet up the wall to be near enough to the remnant still mounted on the wall to climb from one to the other. He stepped back, catching his breath.
He moved back under the ladder to give it one more big push. He strained as the ladder inched its way up the wall.
“Why is this thing so heavy? Just a little more. If I could just move it a couple more feet.”
As he pushed up and pulled the ladder toward the wall, his head was down. As he exerted himself for one last big movement of the ladder, he realized there was another pair of feet close to his. He dropped the ladder hard against the wall and fell backwards.
“Who’s there? What do you want? Leave me alone, I will leave, just let me go.”
Ben slowly looked up the legs and torso until he saw the face of the person standing there.
“What, who, wait, what are you doing here? I thought I … wait, what are you doing? What is that needle? Ouch, what was in thaaaa … ?”


*****
Dorian was just arriving home from his evening activities. It was nearly 3:00 am, and he was tired, but as he drove up his driveway, the wreckage of Crystal's car greeted him. He could not leave it where anyone could see it. If any of his group were to just think they could stop by to see him, they would surely have questions. He drove on to the large equipment shed where the family stored their lawn equipment. With a property as large as theirs, they needed a tractor to mow the expansive yard. He grabbed a chain and hopped on the tractor. Pulling the car proved a little more difficult than he had anticipated. The tractor was having difficulty removing the burnt-out car from the tree. As the tractor tugged at the car, it dug into the ground until it started to inch its way off the tree.
Dorian was watching intently as he started to move the car. He stopped abruptly when he saw a van driving past the estate. He quickly shut off the lights on the tractor. He watched as the van drove by very slowly, as if the driver was trying to see what was going on. Dorian could make out the signage on the van when it came to the point where it was parallel to where he was sitting on the tractor. It was Doc Blanchard, the veterinarian. Dorian began to mumble to himself.
“Just move on, you old fart. Mind your own business. I don’t know why he needed to be driving around this time of night.” 
Dorian went back to pulling the car. He left the lights out so no one else could get curious. The sound of the wreckage moving was like the scream of a dying animal. Once he got it moving, he drove the tractor to a location out of sight behind the old cabin at the back of the property. The building had stood there for decades. When the house was built, his father had used the cabin as a storage shed for tools. Since then, it has been seldom used. 
As Dorian drove the tractor back to the shed, he wondered what he could do about the site of the wreck to cover up what remained. It was at that moment it registered in his mind that it had been snowing. He looked to the sky as the flakes increased, sticking to the grass and trees, creating a white blanket over everything. He smiled as he watched the evidence of the wreck disappear under the frosty coating. He parked the tractor and walked to the house. He was there alone and would be for the next few days. He entered and climbed the stairs to his bedroom. As he entered, he noticed Crystal’s phone was there on the floor. That was another stroke of luck. Between the snow and now the phone, he was certain that he was already under the protection of Balsadore.
Dorian knew Crystal’s screen lock code. He logged in and started texting people. He sent one to her mother, saying she was leaving town. She fought with Dorian and needed to get away. He sent a similar message to a few of her friends. He figured that would buy him time. When he brought Balsadore forth, the world would be more concerned about survival than where Crystal was. He smiled as his thoughts turned to the power he would have as the earthly hand of his demonic lord.


*****


        Bobbie Murrey and Dan Zinkie were driving away from the “practice” with Dorian.
“Dan, does Dorian seem even more out there than he usually does? I mean, I think he really believes he can summon a demon. The whole idea of sacrificing an animal creeps me out.”
“He’s talking about a squirrel or raccoon, I think. I don’t like the idea either. He does seem pretty strange. I thought it was weird that Crystal didn’t come. Those two are always together. He didn’t seem worried about it. Then there was the little whisper meeting he had with Ben when he got there. It looked like he gave Ben a job he didn’t want to do. Did you see the look he gave Dorian when he left?”
“Are we going to go through with doing the ritual with them in a couple of days?”
“I don’t know. To be honest, at first I thought the robes were cool. Later, when we did the little play-acting thing with the ritual, I felt like an idiot in it. I can’t imagine what I would say if we got caught on the roof at the Lockwood wearing them.”
“If we don’t do it, I think Dorian will be so angry he won’t ever forgive us.”
“That could be true, but we graduate in a few months. I am not sticking around here. I don’t think I care what Dorian thinks. I’ve gone along with him for years. I can’t say it has ever done me any good. For the most part, it has gotten me in more trouble than anything. Now that he is going so loony, it may be time just to break loose. What do you think?”
“I would like to see what Crystal has to say. She would know better than anyone if Dorian is off the wall. If she thinks he is a real nut job, I’m for dumping the whole thing. I’m pretty tired of the whole ‘hand of Balsadore’ thing.”
“Agreed!”
“Wait, I got a message from Crystal.”
“What’s it say?”
“It doesn’t sound like her. She doesn’t write like this, but it says she had a quarrel with Dorian and she is leaving town.”
“It says she is leaving town? Where would she go?”
“I don’t know. I don’t believe she would do that.”
“Who would be writing a message like that on her phone other than her?”
“I don’t know, but I’m telling you it doesn’t sound like her. I mean, quarrel? Since when does she use that word?”
“I don’t know how she writes, but someone would have to have her phone and know how to get into it to send a message like that.”
“I’m scared, something may have happened to her.”
“Oh, just chill. You are getting all upset over nothing. She probably fought with Dorian, and she is just being a drama queen about it.”
Bobbie just shook her head. She reread the message and remained quiet about it, but her gut feeling was that something was wrong.


*****


        Sally had just gotten back to her apartment. It had been a long day. She had planned to call Barney the previous evening, but she was called out for the robbery of the veterinarian’s van. By the time she had written up the call, there had been an accident out on Highway 37. That took three hours to sort out, and then she was pulled out to assist in a domestic dispute outside of Rock Falls. By that time, it was too late to call Barney. She went ahead and stopped with a couple of the other deputies for some food. She hadn’t eaten since she nibbled while making food for Barney the day before. Now, she was exhausted and would be needed on shift in just five hours. 
        She walked in and plopped on her couch. She was too tired to change for bed. She just needed to sit for a second to catch her fifth, second wind of the day, so she could complete her nightly routine before bed. 
Two hours later, she awoke with a stiff neck, having fallen asleep sitting up on her couch. She walked toward the bathroom, rubbing her neck. This was great, she was tired and stiff and had to be at work in three hours. 
“Sally, girl, this is no way to live. I wonder what Barney is doing? I should have just stayed with him. I’m not sure what would have happened if I did. I need to give him space to get his head on straight. That Lockwood has him messed up.”
She climbed into bed and set her phone to wake her in two hours. And then she proceeded to lie awake thinking of what a future with Barney looked like. Would she remain with the sheriff’s department? Would they stay in the area or move elsewhere? They were still in their forties. They had a lot of living yet to do. She knew Barney was not willing to just walk away from his job, but she felt he would eventually. The what's next question was keeping her eyes open.
After spinning the same questions around in her head for nearly an hour, she dozed off. That only lasted for the remaining hour she had until her phone rudely interrupted her dreams. 
“Damn, he was just about to ask me to marry him. I hope that isn’t just a dream. I’ve waited for so long for that to become a reality.”
She dragged herself out of bed and went to stand under the shower until she was really awake. Dressed and on her way to work, she thought about calling Barney to explain why she had missed calling the evening before. The phone rang several times with no answer. She hung up, wondering what was going on. It was 7:00 am. He should be awake by then. He had been overtired, but he would have rested all evening and then had a good night's sleep if he did what he said he was going to do. 
She couldn’t take another personal day to spend out there. She needed to get to the office and take care of the duties waiting for her there. She decided she would call again in an hour. If he still didn’t answer, she would assume there was something wrong and drive out to check. So, with her mind solidly distracted by her concerns for Barney, she started her day.


*****  
    
        Two hours earlier, Barney was standing in front of the Lockwood. It was 5:00 am, and the sun would not be rising for another two hours. He had work to do in preparation for the Paranormal Posse visit. Knowing that Patty was depending on him, he felt silly not entering the building to start his day's chores of getting the halls completely emptied for the celebrity investigators.
        He shook his head and spoke clearly to himself.
        “Get your butt to work. If the ghosts want to talk, let them! You are fine. If the shadow man shows up, ignore him. After all, the people coming will want to see him. That’s enough talking to myself. I need to just think my thoughts to myself.”
        Barney walked to the room to the left of the main entrance, where he kept his snow shovel and large broom. He would move the debris with either or both of these implements. Some things he would have to haul to the dumpster at the end of the west hall. He needed to start in the east hall and move the trash down to “Grand Central”. He rolled a large cart and a wheelbarrow down about midway and then went to the end. Since he needed both hands to work, he had a light band on his forehead. That allowed him to see in the shadowy darkness until the sun came up. 
        It was going to be a busy day. He needed to get the three halls cleaned on the first floor. He would complete the same job on the second floor the next day, since he had been working on that when he allowed himself to go a little haywire, he wouldn’t need the whole day. That was good because he had the third floor of the main building yet to do, as well as some touch-up work in the tunnels. If he stayed at it for the three days, he should be totally ready for the visitors on the 14th.
        He started with the shovel, moving the chunks of ceiling tile and the plaster from the walls into piles that he could scoop into the cart. He slowly worked his way down the hall until he had made it to the midway point, where the cart was sitting. He returned to the end with the broom and did a fine sweep of the small material the shovel missed. He swept it into three large piles. He moved the wheelbarrow to each pile and loaded it. 
        He was on the third and final dust pile when it happened. He heard every door on the west wing begin to close one at a time from the far end, moving toward him. He focused the light on his head down the hall and watched as an invisible force slammed each door in succession. He was frozen in his place. As the sight before him unfolded. When the final door of the hall slammed. The building went silent. Every fiber of his being told him he should run, but that was not to be. 
        “Listen, I know you think this place is yours, and maybe it is most of the time, but you don’t own it. Patty Robertson owns it, and she wants it cleaned up. That is all I am doing, cleaning up the place. I don’t want anything to do with you, and you can leave me alone. You don’t have to prove to me that you exist. I believe you do. So, just go about your business, and I will go about mine.”
        Barney went back to working. He swept for another ten minutes, finishing half of the first floor of the east wing. His cart and wheelbarrow were full, and the dumpster was at the end of the west hall. That was where all the doors closed. He was about to roll the cart down there when he heard his name.
        “Barney?”
        “Oh no, not again. I hate it when they call me by name. I’m just going to ignore that. Don’t talk to me.”
        “Barney, are you in here?”
        That seemed odd; it was like whatever it was, was looking for him. That wasn’t how it had ever been before. Usually, it was just a whisper; this was pretty loud.
        “Barney Doyle, I’m here looking for you.”
        He was not sure what to do. Should he answer? Was it actually a person in the building? Curiosity got the better of him.
        “Hello? Is there someone here?”
        “There you are. I’ve been looking for you.”
        Barney looked up, and standing there was a man in his early thirties. He was dressed oddly. He wore an old-fashioned pair of overalls with a denim coat over top. He wore a slouchy flat cap. The kind James Cagney wore in the movie Public Enemy. 
        “I’m here to help clean the place.”
        “What?”
        “I’m here to help, champ. Are you going to turn an extra hand down?’
        “I don’t understand. Where did you come from? Did Patty hire you?”
        “My, you are difficult to help. How else would I know your name?”
        “Oh, okay, that sounds good. I guess. What’s your name?”
        “My name is Jasper, Jasper McCawley.”
        Barney was confused by the way the guy looked and the fact that he just showed up. But the guy did know his name, and he was offering to help.  
         “I’m surprised Patty didn’t tell me you were coming. But we do have a lot of work to get done before the special guests arrive. If you’re going to help, we'd better get to it.”
        “That’s the spirit, bub. Between the two of us, cleaning this place will be eggs and coffee. What would ya like me to do?”
        “What about eggs?”
        “What do you mean?”
        “You said something about eggs.”
        “I said cleaning this place will be eggs and coffee, you know it will be smooth sail’n.”
        “I’ve never heard that saying before.”
        “That’s a gasser, Joe. You look like you’d be hep.”
        Barney thought the guy sounded as strange as he looked. There was no point wasting time trying to decode the guy's words. He was ready to work, and work needed to be done.
        “Okay, well, we need to clear the rest of this hall and then start on the center wing. If you would clear the hall and push the debris into piles, I’ll fill the cart and wheelbarrow and dump them. Does that work for you?”
“You shred it, wheat, we’re on the high-gear road.”
“Okay. I assume that means it works for you.”
Barney shook his head as the two of them set about cleaning. It wasn't until they were finishing the first floor that he realized they had been working for nearly fifteen hours. They had talked the whole day, and Barney was actually getting where he could understand Jasper a little. 
He realized they had worked in the shadows of the Lockwood all through the daylight hours, and it was dark out once again. So much for staying out of the building in the dark. It was time to call it a day. He went looking for Jasper to tell him it was quitting time and thank him for all his hard work. 
“Jasper, it is time to call it a day. We worked right through two meals. You must be hungry. I don’t have much, but I’d be happy to feed you before you go. Jasper? Where are you?”
There was no answer. Barney thought that was strange. Did he just leave without saying he was going? The guy shows up unannounced, works all day, talking the whole time, half of it gibberish, and then takes off. I am confused. As Barney looked around at the work they had done, he was surprised to see so much debris still in the first-floor halls. He swore they had them totally cleared. Rather than leaving the building for the night, he walked up the stairs to the second floor to make sure he was satisfied with the work they had done there. He was shocked when all that appeared to be done was the half of the east wing he had completed before Jasper started helping. 
“This doesn’t make sense. Jasper and I worked all day. How did we not get more done? I would have completed more by myself. Did Jasper haul the trash back in while I was hauling it out? Where did he go? I never heard a car pull in when he arrived, and I didn’t see one outside all day. Someone would have had to drop him off and pick him up. I would have heard the car. I guess they could have waited down at the gate. Why didn’t he say something when he left? He was a talker; I listened to his chatter all day.”
Barney stood in the dark of the second floor at “Grand Central”, shaking his head. Something was wrong. Had he seen Jasper, talked with him? Was it all in his head? Reality was bending again, skipping from what was to what wasn’t in the blink of an eye. If Jasper did not exist, then he would have spent the day imagining things. He wasn’t sure what had happened and what had not. He needed to get out of the building. He started toward the stairs when he was stopped in his tracks by a woman’s blood-curdling scream. 
“Not this again! I am not going to let this get to me. I’m going to my apartment and making myself something to eat, and going to bed. You ghosts can just have a good old time without me.”
Barney headed for the stairs and did not stop. He walked through the grand entry to the front door. He heard footsteps behind him, but chose not to look back. As he opened the door, he heard a voice whisper.
“Don’t go, Barney, stay and play.”
A shiver went up the back of his neck as he hit the outside air. He took a deep breath and attempted to clear his thoughts. He had experienced another lost day. It would not be so bad if this all had not happened to him before. It wasn’t just the issue he had when Sally found him. He knew he had problems with time when he was still with his wife. He was still not sure what exactly happened during that time in his life. 
It also happened when he was a teenager. That was when the incident happened. Neither he nor his family ever spoke about it. It hung over them for years, an unspoken curse on all of them. His father never forgave him. But it wasn’t his fault; still, he had taken the blame. After that, he was never good enough for his father. He was criticized daily for not thinking, not taking responsibility. He could still hear the chiding for not being man enough. 
He stood in the cold darkness in front of the Lockwood. The waning crescent of the moon offered a dim light reflecting off the freshly fallen snow. The frosty surface twinkled as the frozen crystals played with the moonlight. The mystical quality of the glistening snow surrounding the massive building seemed to be highlighting the darkness of the structure. He could feel the energy of the place growing as the evening wore on. 
He felt compelled to reenter the building. It was as though the Lockwood was laying claim to him. It was beckoning him to become a permanent resident. There was a part of him that longed for the feeling of being part of something. He had been alone for most of his life. His marriage had turned out to be a false union. His family had abused and abandoned him emotionally. His work had never fulfilled him or offered collegial support. His life was filled with dark memories. Most of which left him feeling hollow and isolated.
That darkness had followed him since childhood. It took away much of the beauty within his world. It obscured goodness in the shadows of depression and feelings of personal failure. That shadowy presence was crawling out of his surroundings. It was soaking into his very being. It seemed to be filling him with aggression and anger, yet he embraced it.  He had the sensation that the darkness was growing within him. There was no shaking the feeling.

Chapter 5


Sally had been busy with one call after another all day. She had covered nearly the entire county. Her plan to stop and see Barney had been thwarted several times throughout the nearly fifteen-hour day. It was now nearly 8:00 pm. She was questioning if it was too late to drive out to the Lockwood. By the time she would get there, it would be 8:30. She wasn’t sure Barney was ready for evening guests, or that their relationship needed any complications yet regarding overnight visits. She did know that she was worried about him, and at least needed to try calling him again. She picked up her cell phone and pressed the speed dial for Barney.
The phone was buzzing in Barney’s pocket for nearly half a minute when he realized what was happening. 
“Hello?”
“Hi, I’m glad you answered. When you didn’t earlier, I started to worry, and then work took control of my schedule. I haven’t had a chance to call until now. I was thinking of driving out there, but I wasn’t sure what you would think of that.”
Sally had been so nervous about Barney that she wasn’t letting him get a word in as she poured out the story of her day.
“No need to drive all the way out here. I’m fine.”
Sally was a little taken aback by the minimal response from him. He had stated he cared for her, as she did him. Why was he being so non-communicative? She needed him to talk so she could feel good about where they stood. He had turned down her visit abruptly. That did not sit so well.
“You don’t want me to come out to see you?”
“Oh, it’s not that. I just think you probably need your rest after such a big day.”
Okay, so he was worried about her rest. Was she just second-guessing herself for telling him how she felt? Was he sounding cold, or was she reading too much into  … well, everything? She couldn’t tell. Her self-esteem was not sitting very high, and his perceived lack of warmth was not helping it. She was getting herself into an emotional turmoil just talking with him, or rather, trying to talk with him.
“All right, well, if you aren’t interested in a visit I will just let you get back to whatever you were doing. Call me when you are interested in getting together.”
“Oh, okay. Good night.”
“Goodbye!”
Sally got off the phone, no longer concerned about Barney; she was just plain angry with him. He had been cold and indifferent. After what she had told him, she had poured her heart out to him, told him how she really felt about him. She had made it clear she wanted to be with him; how dare he treat her with so little compassion? Hell was going to freeze over before she called him back. The ball was in his court. If he wanted to speak with her, he was going to have to make the first move. 
She fumed for about forty-five minutes, rewinding the affront that was that phone call. As her anger began to subside, she started thinking about what she had actually told Barney. She had said she cared for him, but she hadn’t stated anything beyond that. When she thought about it, pouring her heart out really didn’t define what she had done. In fact, it probably didn’t look or sound anything like that to Barney. She was overreacting because she wanted things to move faster than they probably should. 
“Sally girl, you can be an idiot at times. He could have been tired; he may have been distracted. There were any number of reasons he wasn’t warmer on the phone. It could be that he just is not a phone call person. Not everyone likes to talk on a phone at all, let alone about feelings. You just made a fool of yourself. Tomorrow I have to drive out there. We will do better face-to-face.”
Sally made herself a peanut butter sandwich. After her meager dinner, she went to bed. It was early, but she had to be on the road by 5:00 am the next day.


*****


        Barney was too distracted to realize the impact his phone call with Sally had on his friend. She would not have known he was standing on the steps of the Lockwood as he spoke with her. His mind was only partially on their conversation as he was filled with thoughts of what was transpiring within the building’s walls. Barney felt he was now almost a part of the community that hid in the shadows of the structure’s confines. That throng of residents that did not show themselves to the living. Barney had somehow crossed over. Though still a living being, his spirit had accepted the darkness.
He walked back into the building, leaving his flashlight behind. He had no need for that. For inside the building had been transformed. The marble was now clean and shone like new. The lights were all functioning. And the building was alive with the bustle of nurses, the patients positioned in rooms, waiting spaces, and convalescent areas all over the building. 
Barney walked through the halls unseen by the crowd of inhabitants, for while he was among them, he was not fully with them. The living and the dead may occupy the same space and see glimpses of each other or hear voices from the other side, but they are not on the same plane. Given the right electromagnetic or atmospheric conditions, shadows or silhouettes pierce the divide. The interaction is easier if the living is in tune with the darkness.
That darkness dwelt within Barney. He had done things in his life that bound him to death. Now, even as a living soul, he embodied the spirit of the dead and dying. It was the burden that gnawed at his mind. He could feel it more and more as the days wore on, surrounded by the energy of the Lockwood. He had fought it for so many months. It was now a feeling he was learning to embrace.
As he walked, he saw a darker figure whose features he could not fully discern. It seemed the others in the building stepped far aside as he strode the halls. He was different from the masses. His energy seemed to leave a trail of evil. Barney knew this figure. He had chased him many times. He was now looking upon it with full knowledge that they were connected in a way he did not quite understand. He contemplated that connection; he was distracted by another man dressed in overalls, wearing a slouching cap. It was Jasper! What was he doing back in the building? Barney called his name.
“Jasper, what are you doing here?”
There was no response.
“Jasper, you must be able to hear me. Why are you ignoring me? Wait, are you talking with them? How can you talk with them? You would have to be one of them, you would have to be dea …”
Barney’s voice trailed off. That would explain what had happened, why no work was completed after he saw Jasper during the day. Jasper was one of them, and he had crossed over in the shadows of the building where Barney could see him. He was the building’s messenger, beckoning Barney to join them. 
Hours melted away as Barney stood in wonder at the activity within the decaying walls of the building he had been responsible for over the last two years. As the sun’s rays began to filter through the windows and gaps in the walls, the images of all the souls he had witnessed faded. One by one, they walked into oblivion, disappearing as the decrepitude of the structure once again became apparent. At length, it was just him standing in the debris-filled space. No sleep, no food, alone. 
“Did I really see all of that? Where did the night go? Time is moving so quickly. It is happening without me realizing it. I was just leaving the building a few minutes ago. I don’t understand what is happening. I’ve lost time before. I’m afraid I do things when that happens, things I am not proud of. Did Sally call me last night, or did I imagine that? Why are my boots so wet? I thought I was in the building all night. How would they have gotten wet? I’ve got work to do. This place has to be ready for the Posse people. I promised Patty.”
Though exhausted, Barney went back to work with a vengeance, clearing hall after hall. His hands were frozen by late afternoon. He had neither slept nor eaten. He had nearly all of the interior work done for the Paranormal Posse. He still needed to run the large electrical cable for their RV. He also needed to clear the snow from the driveway. He had one more day to complete that work. He needed to rest. Leaving the building seemed to take every ounce of his strength. It was as though the structure itself had its grasp on him. He pushed his way out of the door. He could hear wailing from inside calling him back. It was compelling him to return and never leave. He knew what that meant. He would need to become one of them. The darkness in him wanted to make that possible. He felt a strange longing to end the misery of his living existence. His desire to fulfill his duties forced him to postpone what was becoming increasingly inevitable.
He walked to his apartment still confused as to what exactly had happened the night before. Seeing Jasper in the building, walking among the spectral collective, was odd. Why had he shown himself to Barney the day before? Was that a sign that Barney’s time among the living was coming to an end? What had he done all night? The lost time, the wet boots, were vacant clues that he had been elsewhere and done something, but what?


*****


Twelve Hours Earlier


Ben awoke from a drug-induced sleep. His head hurt, and he could not move. He was tied down to a large wooden table. His arms were pulled to the corners and tied to the table legs. His waist was tied to the opposite edge, and his feet were tied to the legs of the table at that end. He couldn’t move other than to squirm against the restraints. He looked around as well as he could. It was an old cabin. The rancid smell of the place was mixed with the smoke from what must have been a fireplace. 
He could hear movement near the top of his head, but he could not see the person. His eyes were attempting to adjust to the dimly lit room. Ben’s body ached from what he assumed were hours of being tied down in such a manner. It was unclear how long he had been out. He tried to recall what had happened.
He remembered he was doing a task for Dorian. He was trying to get on the roof of the Lockwood. That was it. He was lifting the metal ladder when someone stabbed him with a needle. There must have been something in that syringe that knocked him out. He thought about who had snuck up on him. As his head throbbed, his mind was still feeling the effects of the sedative. The cobwebs were masking the memory of his abductor. They had to be in the room. Then he heard someone talking.
“Yes, I have to do this. I’m not getting the rush I once did from the critters. This is going to be an achievement I can relish. I will transform this person into more than they are now. I will provide them with sight from within. They will no longer be tied to the false physical world. The existence we lead is all just an illusion. People create it to give themselves a sense of place. There is no such thing; it is a hoax we perpetrate on ourselves. 
“You, father, are the biggest supporter of this hoax. You hate me for what happened to my brother. You believe he is gone, when in fact he was set free. He no longer has to deal with your foolish expectations, your chiding, and complaining that he and I needed to be more.”
The person speaking walked to the side of the table. No face was visible. A large hooded robe covered his features. The figure’s monk-like appearance was familiar to Ben. He strained against the ropes.
“Who are you? What do you think you are doing? I demand you release me. I was only looking for a way to get on the roof. My friends want to perform a silly ritual. It would have caused no harm. I would have left if you just told me to; I would have gladly gone away. Why am I tied up like this?”
“Silly ritual, was it? The energy of the Lockwood is not silly. It is real, and its power shall become clear to you. Once your vision is changed. Once you see with your inner eye, you will know its true power.” 
“What are you talking about? What inner eye? I just want to leave and go home. Just let me go. I won’t tell anyone about this.”
“Oh, you won’t be telling anyone anything after we are through here tonight.”
“What do you mean?”
Tears were streaming down Ben’s face. He feared for his life. He believed whoever the hooded figure was had to be out of his mind. There wasn’t any way to reason with the guy.
“Come on, man. I haven’t done anything to you.”
“You should thank me. You will be the first human I have transformed. I have practiced several times for this very moment. You will be perfect when I finish my work.”
Ben was screaming as the figure moved away. When he returned with a knife that was glowing bright red from sitting in the flames of the fireplace, his screaming grew even louder.
“Stop! I’ve done nothing to you. Why are you doing this? The knife is so hot. I can feel it from here. What are you doing? Don’t DON’T AGGGHHHH!”
The knife came down into Ben’s left eye. His scream was gurgled and high-pitched. He writhed on the table, his back arching off its wooden surface. Then he went limp as he passed out from the searing pain. 
Not wanting to miss the boy’s anguish as the second eye was burned out of his skull, the hooded figure waited for him to come back around. He stirred, crying from the agony the burning had caused. He was now begging with all of his soul for the torture to stop. 
His tormentor walked away, only to return with a second knife, glowing from the flames. Ben could tell where this was going. The tear-filled, blurry sight of his right eye registered its last view of the world. It was of the hooded figure bringing the knife toward his eye. The feature he could see was a grinning mouth, and then pain and darkness. His screams trailed off as he once again passed out from the shock. His ordeal was far from over. The man in the hood pushed Ben’s jaw open. He reached inside his mouth with a metal tongs and then, using the knife, now somewhat cooled from its plunge in the boy’s right eye, he sliced through the muscly thickness of his tongue. With this act, he rendered the boy mute.
Sweating profusely, in shock, and overcome with pain, Ben lay limp on the table. He moaned and whimpered as his torturer stood surveying his work.
“You are now a new being. You may walk among the spectral throngs of the Lockwood as one of them. Your eyes were of no use, your tongue meaningless. You may commune with the spirits on a higher level. I have given you that gift. This is my legacy, my offering to the darkness. You have become one with the darkness. When the time comes, I shall lead you to your home, where light is of no consequence, and your inner sight shall be your guide.”
Ben’s mind was on fire; he had been tortured beyond endurance, and some maniac was talking gibberish about his home at the Lockwood. He hated this guy; he hated Dorian for sending him into this, and he hated life. In his mind, he was begging to just die and end the agony.
The hooded figure removed the cloak and walked out into the wet snow.


*****


Five O’clock in the morning came along with Sally’s alarm. She had not slept well. Her concerns for Barney kept her awake, and then she had strange dreams. Most nights, she would say she doesn’t dream at all. That isn’t exactly true, as everyone dreams. So in reality, she often just didn’t remember them. That was not the case for her during the recent night. She had dreams that were vivid, and she remembered every moment of them.
Barney was with her, and they were enjoying each other’s company when a whirlwind of ghost-like figures swept him up and carried him away. In her dream, she ran after them, yelling and shouting at them to let him go. The ghosts did not listen; they flew until they were at a great castle. As she thought about it, the castle was a giant building that had turrets like a castle, but it wasn’t a castle; it was the Lockwood. That is how it goes in dreams, elements of what you see can be two different things at one time. Barney was hauled into the castle/Lockwood, and the door was barred shut. Sally tried to enter, but while she was standing at the door, she was transported across a moat. There she was held at bay by a large barking dog. 
She was surrounded by people, many of whom she felt she knew, but in the dream did not recognize. They were all talking about her and Barney. They were saying mean things about the two of them; many were lies about how they were having an affair, and she wasn’t doing her job. Some were saying Barney was as crazy as Dober. She couldn’t get to Barney, and the people were not friendly; she was all alone, and in her dream, she felt desperate to just hold Barney’s hand. Then the Lockwood/castle just vanished. She was left with the people around her now laughing. It seemed the source of their glee was her standing crying because she had lost the man she loved. That was when the alarm brought her out of the nightmare.
Sally played with the spoon in her cereal, not actually consuming any of it. She was trying to figure out what to do about Barney. The phone call had been so disturbing, and she felt she should just leave some space between them. That was the logical thing to do, but she didn’t want to be logical. Relationships aren’t logical. They can be messy in good ways and also not so good. The frustration overflowed as she began talking to herself.
“My feelings for Barney are messy. I had been reticent to tell him how I felt when we were younger, and then I missed my opportunity in college. Now, when I could have been quite clear about how I felt, I relied on him picking up on my signals when I said I CARED for him.
“Barney had said he cared for me, but did he mean in the way I meant? Damn my introversion. That has always been my problem. As a cop, I can play a role. Behind my badge, I can act the part of someone able to speak their mind without any qualms. In my personal life, I know I tend to act like the church mouse, keeping my thoughts and feelings hidden from public view or earshot.”
Sally stood from the table and dumped out the bowl of raisins and flakes. There would be no breakfast, just as there had been very little sleep. Her day was going to start much as it ended, with her totally confused as to how to get herself and Barney on the same page. 
She drove to the Sheriff’s office and switched to her cruiser. The vehicle was cold and uncomfortable. She had to head over to the hair parlor first thing. There were still people clamoring for action regarding Dober. That gossip needed to be nipped before someone ended up out at Dober’s place. Knowing that old coot, he was likely to take a shot at them. Then they would have a real problem.
It took about twenty minutes to get to Dorothy’s house. It was still pretty early in the morning, but Sally had a lot to do. Dorothy was just going to have to deal with her being an early visitor. She knocked on the door. When there was no answer, she knocked again. In about a minute, she could hear someone yelling from inside that they were coming.
“Sally? What are you doing here so early?”
Standing in front of the deputy was a large woman in her mid-fifties. Her hair was nearly standing straight up off her head. She wore a bathrobe that was a size too small and a long nightgown that must have been worn for five to ten years, as the fabric was worn thin.
“Sorry to get you up so early, but I have a lot on my plate today, and your house was the first location on my route.”
“What do you want?”
“I need your help. It seems that there are rumors about Dober Grumby abusing animals and molesting a child. There is no evidence of Dober doing anything. It appears this is a rumor gone wild.”
“What does all this have to do with me?”
“Well, it could be that the rumor started between some of your customers. I was hoping you could set the record straight with your clientele. We’re concerned if we don’t get this rumor stopped, someone will attempt to confront Dober on his property. He is a private guy and doesn’t like visitors.”
“I’m feeling like you are blaming me for the rumor.”
“Oh no, that is not what I intended to do. It is just that you are an important citizen in the community and you have the ability to reach a lot of people on every rung of the social ladder.”
“I will do what I can, though I can’t help but feel offended that you are suggesting that these rumors could have started here.”
“I’m so sorry to have offended you. That was not my objective. I am merely trying to avoid hurting someone who does not deserve this much attention. I appreciate you attempting to help stop the rumors. I’ll be going. Again, I’m sorry if I offended you.”
“I’ll do what I can, officer.”
“That’s all I can ask. Thank you.”
Dorothy was visibly perturbed by the whole conversation. Sally was not sure if she had helped the situation or created more of a problem. She certainly did not seem sympathetic to Dober’s position. She was much more concerned with being called out for allowing or promoting rumors through her business.                     
Sally could not spend much time thinking about whether she had hurt the feelings of the hairdresser. The fact was that the rumors had started at her establishment, whether she wanted to admit it or not.
Sally drove away, putting the whole conversation out of her mind. She had other things to worry about. At least while she confronted Dorothy, she was not thinking about Barney. Having now thought about him, she was back to worrying about what he was doing. She could fit a quick trip to the Lockwood into her schedule. She sat in her car looking at what she had on the docket. Her time was booked through the entire day. The only way she was going to have time to check in with Barney was if she was able to get ahead on her schedule. She was determined to make that happen, but her hopes took a devastating blow when she received a call from dispatch.
“Dispatch to Deputy Thomas.”
“Go for Thomas.”
“Sally, we just received a call from Max Osborn. He has a daughter named Crystal and a son named Ben. It seems they are both missing. He would like a deputy to stop by his office.”
“Got it, what office are we talking about?”
“The Brendon Real Estate Offices. They are just outside of Eau Claire.”
Sally looked at the time; it was nearly 9:00 am. It would take her about thirty-five minutes to get to the office.
“I can head there now. I should be there around 9:40. Has he said anything about where he thinks the kids are or if he thinks they are together?”
“He thinks it has something to do with one of their friends, Dorian Brendon.”
“Brendon? The same name as the Real Estate Office?”
“Yes, he’s the son of the woman who owns the place.” 
“Is this guy calling us because he is concerned his boss’s son could be involved with whatever is going on with his kids?”
“You’ll have to get the whole story from Osborn. I just know that we have to act right away whenever there is a possibility of a missing child. Both of these kids are under eighteen.”
“I understand, I’m on the way. Clear call.”
“Out.”
Sally drove into the city of Eau Claire. The Realtor’s office was on Prill Road, just off Highway 53, outside of the city limits. She parked in the lot and walked in, where she was immediately greeted by the receptionist.
“Welcome to Brendon Realty. Did you have an appointment with one of our agents?”
“I am Deputy Thomas with the Sheriff’s Department. I’m here to speak with Mr. Max Osborn.”
“Oh, you're here for Max. Just a moment, I will see if he is in.”
The receptionist stood up and walked around the divider she sat in front of and down a hall.
She tapped on an office door and then entered and shut the door.
“Max, there is a sheriff deputy out there to see you. Is everything okay?”
Max stood up and walked toward the young lady. He grabbed her around the waist and pulled her to him. He raised his hand to her face and brushed a few strands of hair from her forehead. He leaned in and kissed her. She reciprocated and then pulled back.
“Did you hear what I said? There is a deputy here for you. What is going on?”
“Dani, calm down. I asked them here. My kids are off doing something stupid. Neither of them is answering their phones or texts. I need them tracked down before it costs me more than it probably already will. Now, how about one more before you go get the guy waiting out there?”
“Oh, Max, you’re naughty.”
“You inspire me.”
“I don’t seem to inspire you to leave your wife.”
“Dani, you know the problems that will create. Be patient, I promise this isn’t a forever problem.”
“Okay, okay. Anyway, it is a female deputy waiting, not a guy.”
“Oh, is she cute?”
“Stop it, behave yourself!”
Dani left the office and made her way back to the lobby.
“Deputy, I will take you back to Mr. Osborn’s office now.”
Sally followed the receptionist through the hall. As she walked, she noticed the artworks displayed were all original works. The building and decor all presented an image of wealth. She made note of that since the kids were missing, the possibility of kidnapping was on the table.
“Mr. Osborn, this is Deputy Thomas.”
“Thank you, Dani. Deputy Thomas, thank you for responding to my call. I have a daughter, Crystal, who will be eighteen in a month, and a son, Ben, who just turned seventeen. I have not heard from either of them for the past two days, possibly three.”
“You’re not sure if it has been two or three days?”
“Well, I did get a rather cryptic text from Crystal saying she quarreled with her boyfriend, Dorian Brendon, and she was leaving town. That was two days ago. I hadn’t taken her seriously. You know how teenagers are. They like to be dramatic and overstate their emotions and such. But neither of them has slept in their beds the past three nights.”
“So, when was the last time you or your wife actually saw either of your children?”
“Let’s see, I believe I saw Ben last Monday, and Crystal was on the weekend, I think.”
Sally looked at the man and wondered why he had even called. It was more than apparent that he had little concern for the whereabouts of his children.
“Given the apparent looseness of your contacts with the kids, what makes you think they are actually missing?”
“I’m not sure I like the judgmental tone of your question.”
“Sir, I am not judging anyone. I am simply questioning how you determined their absence was different this time from what appears to be a normal routine of separation.”
“Well, to be quite honest, I usually see them more often when they need money. This seemed to be longer than usual.”
“Do you have any ideas as to where they may be right now?”
“If I knew that, I wouldn’t have called you.”
“What I am asking is, where are some places they could hang out where they could sleep and eat and essentially survive away from your home?”
“They spend a lot of time at the Brendon estate. I know Buck and Margo Brendon are out of the country right now. Dorian, their son, is out at their mansion alone. They could be there, but there would be no reason they wouldn’t answer their phones.”
“That brings us back to the supposed quarrel between Crystal and Dorian. Is it possible that Crystal did indeed leave town? Would she have the resources to do that? Does she have access to a credit card or bank account? Does she have her own vehicle?”
“She has her own car, and she does have a credit card, which she is allowed to use in emergencies. Her bank account is in her name and mine. I would be aware of any withdrawals. I don’t know if she had any cash saved up. She gets a couple of hundred a week allowance.”
“What about your son?”
“He has a car and access to a credit card as well. I have a bank account with him, the same way as my daughter.” 
“I assume he receives that same allowance?”
“Yeah, that is correct.”
Sally shook her head at the thought of how much money was at the disposal of the Osborn kids. 
“I will need to know if there is any activity on their credit cards. If your daughter is aware of how easily the credit card could be tracked, she could stick with cash. That is, if she is on the move on her own volition, and does not want to be found. Do the two children get along? Is it possible your son would leave with your daughter if she did decide to run away?”
“I can’t believe she really would just leave town on her own. She will graduate in just a few months. Why would she throw that away? As far as Ben going with her, well, that could happen, I suppose. They are pretty close. Ben does not like school; his grades show that quite regularly. Wherever they are, I assume they could be together.”
“You mentioned the Brendon boy. Are there any other close friends?”
“The two of them hang out with Dorian, and I think the banker’s daughter.”
“What is her name?”
“The banker’s last name is Murrey. Her name is Bonnie or something like that. There is another boy also. I think he may be the Murrey girl’s boyfriend. That is all I am aware of, as far as kids they could be with.”
“Do you have any monitoring set up on your daughter’s Internet devices?”
“What do you mean?”
“Do you have any idea if she has been communicating with a stranger on the Internet? There are many people out there who attempt to lure young people to meet with them and then engage in forms of abuse.”
“Oh, I think Crystal is too smart to fall for something like that.”
“So, I assume your answer is that we can’t rule that out, because you don’t monitor her access?”
“No, I don’t know for certain. I still believe she would not leave town on her own.”
“The alternative is not good. Have you noticed anyone around your kids that you would consider suspicious? Do you have enemies that would want to harm you through your children?”
“No, I’m not aware of anyone in either case.”
Osborn was shifting more often in his chair. He was getting progressively more uncomfortable about how little he really knew about his kids. He was also getting annoyed at the deputy for essentially pointing out how absent he was in his children’s lives.
“Would your wife be aware of anyone?”
“I don’t think so, we are not home very often. My wife and I have not spoken for a couple of days. We lead pretty busy lives.”
“I will probably need to speak with her at some point.”
“Oh, I suppose that would be fine.”
There was something about his answer that made Sally wonder if the Osborns had spoken about the missing children at all.
“She is aware the kids are missing, right?”
“Well, we haven’t spoken about it. I was hoping we would track them down before she needed to get involved.”
“I’m sorry, are the two of you separated, or nearing divorce?”
“Is that really any of your business?”
“Actually, yes, it is. It could have a bearing on this whole situation. Marital problems can create all kinds of issues within families. So, while I am investigating what is going on with your children, there are no off-limits topics. Now, please answer the question.”
Osborn was perturbed by the situation and the deputy’s insistence on having all topics open for the investigation.
“Okay, so my wife and I are not separated, but we are not in a good place regarding communication. She is living at the main house of our property, and I am living in the guest house right now. I am not happy that you have placed me in a position of having to answer this type of question. Now, can you please go and do your job? Find my kids.”
“I will do my best, sir. I will be starting with the Brendon boy. As soon as you have the opportunity, please check the credit cards for activity, and let me know what you learn. I will be giving you a daily progress report until we have some answers.”
“Alright, that sounds reasonable. If you discover one or both. I need to be informed immediately.”
“Of course. I had best be on my way.”
Sally left the office and returned to her vehicle. She could not help but think how sad the Osborn family’s situation seemed. They didn’t really sound like a family at all. They were a group of seemingly disinterested housemates. In her opinion, money certainly didn’t add to their relationship. It wasn’t her job to judge their family dynamics. Her task was to find the kids.
Gnawing at her mind was the thought that whoever had mutilated the raccoons was still out there. What if they had decided to move up the food chain? She was headed to the Brendon home. It seemed as though the boyfriend with whom Crystal supposedly quarreled would be the best place to start.


***** 


It was nearly noon when Barney stirred from his sleep. His mind was muddled. Had he really been in the building the night before, or had he dreamt the whole thing? He was hungry. It had been a while since he sat down for a meal. He walked to his refrigerator and saw the leftover meatloaf Sally had prepared for him. He began to think of her. 
“When was the last time we spoke? I think it was a phone call. I don’t remember how it went, but I think she said if I wanted to talk, I was supposed to call her. Now that I think about it, I may have messed up in how I spoke to her. Should I call and just say, Hi? Should I apologize? I’m not sure what I would be apologizing for. Maybe I should just give her space. I’m not sure about a relationship anyway. I can’t leave the Lockwood, not now, or … maybe ever. 
“I feel I have become a part of this place. I believe I will be fully enveloped by it soon. As he thought of the mass of spirits inside, the spectre of the shadow man crept into his psyche. Who is that shadow? It has different energy and seems to be able to interact with me, unlike the others. I don’t really fear any part of the place except him.”
Barney stopped talking to himself as his thoughts turned to his place among the dead. The darkness that filled his soul was at home there. 
He realized he was still standing, looking into the refrigerator. His mind was disjointed. 
“What was I doing? Eating something, that’s right.”
 He grabbed the cold meatloaf and made a sandwich. As he sat at the table, he had to plan his day. The building was ready for the special visitors. They would be arriving the next day. That would actually be during a new moon. The complete darkness of the place would be excellent for them to interact with the residents of the Lockwood. 
All he needed to get done was the power line for their RV and plow the driveway. He finished his meal and then put on his coat, hat, and gloves. He exited the apartment to work outdoors. As he walked out, he was surprised by Dober. 
“I didn’t expect to see you today. I don’t have any work inside. All I am doing is setting up a power cord for an RV that will be parked here tomorrow and plowing the drive.”
Dober just shrugged and headed off to climb into the old truck with the plow mounted. Barney watched him walk away, thinking it was strange that he didn’t say anything. He went to the storage shed and grabbed two extension cords. He figured they would reach from the receptacle outside his door to the spot on the driveway where he anticipated the RV would sit. He strung them out, and it was a good fit. He flipped them out of the way and headed for the truck.
“It should take about an hour to do a decent job of clearing all of the snow from the drive and getting the gate area cleaned out.”
Dober just looked out the truck windshield.
“What have you been up to?”
His friend turned his head and, with a very sober expression, spoke.
“I think you are well aware of what I have been doing.”
“What is that supposed to mean?”
“It means you know very well what I have been doing; you put me in that position.”
“Oh, you are probably talking about the Sheriff’s Deputy that stopped to talk with you. How did that go?”
Dober didn’t talk; he just waved for Barney to stop. He then exited the truck.
“Hey, wait. I’m sorry that she bothered you. I told her not to do it, but she wouldn’t listen. You know, the people around here don’t understand you. They think you are capable of doing things, just because you act like the town fool. If they knew you as I do, they would be more likely to leave you alone.”
Barney kept talking as he got out of the truck and walked around to try to talk with his friend. When he got around the truck, Dober wasn’t there. He looked for where he had gone so fast and couldn’t see him anywhere. 
“Where are you? How did you get out of sight so fast? Come on, Dober, you know I don’t want something like this to destroy our friendship.”
There was no response. Barney searched the landscape for a sign of where Dober had gone. That was when he realized there was no car in the drive or down by the gate. Dober wouldn’t have walked there. He looked at the snow around the truck. There were footprints, but he wasn’t sure they weren’t all his. Where did Dober walk?
“Come on, Dober. Come out of wherever you went. We need to talk about this. I’m sorry, okay? I swear I asked her not to try to question you. Please come out where I can see you; you’re freaking me out.”
There was still no response.
“Did I just imagine he was here? I must have more guilt bottled up in me for mentioning Dober’s name to Sally. Between the guilty feelings and so little sleep, I’m imagining things. That has to be what’s going on here. Maybe after I get the work done here, I could drive over to Dober’s cabin and apologize. Yeah, that is what I should do.”
Barney got back into the truck and started plowing. The driveway was over a quarter of a mile long, and the snow had drifted, creating piles nearly five feet tall. It took forty-five minutes to get the driveway cleared to the gate. The gate area was going to take some time because the snow was piled up from a portion of the drive, as well as from the road in front of the place. It was piled higher than the gate itself. It took another forty minutes to clear it all out and haul it across the road.
Barney looked at the road with a small sense of pride. The place looked ready for the guests who were arriving the next day. The pride was soon replaced with an overwhelming feeling of apprehension. These people were going to be in the building, his building, his Lockwood. They were going to be attempting to interact with the energy of the place. There was a part of him that thought he should be protecting the entities that dwelt behind those walls. They had shown themselves to him; they were beckoning him to join them. They trusted him. Was he betraying that trust by allowing these people to come in and possibly exploit the residents? Had they not been through enough in life? Must their death be placed on exhibit? It was a question for which he had no answer. He had mixed loyalties with which to contend. He worked for Patty, but he was becoming part of THEM. He determined it was best to just keep working. 
He pulled the truck up to the shed in which it was stored. Barney grabbed the keys and locked the doors. Even this close to his apartment, some people would still attempt to steal the truck or just mess with it. He had to steer clear of the building. It was drawing him in much like it had the night before. That was not what he needed or could handle. There was already enough confusion and mixed emotions within him. He had to hold it together and last through the next couple of days while the celebrity guests were on the property. Barney decided that even though it was early in the day, he would just go back to the apartment and watch TV until he fell asleep. 
He wasn’t certain what time the group would be arriving the next day, so he needed all the rest he could get. He also needed something to take his mind off the things he was seeing or thought he saw. It was obvious he was losing time more often, and he seemed to be regularly crossing through the divide between life and death. With each exposure to the other side, the darkness within him grew. It was filling him, making him feel so very heavy. That burden was adding to his level of exhaustion and pulling him toward the Lockwood. Going into that building would start the cycle all over. He did have a lot of questions that may only be answered within the Lockwood, but they would have to wait.


*****


  Sally started her search at the high school. It was a school day, so Dorian, the Banker’s daughter, and her boyfriend should be in school. Memorial High School was located about seven miles from Osborn’s office, in the center of Eau Claire. 
She had just driven into the parking lot when she received a call.
“Dispatch to Thomas.”
“Go for Thomas.”
“Sally, I have Doc Blanchard on the phone for you. I will patch him through.”
“Okay.”
“Ahh … hello? Sally?”
“Hi, Doc. What can I do for you?”
“I wanted to let you know that as I was checking my van today, I realized two of my surgical knives are missing. Whoever took the Azaperone must have taken the knives as well.”
“Could those be used to remove an eye?”
“Why would you ask that?”
“I have another investigation that involves removing eyes from raccoons.”
“Oh, does that have anything to do with the missing kids?”
“I don’t know. I’m just concerned the raccoon mutilator may be our thief. Is there anything else about the theft I should know?”
“I don’t think so, but something was going on out at the Brendon estate early this morning.”
“What do you mean by something?”
“There were lights around their lower driveway that went off as I drove by.”
“What time was that?”
“It was around 3:30 am.”
“Why were you driving by at that time in the morning?”
“Oh, I ahh … was coming back from a call.”
“So you saw someone around the lower driveway of the estate, and when they saw you, they turned off their lights. Is that right?”
“That is what happened.”
“Okay, well, I will be checking out there later.”
“Okay, I just wanted to let you know.”
“Thanks, Doc. I’ll get in touch if I need anything else.”
“Okay, thanks, Sally.”
The deputy took a moment and wrote a few notes about what the Doctor had to say. When she was done, she got back on track and headed for the entrance to the school. 
The entry doors were set up for security, and the deputy had to be buzzed in. Sally went to the main office to request to see the three students. As she entered the office, she noticed the nameplate of the person at the front desk. It read Angie.
“Hello, Angie, I am Deputy Sally Thomas with the County Sheriff’s Department. I am investigating the whereabouts of Crystal and Ben Osborn. They did not happen to attend school today, did they?”
“Oh, give me a second to check on that, Deputy.”
Angie looked at her computer screen and tapped a few keys, presumably to check the attendance log.
“It looks like they were both marked absent today.”
“I guess that is not a surprise; they are the missing kids I am looking for.”
“It looks like they missed yesterday as well. That did not raise any red flags, as they have a record of missing the maximum number of days.”
“I’m looking for three other students who are friends of the Osborn kids. Their names are Dorian Brendon, a girl whose last name is Murrey, and a young man, I believe, is her boyfriend.”
“Oh, Dorian, I’m not surprised he is someone the police want to speak with.”
“Why would you say that?”
“Dorian has been a challenge all through his high school career. If he worked in his classes half as hard as he works at causing trouble, he would be at the top of his class.”
        “Is he in attendance today?”
        “No, he is not. He has been out of school all week.”
        “Can you help with the names of the other two students?”
        “I believe you are looking for Bobbie Murrey.”
        “Bobbie, that must be it. Do you have any idea of who the boy could be?”
        “I think you are looking for Dan Zinkie. He has been involved with that group over the years. He at least would know who you are looking for if it’s not him.”
        “Okay then, you know my next question.”
        “You need to know if they are here today.”
        “That is correct.”
        “Let’s take a look. It appears Bobbie is, and let’s see, Zinkie? Yes, Dan has been marked present as well.”
        “Could the two of them be called to the office? Is there a place I could speak with them?”
        “Yes, I will call them down here. We have a small conference room just off the copy machine room, down the hall to the left. Would you like any coffee or some water?”
        “Some water would be great. I have been going all day without a chance for a break. When it’s cold out like today, it gets pretty dry. I’m starting to feel it.”
        “I’ll bring you some, go ahead to the room.”
        “Thanks.”
        “Do you want the two students at the same time or one at a time?”
        “Go ahead and send them both in, thanks.”
        “Will do, it could take a couple of minutes for them to get here. The school is spread out.”
        “Thank you.”
        Sally walked to the conference room, and Angie followed shortly after with the water. As the deputy sat there, she pulled out a small notebook. She jotted down a few questions for the kids she would be meeting. It took about ten minutes for them to arrive. Angie had walked them back to the room. The pair looked very apprehensive as they entered.
“Please sit down. I have a few questions for you.”
Dan looked at Bobbie and then back at the deputy. Since he had his share of experience with the law, he was aware of his rights.
“Are we in trouble? Should we have our parents here?”
“No, I don’t think you’re in trouble. If at any time you don’t want to answer my questions, you can request an adult present. That is your right. My main purpose here is to get any information I can regarding two missing kids that I believe you know very well. I’m looking for Crystal and Ben Osborn. Can you tell me anything about their whereabouts?”
Dan was still suspicious of the deputy’s motives.
“You don’t think we had anything to do with them going missing, do you?”
“No, I didn’t, should I?”
Bobbie was quick to answer.
“NO! I haven’t seen Crystal for three days. Not since we saw her out at Dorian’s house.”
“When you saw her out there, did it appear that she and Dorian were getting along?”
Bobbie responded.
“Yeah, sure, they didn’t seem to be having any problems, but …”
“Please finish what you were saying. Your friend's safety could be at stake.”
“Well, I don’t think Crystal liked how far Dorian was taking his latest obsession.”
“What do you mean by obsession?”
Bobbie looked at Dan. He knew she was concerned about sharing the whole ritual thing that Dorian wanted to do. Dan opted to be the one to explain.
“Dorian is into the occult. He believes he can call forth a demon that will wreak havoc upon his enemies. I know it sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, but he truly believes in it. I think Crystal was afraid he could do it. She was not comfortable with Dorian deciding who the enemies were. Dorian is an angry guy right now. Bobbie and I were supposed to be part of his ritual along with Crystal and Ben. We decided it was too weird, so we were planning to back out even if it meant we were no longer friends with Dorian.”
“In your opinion, could Dorian harm Crystal or Ben?”
“We met Dorian two days ago to practice for the ritual. We met in a warehouse that his dad owns. He bought these stupid robes we were supposed to wear for the real day. We put them on for the practice. I felt like an idiot.”
“Were Crystal and Ben at the practice?”
“Ben showed up, but Dorian pulled him to the side and whispered with him for a while, and then Ben left. He didn’t look like he wanted to do whatever it was Dorian sent him off to do.”
“How do you know he didn’t want to do it?”
“It was the look he gave Dorian as he left.”
        “So, you don’t know where he went?”
        “I don’t know, but I think it could have something to do with where we … or, actually, Dorian had planned to do the ritual.”
“Where is the ritual supposed to happen?”
Bobbie looked at Dan. He shook his head yes, and she turned to Officer Thomas and spoke.
“Dorian said that the Lockwood had the energy we needed for the ritual to work.”
“The Lockwood? Where were you planning to do it out there?”
Dan took over answering again.
“Dorian said on the roof. He said the energy from the place would concentrate in the center of the roof. He might have sent Ben out there to do some looking around, maybe to see how we were getting on the roof. I’m just guessing. The look on Ben’s face when he left seemed to say he hated Dorian for making him do something he was dreading.”
“When was this ritual supposed to take place?”
“Soon, I think. Dorian never said specifically when. The practice was supposed to get us ready, so I assumed it was sometime this week. It had something to do with the moon. I didn’t listen that close to that B.S. Did you understand that stuff, Bobbie?”
“No, it had to do with phases or something like that. He was really into it, but I don’t know when he was planning to do his little play acting as a priest of Balsadore.”
“Neither of you answered my question regarding whether Dorian could harm either Crystal or Ben.”
Once again, the couple looked at one another. Bobbie was starting to cry. Dan held her hand and spoke for the two of them.
“Maybe, up to a couple of weeks ago, I would have said he would never hurt either of them. Lately, I’m not so sure. He has really gone off the deep end with the idea of conjuring a demon. You see, he thinks he will be the demon's hand on earth.”
“His hand? What does that mean?”
“He thinks he will have the demon’s powers and can use them against anyone who stands in his way. I think he is acting crazy. I don’t know if he is sleeping or not. His parents are out of town and will be for another week. He has some issues anyway. There was something that happened to his brother.”
“Dan, I don’t think we should say anything about that. That is their family stuff. We shouldn’t judge it or tell the story. We don’t even know the whole story.”
“Why don’t you tell me what you do know. Anything you say would be considered hearsay evidence and inadmissible in court. But it could help me find your friends. You want that, don’t you?”
”Of course we do, but this is ancient history. It has to do with Dorian’s younger brother.”
“There is another Brendon boy? Does he go to school here?”
“No, he died when he was younger. He and Dorian were playing with fireworks, and something went horribly wrong.”
“Were they injured?”
“Dorian wasn’t, but his brother was badly hurt. I think he was burned somehow. I never heard the full story. The family doesn’t talk about it. It seemed like they blamed Dorian. He doesn’t get along with his parents, particularly his dad. His mom kind of ignores him.”
“Can either of you think of a reason that Crystal would leave town on her own, and if she did, would Ben go with her?”
Bobbie was shaking her head no as she looked at Dan. He shrugged. Bobbie spoke for them.
“I don’t think Crystal would leave without telling me. She graduates in a couple of months. If she left now, she would be throwing college out the window, and I think she has too many plans to do that. As far as Ben is concerned, he could be with her. They are very close. Their parents are … well, they aren’t very involved. I don’t want to say too much about them; they are busy and not around much.”
“Okay, you told me about the last time you saw Crystal. When was the last contact of any kind, either of you have had with Crystal or Ben?
“The day we saw her at Dorian’s, I received a text saying she had a ‘quarrel’ with Dorian and she was leaving town. She wouldn’t use the word quarrel and she would have told me where she was going.”
“What are you saying? Do you think someone else wrote the text?”
“I don’t know, I just know it doesn’t sound like her.”
“Have either of you seen any social media postings from Crystal or Ben in the last two days?”
 “Wow, I hadn’t thought about it. No, I haven’t seen anything, and that is really not like Crystal. She posted all of the time. Ben wasn’t that active, but Crystal probably did twenty to thirty posts a day, and there had been zero from her.”
Bobbie was physically shaken by the revelation that her friend had been silent the entire time she had been missing. If she wasn’t hurt or somehow restrained, she would have been online. Tears were welling in her eyes as she began to think Crystal was in serious trouble.
“Can you think of where the two Osborn kids might be?”
Neither Bobbie nor Dan seemed to have an answer to that. Bobbie spoke up.
“Deputy, do you think something has happened to them? Are they hurt or …”
Bobbie was in full breakdown, crying at the thought of her friends being injured or possibly worse.
“Please, don’t jump to conclusions that they are harmed in any way. At this point, all we know is that they have been missing for two days. We have no evidence beyond that. I think you two should head back to class. Thank you for all of your help.”
“How will we know if they are okay?”
“I’m sure you two will be the first people they contact when they are back.” 
“What if they … If they don’t come back?”
“Let’s not worry about something that hasn’t happened, okay?”
“Okay.”
The two kids left the conference room. Sally sat thinking about what they had said. In the back of her mind, she knew that missing persons who are gone for more than forty-eight hours are less likely to be found alive. That outcome increases as the hours and days wear on. The thought sent a shiver down her spine. She gathered her notes and walked out to the front of the office.
“Angie, thank you for your assistance.”
“I hope the kids were helpful. I know they can be a handful.”
“They were invaluable, I think they are concerned for their friends.”
“That is good to hear. We see these kids acting too cool to care a lot of the time. It’s good to know down deep they still have some compassion for their fellow man.”
“Thank you for all your help.”
“Are you any closer to finding the Osborn kids?”
“Let’s just say I have a few more places to look.”
“Good luck, we don’t want anything bad to happen to any child.”
Sally nodded and left the school. She needed to find Dorian Brendon. In her vehicle, she did a quick address look-up. It appeared the Brendon estate was close to the Lockwood. She could maybe kill two birds with one stone. After checking on Dorian Brendon, she could see what Barney was up to. 
She was not happy with how she and Barney had left their last conversation. It would be much better to spend some time face-to-face. It was late afternoon. By the time she was done with the Brendon kid, she could spend a portion of the evening with Barney and speak more clearly about her feelings. She might as well lay it out there and see if they have a chance or not. The thought of being that open was forming a tightness in her chest and a lump in her throat. She realized, just sitting there in her cold vehicle, that she was beginning to perspire. She was really going to have to force herself to be straightforward.
________________
Chapter 6


        Barney was seated at his table, eating two fried eggs. As he stared at the meal, he absentmindedly swirled his fork in the food, forming a yokey puddle on his plate. He watched as it oozed and surrounded the egg whites, creating a yellow moat around them. There were several things on his mind; first and foremost was the Lockwood. He had forced himself to walk away from the front door of the place. It was difficult for him to understand the tremendous energy that was pulling him into the structure. He just knew he had to fight it for a few more days. That was going to be quite a task, a battle of wills. His was losing ground as the power of the building seemed to be growing. That darkness he had kept hidden for so many years was festering within and building a bond with the spirits of the Lockwood.   
        Sally was on his mind as a constant reminder of life outside of his little world at the sanatorium. She represented an alternative future. One that would be in the light. She was warm, while the Lockwood was cold. She was love, while the Lockwood was hate. She was peace, while the Lockwood was chaos. She was a hug, while the Lockwood was a trap. It was an obvious choice, but the darkness in Barney wanted what it wanted. Though his mind easily knew the logical path he should take, it could not control his spirit. That spirit wanted out of the confines of his mortal being. It was a prisoner of his human needs, and it felt stifled by his living existence. 
The urges that his mind was enslaved to were dark and inhuman. Brought on by a childhood disrupted by pain and suffering, and then emotional abandonment, his inner self needed release. Thinking about his inner conflicts was depressing and driving him more and more into a confused personal hell. It was swirling around and around until he felt he was on a merry-go-round and grabbing for brass rings. Each ring was different. One was Sally, the other the Lockwood. Both were just out of his grasp. Around and around like a cyclone, the voices and faces of the Lockwood intermingled with the thoughts of Sally. The mental storm had become unbearable.  
“Stop! I can’t take this anymore. No wonder I lose time here. It must be my way of escaping the noise and chaos. I need peace for just a while, I can’t think anymore. Leave me alone for one night, leave me alone.”
Barney was tired. He took his plates to the sink and walked into his living room. Distraction was his only course of action. He turned on his television, hoping he could get interested enough to keep his mind occupied. It was a strategy he had little faith in. There was no likelihood of finding anything that could compete with the draw of the Lockwood or his feelings for Sally. Though destined for failure, he was hoping to at least watch himself to sleep. As tired as he was, there was perhaps a chance of that. 
He looked at the clock; it was nearing 8:00 pm. He thought for a moment about how it had gotten that late. It had just been late afternoon when he came back to his apartment. There were nearly three hours that he was uncertain about experiencing. The loss of time was happening more frequently. It was starting to feel as though he was living two lives, neither of which was aware of the other. He could not focus on that right now; he needed to get some sleep. 


*****
                                                                                                                                        
  Deputy Sally Thomas had driven to the Brendon estate. Stopping at the gate, she surveyed the property. There was a winding path to the house, with decorative streetlights illuminating the driveway. The light cast by them lit up the landscape to either side. She noticed the tree that had burn marks and large gouges. It seemed so out of place among the carefully manicured evergreens and the professionally landscaped yard. She wondered if that was where Blanchard had seen the lights early in the morning.
