The home had exterior lighting that showed it off. It was large with a modern, angular design. From where it sat on the hill, the large windows must have afforded views of the countryside nearly ten miles away. The wood and stone exterior of the home wrapped around the front door, forming a small courtyard. There were a few lights on in the house.
The deputy pulled through the gate and drove up to the house. Circling near the front door. She exited the car and waited a moment to see if someone would step out to greet her. The whole place, though beautiful, had a stark coldness about it. It seemed frozen, as if for a photograph for some architectural magazine. Not really a home, more like a monument to something.
Sally walked through the courtyard to the front door and rang the doorbell. There was no answer. She walked around the house looking for signs of life. When she saw the garage area on the side of the home, she looked in the window to see if there were any cars inside. There were several in the garage, but there was an obvious open spot inside the first garage door. That could be where Dorian parked. She needed to know what Dorian drove. She could look up the license plate numbers, but she anticipated the Brendon’s owned multiple vehicles, and determining which one Dorian drove might be difficult.
She returned to her vehicle and ran a license plate lookup for the household. She found seven vehicles. Through a process of elimination, she narrowed it down to either a BMW 228i or a Toyota GR86. Neither of those vehicles was in the garage. Sally wanted to do further searching on the property, but that would be stepping over the line of legal searches. She would need a court order to enter the garage or the house. If there was no success, she could request a wellness check, based on Dorian and the others having gone missing. For now, she needed to alert dispatch of the cars and license plates that they needed to be looking for.
“Dispatch, Thomas here.”
“Go for Dispatch.”
“I have two vehicles I need found as part of the missing Osborn kid case. I will text the license numbers and the makes and models. One of them should lead us to Dorian Brendon. He is being sought as a person of interest for the Osborn kids. He is either with them or may know of their whereabouts.”
“Got the numbers. You want these out to all units?”
“That is correct.”
“Okay, Sally, is there anything else you need?”
“No, I’ll be coming in after one more stop. I am going to be requesting a judge's permission to enter the Brendon home. There is a chance the kids are in there.” 
“Are you saying they could be harmed and trapped in there?”
“No, well …  I don’t know. At this point, we need to find those cars. It looks like Dorian Bendon drove out of the garage at his house. That means the car is out there somewhere. I need it on the BOLO (Be On the Lookout) list. I think they could be running.”
“Okay, I’ll get it moved out to the BOLO. That will kick in the ALPRs. We should get some kind of tracking from the Automatic License Plate Readers before morning. If that car moves anywhere near the interstate or in the city, we will see it.”
“Good, as I said, I have one other stop before I come in. I should be returning in the next ninety minutes.”
“I’ll mark your log.”
“Thanks, Out.”
“Okay, End.”
Sally looked at the time; it was 8:00 pm. That shouldn’t be too late to stop by the Lockwood and speak with Barney. She needed to see his face. She pulled onto the road and drove the ten miles to the sanatorium, thinking about what she was going to tell him. She was determined to lay the cards on the table. She had spent the last thirty years wanting to share with him how she felt. The opportunities had come and gone, and she had remained silent. Even in the most recent situation, she had only mustered the courage to say she CARED about him. She emphasized the word, but that was a weak substitute for saying what she needed to say. She loved the man and needed to use those words.
Why would he leave his job for someone who just cared for him? That was no commitment. She needed to make it crystal clear. 
Crystal, why did she have to use that word? It made her think about what she should be working on. Here she was spending time on her personal life and feelings, when there could be a young girl somewhere, wondering why no one is helping her. 
The gate for the Lockwood was just about a quarter of a mile away, but she could not continue. There were kids who needed her to do her job. She needed to wait until she had completed her responsibility to them. She turned around and drove down the road away from the Lockwood. 
“I feel so confused. Am I driving away because I need to do my job? Or am I running away because I have trouble facing my fear of exposing my feelings? I really don’t know. It’s ridiculous that I can’t just let myself be real around the people who are important to me. I can’t fix that right now. I can do my job. I will head back to the office and start the paperwork for a warrant to search the Brendon home. And I need to stop talking to myself.”
Sally was feeling tormented by her own introversion when it came to her feelings. She felt trapped by her inability to share herself. That was what had driven her loneliness for the majority of her life. 
        The drive took half an hour. She pulled into the parking lot of the Sheriff’s department and sat in her car for a few moments. She was trying to gather her thoughts and put them in the right perspective. Eventually, she felt in control enough to enter the building. As she walked into the office, she saw Deputy Fred Wareing. Fred had been with the department for nearly thirty years. When you were struggling with a case and needed advice, he was the go-to guy on the force. He had been through just about every kind of situation imaginable. 
        “Hey, Fred. Do you have a few minutes?”
        “Hi, Sally, for you, I have all the time in the world. What do you need?”
        “I have a difficult case I’m working on.”
        “I heard about the missing kids. The Osborns are well known.”
        “I assumed they had some influence. I interviewed the dad. The main thing I came away with was that they had money and didn’t pay much attention to their kids.”
        “That is not surprising. They are too busy attending social functions all over town.”
        “Okay, so I am in the weeds on this one. I’m dealing with kids messing with the occult, and I’m concerned about someone out there who is mutilating animals. I’m worried they could have taken the next step. Or these kids could have just taken off on their own. They have plenty of cash to do so. The point is, I don’t have anything solid to work with or start from.”
        “I see your issue. The best advice I can give for that kind of situation is to just trust the process. Follow every lead and look for details. Have you spoken with their friends?”
        “Yes, that was where I learned that Crystal Osborn’s boyfriend, Dorian Brendon, is into the occult. Not just a little either. The kid wants to try to summon a demon.”
        “That should be a big red flag. Have you spoken with him yet?”
        “No, he did not answer the door at his home. It looked like he was gone from the vacant spot in the garage. We have a BOLO out on two vehicles. I think one of them has to be his.”
        “That’s good. Have you looked into his bank account? That would tell you if he has pulled any large sum of money.”
“I have started the paperwork for that kind of search. I intend to do a thorough search of the Brendon home.”
“That is important. If the kid is into the occult, is it possible he is behind the animal mutilations?”
“I’m thinking it is very possible that they are involved in them. What little knowledge I have of such things, animal sacrifice is an important element, particularly for rituals.”
“Kids, the crap they get themselves into is pretty wild. What are they thinking?”
“I don’t believe they are thinking. They are reacting to the lack of parental care. These kids are paid off to stay out of their parents' way. What I learned from my interview with Osborn was that he had no idea what his kids were doing or where they might be.”
“So, you have to search wherever they were playing their occult games.”
“I think that is the Brendon home.”
“Now, you are walking on some pretty tender ground. The Brendons own half the politicians in the area. You make a false move out there, and we have major issues.”
“Yeah, I figured that out. That is what’s holding up my investigation. I need to exhaust my efforts to find Dorian and his vehicle before I request the search. Since I’m investigating the possible harm to underage individuals, I believe I will get the warrant quickly.” 
“I think you are doing everything right, kiddo. I think you have to let the BOLO efforts run their course through tomorrow.”
“In the meantime, I have Dober Grumby.”
“Is that guy still around? I would have thought he’d have died by now. What does he have to do with this?”
“I think he also could be the animal mutilator. He ran me off his place when I tried to just stop and ask a few questions.”
“Do you have anything to take to a judge to justify a warrant?”
“Not really, it’s just a gut feeling. I am worried if he is the guy; he lives close to where these kids were going to do their silly ritual, out at the Lockwood. He could be holding them or worse in that creepy cabin of his.”
“You need something to give the judge cause. No judge is going to go on your gut feeling.”
“I know, that’s what grinds me about him running me off. If I could have gotten a few questions with him, I think I could find something.”
“You need to find another way. Does the guy have any friends?”
Sally thought about Barney; she didn’t want to pull his name into the discussion. 
“I don’t think he has many of those in his life at this stage.”
“No, I don’t suppose he would.”
“Thanks for spending the time to talk. I needed that, just to help organize my thoughts.”
        “No problem, I may ask you to do the same for me sometime. You got a good mind for this stuff. I’ve been impressed with your work since you joined us. I wasn’t sure a city cop would fit in with us country folk.”
Fred was smiling as he gave Sally both praise and some good-natured ribbing.
“Well, some of you country folk are pretty savvy about how things work around here. I try to learn from the best.”
Sally returned the collegial smile and the two parted, going their separate ways. It was going on 10:00 pm. Sally was dead in the water until she had a hit on the BOLO list with either of the cars she was looking for. She decided to do her paperwork and head home to get a couple of hours of sleep.
While she was writing up her shift notes, Deputy Larry Suttle handed her a BOLO report. The BMW was found at the Chippewa Valley Regional Airport.
“Do we know who drove the car out there?”
“No, the license plate was picked up by the security guards and called in.”
“Okay, I guess I need to head out there.”
“Tonight?”
“Yeah, I don’t want to wait until morning. There are kids' lives at stake here.”
Sally wrapped up her paperwork and headed to her vehicle. It took twenty minutes to get to the airport, which was three miles outside of the city. Once there, she started looking for the car in question. She found it parked near a private hangar. Inside the hangar, two gentlemen were working on a small jet.
“Excuse me, I’m Deputy Thomas with the County Sheriff’s Office. Could either of you tell me who drove the BMW here?”
A man she presumed was an aircraft mechanic looked up from his work. His hands were those of a person immersed in oil and grease. His coveralls were stained, and he had that skin tone that only comes from years of smoking, proximity to oil, and weathering from the elements outdoors.              
“The one just outside?”
“Yes, just around the side of the building.”
“That belongs to Buck Brendon.”
“Do you know if he drove it here?”
“I guess so. I didn’t see him personally park it there, but his wife and him dropped it off when they took their jet out of here a few days ago.”
“You are sure it was left here on that day?”
“Yeah, pretty sure.”
“Okay, thanks for your help.”
Sally walked out a little disappointed that the lead was a dead end. It did, however, tell her they were looking for the Toyota. That was a little progress. Sally called in to dispatch to remove the BMW from the BOLO list. She then drove back, switched to her personal vehicle, and went home for the night.
At home, the events of the day hit her hard. She had been going for nearly eighteen hours. She removed the belt and shirt, ballistic vest, and trousers and fell into bed. Exhaustion brought sleep quickly. It was not a restful slumber as dreams of teens being mutilated filled the night hours. The face of the aggressor was never clear. It would appear blurred or covered. She woke up several times, sweating with tears in her eyes. As the night wore on, the image of the teens stumbling blindly, grasping at their faces, pain-sticken from having their eyes burned out of their sockets, became more and more vivid.
Her gut kept telling her that Dober was the culprit. By 4:00 am, she was wide awake, and her mind was searching for a reasonable cause to give the judge for searching Dober’s cabin. She had to be missing something; the guy couldn’t be that good as to hide everything he had done.
Sally rose from bed and went to her kitchen to make coffee. As she sat at her table, she ran the facts of the missing kids through her mind one more time. The timeline was important. Crystal went missing on the day she tried to talk to Dober. Was it possible he had her in that cabin? Maybe that was why he wouldn’t answer questions and wouldn’t even come out of the place? If he was responsible for the raccoon mutilation, was he now using human subjects for his torture? 
The mere thought of it was driving the deputy crazy. She had to take a look inside that cabin, search warrant or not. Kids' lives were at stake. She couldn’t be burdened with the proprieties of the law. She had joined law enforcement to protect the citizens she served. That was more important than crossing all the t’s or dotting the right i’s. She was making herself angry. She couldn’t sit by and let harm come to the kids.
She stood up and started getting ready for the day. In the shower, the water seemed to cool her temper. She admonished herself for being ready to go off half-cocked. She would do a wellness check on Dober. If she happened to get plain view evidence for looking more deeply while on his property, she would go ahead and do that. If he acted aggressively, she could then act further. It wasn’t exactly by the book, but it offered a plausible rationale.
It took her about an hour to get out of her apartment and another half hour to get to the office. She met Fred when she arrived.
“Well, good morning, Sunshine. You are here early. I heard there was a call late last night. I thought you might call in for the morning off to catch up on rest. You’ve been going nonstop for a couple of days.”
“It’s these kids, Fred. I can’t rest until I know they are okay.”
“That is great intentions, Sally, but you can’t do anyone any good if your reasoning is dulled by lack of rest.”
“I know, I got a good three hours in last night.”
Fred smiled. He knew she was probably exaggerating even at three hours. 
“All I’m saying is you have to take care of yourself, or you can’t take care of anyone else. Also, when you are tired and frustrated, you make bad decisions about sticking to the law. I know, I’ve been there.” 
“Fred, you have a knack for saying the right thing when it’s needed, don’t you?”
“I try, Kiddo.”
When she got to her desk, there was a note from Deputy Suttle. A car matching the description of the Toyota GR86 she’d placed on the BOLO list had been located, apparently abandoned, on the east side of Eau Claire. The license plates were removed. She found that suspicious. Who would do that? Why would they do that? She pulled the VIN for the Toyota and grabbed the keys for her patrol vehicle. If this was the car she was looking for, she wanted to see it in person. Sally needed to get the forensic work going as soon as possible.


*****


        Don Musgrave was filming his partners in investigation, Spooky Boy Boo and Mary the Medium, as they approached the Lockwood.
        “Oh my god, look at the size of this place.”
        “Look at that place, it goes on forever. Are there three of those long wings? And it looks like three floors. Do we have access to the whole thing?”
        “Yes, we do. Once we get set up, we will go through the history of the place, Mary. I think we have a gem here. There hasn’t been anyone in here doing a full investigation since the Central States Paranormal Group did their initial intro video. It’s been amateur groups since then. We have an opportunity to do something really big here. I have a surprise in store for the two of you that we haven’t talked about.”
        “Oh no, not one of your surprises. We both know how those end up for us. One of us is going to be doing something we do not want to do, probably alone.”
        Musgrave smiled at the camera. 
        “I think our followers will be happy. I ran a poll online to see which one of us would have the opportunity I will be sharing later.”
        “Were you in the poll or just Mary and me?”
        “What makes you think I wasn’t an option?”
        “Were you?”
        “Okay, no, I wasn’t, but someone had to run the poll.”
        Of course, most of what they were going to film was discussed ahead of time, and they liked to set up the kind of banter that their followers had grown to expect. Once the cameras were off, they were all business.
        “Boo, I need you to get the drone shots. Take it down to the gate and bring it up over the building from the left. Then circle it around and bring it back across from the back.”
        “Sounds good.”
        “Mary, you and I will start setting up our control center inside. Nick, you need to get the power set up for the RV.”
        Musgrave stepped out of the RV and saw Barney walking toward him. 
        “Hello, I’m Barney Doyle. You must be the Paranormal Posse.”
        “Hi, Barney, I’m Don Musgrave, this is Mary the Medium, and this guy is Spooky Boy Boo. This guy is our RV driver and all-around support dude, Nick. ”
        “It is a pleasure, everyone. I believe I have the Lockwood ready for you. The electrical line is set up right over there by the front door. It will work a little better if you can position the RV parallel to the front steps. I can walk you in and show you the space you may want to use for your control room.”
        “Nick, go ahead and move the RV for the electrical. Boo, take the drone and get those shots. Okay, Barney, Mary, and I will follow you. I’ll be filming as we walk. We may use some of the footage in our opening montage. You don’t mind being in our video, do you?”
        “I’m sure you can find more interesting sights than me, but I am fine with you recording me. We will be going in the front door. The Lockwood has been my home for the past two years.”
        “So you are out here all the time?”
        “That’s right. I’m kind of a caretaker and security guard for the place.”
        “I bet you have seen a lot of things out here.”
        Barney looked at Musgrave. His words seared into him as his loyalty to the spirits of the Lockwood felt challenged by the statement. He was tongue-tied on his answer as he tried to shield what he had begun to think of as his community.
        “I … I can’t really say I … I’d rather you discover what is here for yourselves.”
        “That is an interesting answer. You sound like a man who doesn’t want to believe in the paranormal.”
        “Some may think that.”
        Barney was trying to change the subject.
        “What time are you planning to begin your actual investigation?”
        “We like to wait for the dark. Beforehand, we like to tour all of the spots we intend to cover. That gives us a better handle on where to record and when. We have several elements we record before we really get going.”
        “Well, here is the space I thought would work for your control room.”
        “Yes, this is good. Is there a lock on the door?”
        “I can rig one up for you. Why do you need a lock?”
        “We have just started to do that because we have had people sneak in during our investigations. We don’t need to lose any of our equipment.”
        “I think you will be pretty secure out here. However, I will get the lock on the door for you.”
        Barney walked out to get a lock and the tools to install it.
        “That guy isn’t telling us something.”
        “What do you mean, Don?”
        “I think he has had a major experience here. I would like to record him telling about it.”
        “He didn’t seem to want to talk.”
        “I know, but there is something that went down here that we need to try and replicate. I need to know what that is.”
        “You’re going to have to push him to get it.”
        “You know I can push.”
        Barney walked back in with his toolbox and a strap lock. He got right to work drilling some pilot holes for the screws he would be using to hold the strap in place. As he worked, Musgrave moved closer.
        “Hey, listen, I sense that you have had some real experiences here. It would help us out immensely if you could share some of them. We are going to be promoting this video, and it will mean a lot to the future of the Lockwood. I think Patty will be very happy if we get a good investigation here.”
        Barney knew what Dan was saying. He was prying into the darkness of which Barney was well aware. It angered him that this interloper was pressuring him to expose the very soul of his haven. The Lockwood had embraced him, made him privy to the existence of its eternal inhabitants. It offered its welcoming, open arms to him. Urging him with its siren call to stay and be one of them. 
How dare this internet sideshow barker ask him to lay bare the secrets he felt charged to protect? He had told Patty he would stay and take care of getting these people set up to do their investigation. He had not committed to betraying the confidence of his adopted family. 
“I would love to help you out, but I’m not a ghost hunter. I’m simply a man doing his job here. As far as I’m concerned, there are natural explanations for everything I’ve seen or heard. I’m not one to assume a bump in the night is evidence of spectral inhabitants.”
Don looked at him and could tell Barney was being stubborn and was not going to give up his information easily. He chose not to interview him for the video. They would have to find someone from the local area who was willing to tell chilling stories of fearing to enter the building. Musgrave liked to have some kind of buildup in the introduction of his videos to get the followers on edge. That made anything they found seem more haunting. He knew viewership was based on how creepy and scary he could make the place. Many of his followers were people who wanted to say, “I would never do that.”  The rest wanted him to find new places that could one day investigate themselves, or at least wish they could.
“Okay, I understand you are not one to tell your secrets. We aren’t going to disturb the souls here. We are very respectful. I don’t like the guys who try to provoke spirits or the ones who decide everything they experience is due to a demon. So if you change your mind, just let me know.”
Barney just shook his head and finished putting the lock in place.
“Is there anything else you will be needing? The sun should be going down at 5:30 pm. That is in about an hour. I will be making the rounds of the building right now to make sure no one has snuck in. We do get looky-loos now and then.”
“I hope our visit here has not been communicated to the surrounding community. That could cause all kinds of issues with fans trying to get a look at what we do, or worse, trying to join in uninvited.”
“No, we don’t tell anyone about who is out here doing investigations or when they are here. Your presence has not been announced anywhere.”
“That’s good. Okay, I’ll let you go ahead and check the place, and then we will start our tour before it gets dark.”
“Alright.”
Barney started to walk up the stairs. He liked to work top to bottom; it felt like he could rule out anyone being in the building without the thought of them entering behind him. As he walked, he thought about his future. What if this investigation received so much attention that the Lockwood became a prime destination for ghost hunters? That could be devastating to the inhabitants of his precious building. While the spirits needed energy from which they could refuel their life force, too much of that energy could warp their manifestations. Too many people wishing to find ghosts could actually create thought forms or egregores. Ghosts willed into being, having never been in human form.
The more he thought of large numbers of people invading his own fortress of solitude, the angrier he got.
“Can I stand by and let this happen? I am supposed to be leaving this job. I have already told Patty I was going. Now, I am not so sure. If I leave, who will stop people from exploiting the spirits of the building? I’m not sure what I can do.”
“About what?”
Barney was startled when a male voice responded to him. 
“Who’s there?”
“Sorry, it’s me, Boo. Did I hear you correctly?”
        “What do you mean?”
“About  … what? You said you didn’t know what you could do. I’m asking about what?”
“Oh, nothing important, just wondering if I should clear some of the debris that fell overnight. I had the place cleared out, but stuff falls all the time.”
Barney was concerned this guy had heard more than he was saying he had. He quickly moved on.
“I'd better finish up my walk-through and get out of your way.”
Boo watched as Barney hurried away. And pulled out his walkie-talkie.
“Bob, are you there?”
“Yeah, this is Bob.”
‘I just found that caretaker guy standing here on the second floor talking to himself. What do you know about the guy?”
“I think he is okay. He may be a little off, but Patty seemed to think he was a good guy.”
“Okay, I think I heard him talking about being worried about how much people were going to be exploiting the spirits of the building. I’m a little concerned the guy is thinking he has to protect the place.”
“He could just be worried about how busy he is going to be if our video goes viral. Let’s just get our stuff all set and meet at the command center. We only have a few more minutes of daylight.”
“Yeah, this place doesn’t have a lot of natural light opportunities. It’s pretty dark up here already. I’ll meet you at the home base.”
Spooky Boy Boo finished his tasks and headed to the room they had set up as their home base, or command center. 


*****


Four Hours Earlier


Deputy Thomas arrived at the location of the Toyota that was spotted on the BOLO list. The vehicle was behind a self-storage unit on Malden Avenue. There was something suspicious about the car. The license plates had been removed, the doors were unlocked, and there was nothing in the vehicle. It was as though anything that could identify the owner had been stripped of it. Even the VIN had been scraped off.  
The car was the correct model and year. It was the right color. They were going to have to check the engine block or the frame. Many people aren’t aware that the VIN is stamped in multiple places. Some are just a little harder to find.
While the VIN was being determined, Sally had called a forensic specialist to do a full examination of the car. That would take a couple of hours. She was very interested if there were any signs of foul play. Had Dorian been kidnapped and driven there by his kidnapper? Had he been lured there somehow and then taken? Did he drive there and remove the identification to assist him in going into hiding? She needed some answers to help her decide her next steps.
No matter which scenario was the case, if they could determine the car was owned by the Brendons, she had a solid reason for searching their estate. It took about four hours to complete the full search of the car. The most disturbing bit of evidence collected was microscopic blood samples found on the floor mat on the driver’s side. They were small enough to have been from an inconsequential injury or the result of an injury that occurred outside of the car and spattered inside. 
Once they found the blood, Sally was ready to take action. She had waited long enough. She suspected that Dober was involved. He had been on her mind for some time. If there was blood in the car, she felt that ruled Dorian out as being the perpetrator. 
The deputy did one last check on the evidence-gathering methods. She did not want there to be any loopholes that allowed the aggressor to walk from justice. So far, they had not found the VIN in the usual alternate locations. Whoever had removed the numbers had been quite thorough. At four in the afternoon, Sally opted to leave the vehicle and head to Dober’s cabin. She was going to do a “Wellness Check”. She knew she was on shaky ground. She didn’t have enough evidence to get a search warrant, but she just couldn’t wait any longer. Her head was filled with concern for the safety of the kids. 
Everything about that car seemed to point to foul play, and there was only one person that was on her list as a possible culprit. She left Malden Avenue and drove the thirty-five minutes to Dober’s cabin. When she was nearing the driveway to Grumby’s, she pulled over to consider her course of action. It was then that she saw Doc Blanchard’s van pass and continue down County Trunk Z. She paused her planning to consider what he was up to this late in the afternoon.
 She parked down the road from Grumby’s and walked toward the cabin through the woods that separated Dober’s land from the Lockwood property. As she neared the building, she slowed her pace, taking care to avoid unnecessary noises. The thought crossed her mind that if she was truly doing a welfare check, why was she sneaking up on the place? 
She was close enough to smell the cabin. It turned her stomach. She covered her nose as the wafting odor overtook her. If murder had a smell, this was surely it. She crept to the window of the cabin. When she looked into the room, she saw a table in the center of the room. Peering through the dirty glass, it looked like the table was stained, but with what? She didn’t see Dober anywhere. 
Continuing the premise that she was on a wellness check, she felt obligated to knock on the door. She adjusted the body armor and unbuckled her sidearm. She wasn’t certain what Dober would do if confronted. She reached her left hand to the door and gave three firm knocks, and announced herself.
“Mr. Grumby, this is Deputy Thomas with the Eau Claire County Sheriff’s Office. I am here to do a wellness check. Please answer the door.”
There was no response. Sally repeated her actions. As she knocked the second time, the door pushed in with a loud creaking sound. She stopped in her tracks. The deputy had to consider the situation carefully. It could be a trap. Dober had threatened her with a shotgun the last time she was there. Had he shot at her then, she could have arrested him for several reasons, particularly reckless endangerment with a gun. If she entered his house without his permission and without a warrant under the Castle Doctrine, he could believe he had the right to use deadly force. That would be inaccurate, but she could still be wounded or worse. Knowing Dober broke the law would be of little consolation if she were his victim. She drew her sidearm and prepared to enter with it positioned straight in front of her.
“Mr. Grumby, this is Deputy Thomas with the Eau Claire County Sheriff’s Office. I am here to do a wellness check. I’m entering the cabin with my weapon drawn. I mean no harm, it is for my own protection. Mr Grumby, are you in there?”
There was still no response. She moved very slowly, opening the door. The squeal of the hinges added to the intensity of the situation. The smell that presented itself as she entered was difficult to handle. She wondered how anyone could live in such conditions. The putrid odor smelled of mustiness, perspiration, rancid food, and rotting flesh. Altogether, it was an unbearable affront to her olfactory senses. 
The cabin was dark as night. The smudged, smoke-stained windows would have offered little opportunity for light any time of day. At dusk, they effectively blocked what little illumination there was available. Fighting through the awful smell, the deputy reached for her flashlight. She held it in front of her alongside her pistol as she walked around the room. 
What she found confirmed many of her thoughts and fears regarding Dober. She found the cages around the room. She lifted one of the straps connected to the legs of the table. There were dark red-tinted stains on the table itself, as well as the floor around it. Knives were stuck in the burnt-out fireplace as if they had been heated before use. This was a torture room; there could be no doubt.
The deputy did not touch anything else in the cabin. She did not want to disturb anything that could be considered evidence. She stepped out of the cabin, still concerned that Dober could be lurking somewhere in the now dark woods surrounding the place. She needed to call this in and get back-up in place, as well as forensic people working on identifying the blood and searching for signs of either of the missing Osborn kids or Dorian Brendon. 
She was regretting leaving her car so far away. She would have to walk through the woods to get to it. She circled the cabin to make sure there wasn’t anyone there to follow her. Satisfied that there was no one, she began her trek through the dense woods. 
“Okay, Sally girl, time to walk through the spooky woods. Maybe I didn’t think this through. This is not a good idea. The shadows seem to be moving all around me. I know it’s just my flashlight casting the shadow, but it’s still no fun.”
 Her feet made crunching sounds as she walked. At times, she could hear a second set of crunching sounds. The sound of a second set of footprints had her nerves on edge. She began to move more swiftly, but the who or whatever was following sped up as well. She was sweating and continuously looking over her shoulder into the darkness behind her. She was nearly at a run when she exited the woods near the road. She spun around and directed the beam of light at the woods. She expected someone or something to burst from the underbrush, but nothing showed itself. She backed across the road to her car. She had never felt more relieved from getting to her vehicle in her life. Inside, she locked the doors. She took a deep breath, still focused on the woods. Was it her imagination, or did she see the branches of a tree move? Her gaze stayed riveted on the woods as she called for backup.
“Thomas to Dispatch.”
“Go for Dispatch.”
“I need backup at the Dober Grumby cabin fire number 2345 County Trunk Z. Possible homicide. I need a forensics team as well. Suspect is at large and should be considered armed and dangerous.” 
“Where are you located?”
“I am in my vehicle parked on County Z near the driveway.”
“Backup on the way, fifteen minutes out. Forensics will arrive in thirty.”
“Got it, we may need to check the woods, contact Douglas for the dogs, have him on stand by.”
“Will do.”
“Okay, Thomas, out.”
“Dispatch, clear.”
Deputy Thomas sat waiting for the backup. Her mind was filled with the terrible images of what must have transpired at that cabin. Guilt began to creep into her thoughts. Had she allowed this to take place? Should she have forced the issue the first time she was at this place? When were those kids taken? She had done her job by the book, but did that cost someone their life? 
Sally had to put those thoughts out of her head. There was nothing she could do about what had already happened. Her guilt would have to wait. The backup would be there soon. The first thing she needed was a plan for how they were going to take control and secure the cabin until forensics arrived. Secondly, they needed to find Dober Grumby before he could do any more harm.
“Dispatch to Thomas.”
“Go for Thomas.”
“Forensics found the VIN on the chassis of the Toyota.”
“Did it match the one from the Brendons?”
“No, it was a car reported stolen from the Twin Cities.”
“What? Damn, we wasted too much time on that one.”
“That is how it goes sometimes.”
“Yeah, but we have lives at stake here. Okay, any word on the backup?”
“Should be arriving about now.”
Sally could hear the cruiser approaching. 
“I see them now, out.”
“Dispatch, clear.”
Sally stepped out of the vehicle and flagged down her fellow deputies. They pulled in behind Thomas’s vehicle.
“It’s good to see you guys. We have a nasty chore tonight, Fred.”
“Larry and I got here as fast as we could. What’s the situation?”
“Dober Grumby is our situation. His cabin is a nightmare. The place is a killing cave. We need to secure it and find Grumby. That freak has done some evil stuff. I never trusted that old piece of …”
“Whoa! Let's get to the plan.”
Sally looked at Fred and realized she was making the case a personal issue.
“Okay, we need to station one of us at the cabin to secure the space. The other two need to search for Grumby.”
“Are we sure the guy is anywhere near here?”
“His vehicle is still parked by the cabin. He has to be on foot. I also believe he was following me when I came back to my car through the woods.”
“Lock the vehicles, we don’t want him trying anything if he gets past us.”
“Good idea, Fred. Larry, I think you should secure the cabin, but all three of us will spread out and walk through the woods on the way to the cabin. This man must be considered armed and dangerous.”
“If we are going through the woods, we need to maintain communication at all times. In this darkness, we will not always be able to see one another. In thirty-second intervals, we need to sound out ‘all clear’. Do you both understand?”
Sally and Larry both nodded. The three deputies spread out along the road parallel to the woods. The February evening temperature was dropping pretty fast. Sally shivered as she stood waiting with her fellow deputies to search the woods. The frigid air was transforming her breath into vapor clouds. That gave her an idea.
“Unless he is holding his breath, we should see the vapor from his breathing. That should give us a little advantage.”
“Right, Sally. Are we ready?”
“Let's go.”
The three deputies entered the woods. Their lights waved from side to side as they worked their way through the underbrush of the forest landscape. The shadows created by the lights caused concern for Sally. It made it difficult to see what was real and what was the trick of light. Each of them stopped now and then to make sure of what they were witnessing. It took them nearly half an hour to get to the cabin. 
“I didn’t see a thing. What about you two?”
“No. Fred, we'd better be careful with the cabin. He could have followed me out earlier and then returned. He could be inside waiting for us to get closer.”
“You’re right, Sally. We should split up. Is there a back door to the place?”
“There is a cellar door on the backside. I don’t know if there is a way from the cellar to the first floor internally. I didn’t see one, but I didn’t want to spend too much time in there, worried he was out here, possibly coming in.”
“I get it. This whole area is pretty creepy.”
“Yeah, Larry, I’m ready for the forensic guys to get here, just to have more people around.”
“Okay, well, we need to do this. I’ll take the front door with you behind me, Fred. Larry, you go around the back and check the cellar. Go carefully with that. I didn’t check it when I was here before.”
“Don’t worry, I’m not taking any chances.”
“Are we all set?”
Fred and Larry shook their heads. 
        “Give me a few seconds to get around back. I want to enter there when you are going in the front.”
        “We’ll give you til the count of ten.”
        “Should work.”
        Larry walked quickly to the rear of the cabin. Sally completed the counting. She stood at the front door and announced her intention to enter. 
        “Mr. Grumby, we are Deputies with the Eau Claire County Sheriff’s Office. We are here to investigate a possible homicide. Make your presence known now!”
        The deputies waited for a response, but none came.
        “We are going in. Mr Grumby, we are entering. Put any weapon on the floor, or we will assume you are resisting!”
        “Ready, Fred? Let’s go!”
        The two deputies entered with their weapons drawn. Each held them out in front with a flashlight in their opposite hand. Once in, they separated and went to opposite sides of the front door. The lights were cast all around the room. It was empty. The place was frigid as there was no fire in the hearth. Deputy Thomas pointed out the table and the straps, as well as the stains on the floor and table.
        “This is what concerned me earlier. Someone or something had been tied to this table, and they were bleeding. That is not a normal situation. The smell of this place is not normal either.”
        Fred walked around, found a lamp, but it did not seem to have any electricity running to it, though it was plugged in. He set his flashlight on a small side table, pointing up so it could cast light around the room.
        “It’s difficult to see anything in this place. It’s a good thing the forensic folks will bring their own light setup. The woods did not tell us much. What is on the other end of it?”
“That connects to the woods that run behind the Lockwood. You can make it from here to the lower level of that place without leaving the woods. I think Grumby walks that way when he helps out over there.”
“He works at the Lockwood?”
        “Yeah, part-time.”
“Doesn’t Barney Doyle live out there?”
“Yes, he does, at least for the time being.”
“What do you mean, for the time being?”
“I’m trying to talk him out of staying there. It doesn’t seem to work with his mental health.”
“Since when have you been so interested in his mental health?”
“Barney and I are long-time friends.”
“Just friends?”
“Okay, sorry I mentioned it. We have more to do here.”
“Do we? I don’t think we have much to do until the forensic guys get here.”
“That is true, but we haven’t heard from Larry. I wonder what he is up to?”
        “I’ll go check on him.”
        Fred grabbed his flashlight.
        “I’ll stay here; the others should be showing up anytime now. I’m anxious for them to start their testing. I want to know if any of this is human blood.”
        “If it’s not, we may be out on a bit of a limb with this investigation.”
        “Limb or not, I think we had to check this out. None of this is normal.”
        It’s not that I disagree; I just know in this day and age, we get sued for overreach easily. I'd better check on Larry. Looks like he got lost.”
        Fred headed out the front door of the cabin. He walked around to the cellar door.
        “Hey, Larry. Did you get stuck down there?”
        There was no response, so Fred opened the door and walked down two of the steps.
        “Larry, are you hard of hearing? What are you doing down here? Larry?
        There was still no answer. Fred drew his sidearm and proceeded down the steps.
        “Larry, if you are playing a prank, I’ll brain you with this pistol.”
        Fred got to the bottom of the steps. The space was very dark. The dirt floor made no sound as he walked. The smell was no better and perhaps worse than the cabin above him. He swung the light from side to side, holding his gun out, prepared to defend himself. He paused as the light fell upon a pile of bones. They were too small to be human, at least an adult human. He moved the light along the floor from the bones to the furthest corner. The light discovered the carcass of a raccoon. It appeared to be frozen there, but the head was misshapen as if it had been smashed with something. There were no eyes. Fred stood looking for a moment, wondering what kind of person does that. He shook off the disgust and continued to search the area.
        “Larry? Where are you? What is going on down here …”
Fred’s words were stopped midword as his light illuminated the crumpled form of his fellow deputy. Larry Suttle was bent over awkwardly at the waist, sort of seated on the dirt floor. As Fred got closer, he knelt beside his friend and could see that his throat was slit and his eyes were gouged. It looked like both wounds had been inflicted with a narrow blade or a scalpel-like edged weapon.
        The deputy stopped himself from touching his brother officer other than to check for a pulse. The sight was very upsetting to Fred. Though he had dealt with deceased individuals before, he had never dealt with a fallen comrade. He was trying to control his breathing, but between the shock of finding his friend and the stench of the place, he was hyperventilating. As he attempted to stand up, out of the corner of his eye, he saw a shadowed figure swiftly move toward him. Before he could react, a heavy, blunt object struck him on the back of his head. He was stunned and fell to the dirt.
        The shadowy figure moved quickly, taking advantage of the larger man’s dazed condition. He pulled out a surgical knife already bloodied from dispatching Suttle and plunged it into Fred’s throat, reducing his scream to a gurgling. The shadow man then used the blade to remove both of the deputy’s eyes, as the man writhed in pain. Fred’s life force ebbed as the blood poured from the wound in his throat and pooled in the gaping holes produced by the extraction of his eyeballs. 
        The assailant stood over the two deputies surveying his handiwork for a moment. The sound of the Forensic Van arriving shook him into action. He ran to the cellar stairs and bounded out of the cellar into the darkness of the woods, disappearing into the night.
        Sally was wondering what the other two deputies were doing. Fred had been gone several minutes when Tom Clark and Steve Trent carried their equipment bags into the cabin.
        “Hi, guys. I’m glad you’re here. This place is no treat to be in alone. The other guys are checking the cellar. I need to know as soon as you can tell me if these stains are human blood.”
        “Okay, deputy, I can run a Hexagon OBTI. It will tell us very quickly, less than five minutes.”
        “Great, while you are doing that, I will go check on the other deputies.”
        Sally left the cabin. She stepped a few feet away to catch a breath of air that was free of the fetid odor in the cabin. Once she felt her lungs were cleared of the stench, she headed for the cellar door.
         “Fred, Larry? Where are you guys? Did you hear the Forensic guys arrive?”
        There was no response from the cellar. Sally looked around the cabin to see if they were outside somewhere. She could not see them in the darkness, but assumed she would see their flashlights if they were out there. She peered into the cellar door and could see a light in the space under the cabin. 
        “Come on, guys, you’re scaring me. What are you doing down there?”
        Sally walked down the steps slowly.
        “Answer me, are you okay? What is going on?”
        The deputy drew her sidearm and prepared for the worst as she continued down the steps. Her feet kicked up a bit of dust when she reached the dirt floor. She stood with her back to the stairs and moved her light around the room. She first saw the small pile of bones. That caused a chill to run up her spine. She feared for what else she would discover. When the light found the two deputies lying in the dirt, she screamed.
        Her voice echoed up to the first floor of the cabin, bringing Tom and Steve running. The Forensic Team was not equipped to deal with dangerous suspects, but they knew they had a deputy who needed assistance. The two of them stepped down the stairs quickly. They found Sally seated on the bottom step.
        “What’s wrong? What happened?”
        Sally could not respond; all she could do was point the flashlight at the corpses of her fellow deputies. Tom moved forward to the bodies.
        “Oh god, oh god. They … they have … Their throats are … and their eyes! Oh god, their eyes!”
        “Are they …?”
        “They’re dead, brutally murdered.”
        “Who did this?”
        “Dober, that bastard. Dober Grumby did this! I’m positive of it now.”
        “Did you see this?”
        “What are you talking about?”
        “I think Fred was trying to write something in the dirt.”
        Sally did not want to get near her friends in their current condition, but if Fred was trying to tell her something, she had to look. She walked over slowly, trying desperately not to look at his face. Tears blurred her vision. She wiped at her cheeks and looked down at the dirt under Fred’s left hand. It looked like letters. She showed her flashlight directly on the spot, attempting to see what he had done. 
        “Does that look like a D to you?”
        “Yeah, and I think an O.
“D, O? He must have been trying to write Dober. Now we are certain of the murderer. I need to call this in right now.”
        “We’ll take care of … this.”
        Sally nodded. Tears filled her eyes as she walked up the cellar stairs. How could this have gone so horribly wrong? She went to the van while Tom and Steve tended to their fallen brothers in law enforcement.
        “Dispatch, this is Thomas. Officers down, Officers down! I need everyone.”
        “Dispatch to Thomas, I can have three cars en route in minutes. What is the situation?
“Deputies Suttle and Wareing are down. They were murdered by Dober Grumby.”
“Do we have an eyewitness?”
“Sort of, they were attacked in the cellar of the Grumby cabin. I’m sure it was him. Fred wrote D O in the dirt. I’m certain he was telling us it was Dober. We were investigating the cabin when they went … they went …”
The deputy was having difficulty holding it together as she spoke. She had just seen two of her friends and colleagues with terrible injuries bleeding out on the dirt floor of a maniac’s cabin.
“Just send everyone! Is the Sheriff back from his conference?”
“No, he won’t be back for two more days; however, if he has just lost two deputies, I’m sure he will be heading back in the morning.”
“I need people here now! The killer is still on the loose. Now I’m certain he had something to do with the disappearance of the kids. I’m heading over to the Brendon Estate. I need to check whether those kids are in there. As god is my witness, if that bastard has harmed them … ”
“Sally, you should not leave there. Don’t go off half-cocked.”
“That was Fred’s advice. You can see what that did for him. I’m headed there.”
“At least wait until the other deputies get there. The Forensic Team should not be left without a deputy for security.”
Sally thought about the fact that two well-seasoned officers had succumbed to the killer and realized the dispatcher was right; she could not leave them at his mercy while she ran off to the Brendons. She told dispatch she would wait. 
“Good, stay on the line until they get there.”
“Yeah, okay.”
Sally was angry, frustrated, and filled with guilt. She had suspected this guy from the beginning. If she had taken him into custody when she wanted to. She could have saved her friends and maybe the kids. Tears started to form in her eyes. She wiped them, as her anger forged into a steel commitment to herself to get this guy.
“Tom, I’m staying as your security until the others get here.”
“Who is coming?”
“I asked for everyone. When they arrive, I need to go check the Brendon Estate. I fear the missing kids are there. With luck, they are just trapped; otherwise, there may be more work for you there, when you finish here.”
“Okay, by the way, not that it really matters right now, but the blood tested as animal.”
“So there wasn’t a person tied to that table, and they were bleeding?”
“That would be my analysis.”
“Well, there may still be a chance the kids are still okay. Even though that doesn’t fit the scenario. This guy is a real scary piece of work.”
“I thought Dober was just a weird little idiot.”
“That was what he wanted everyone to think. He was, or is actually, nearly a genius.”
“Dober?”
“Yes, Dober.”
“Wow, I would have never guessed.”
“The guy was playing us all as fools.”
“I hear the sirens.”
“Yeah, so do I. You two stay together. With all those sirens going, I think you’ll be safe until they get up here. I’m heading to the Brendons.”
“Is that a good idea with the killer still roaming around?”
“That is exactly why I need to get over there.”
Within moments, the Grumby cabin had a half dozen officers all over it.
Sally nearly ran to her car out on the road. It took her about ten minutes to get there. She jumped in and spun out on the gravel shoulder of the road. She wasted no time in getting to the maximum speed country roads can handle. She was sweating even though the temperature was nearing zero. She sped through the multiple turns on the way to the Brendons. She almost lost control at the driveway. She sped up the lane to the house, still lit up as though waiting for some cocktail party held by the wealthy couple. Sally slammed on her brakes when she reached the point near the front door. She hopped out with her weapon drawn and headed for the door. She knocked and announced herself.
“This is Deputy Thomas with the Eau Claire County Sheriff’s Office. Open the door.”
She repeated the statement one more time.
“This is Deputy Thomas with the Eau Claire County Sheriff’s Office. Open the door, or I am coming in!”
There was no response. She assumed there was an alarm on the door. That should alert the security company, and they would call the Sheriff’s Department. Sally kicked the door, and to her surprise, it flew open. It appeared the deadbolt was not set, and the alarm was not activated. Neither of those things was a good sign to the deputy.
“Dorian Brendon, are you in here? Crystal Osborn, Ben Osborn, are you here?”
Sally yelled the names several more times as she made her way around the home. There were no signs of struggle, and no one was in any of the rooms on the first floor. She started up the staircase, yelling for the kids she was hoping to find alive and well. On the second floor, she went from room to room. At the top of the stairs was a large suite with a king-sized bed, a sitting area, two walk-in closets, one for Mrs Brendon and one for Mr. Brendon, apparently. There was also a huge bathroom with a large tub in the middle and a shower area behind it that could have handled a full basketball team.
The deputy moved on to the room at the left end of the hall. The decor suggested she had found Dorian’s room. It was painted black, it had several posters of demonic symbols on the wall. The computer system was all top-end and set up for gaming. There were no signs of struggle or foul play. Sally stood, wondering what to think. She looked out the window at the outbuildings she had seen the first time she was there. From that vantage point, she saw something that struck her as odd. There appeared to be a log cabin behind the large garage she had looked at the last time.
She completed her search of the home and was standing in the large foyer when her radio startled her.
“Dispatch for Deputy Thomas.”
Sally had to steady herself before responding. Being in the large house alone with a killer on the loose was disconcerting enough. Being surprised by the radio going off while she was concentrating took things to the next level.
“Dispatch for Depuy Thomas.”
“Yes, Thomas, here.”
“Deputy, where are you?””
“I’m at the Brendon house. When we ran into trouble at Grumby’s, I had to check to make sure the kids had not been taken from here.”
“Are you in the house?”
“Yes, I am. I know I have no search warrant, but I believed they were in imminent danger. I acted upon the suspicion that Dober Grumby had done something to the missing kids. This house was the logical location where he would have taken them.”
“You may have trouble selling that to the Brendons.”
“If they can’t accept that I did it based on my concern for their child’s welfare, then I will take the consequences.”
“I’ve spoken with the sheriff; he is on his way back and should be here in the morning. Due to the events at Grumby’s, he feels you should have a counseling session before completing any further investigations. You’ve been through a lot today.”
“Did he specifically order me off the investigation?”
“Ahh … no, he did not give any specific orders, just that you should get the counseling session done before any further work.” 
“That sounds like before I come back to work, I have to do it. I haven’t left work yet, so until I go home, I figure I’m in a grey area.”
“Not sure that is going to hold any water tomorrow, but I’m not in charge, I just communicate what I’m told. Are you going to be at that house for a while?”
“I have some outbuildings to check, and I’ll be asking the forensic people to do a once-over to check for any signs of foul play or struggles. There is an issue with a tree here that has me concerned. It looks like it was hit and maybe caught on fire. It’s right off the driveway. I will want that checked as well.”
“Are you telling the forensic guys, or do you need it relayed to them?”
“I’ll take care of it.”
Sally continued, “What we do need is an all points bulletin put out for Dober Grumby. He is wanted in conjunction with the murders of deputies Suttle and Wareing. He is also wanted as a person of interest in the abduction of Crystal and Ben Osborn.”
“We have that out already. The deputies that showed up at Grumby’s cabin as you left it called that in.”
“Good.”
“Do you need backup at the Brendons?”
“I’m good for now. I’ll be calling for forensics soon.”
“Okay, I think you are risking your future by staying in the field, but more power to you, Sally. Dispatch, out.”
“Thomas, clear.”
Sally knew the dispatch was right; she was risking her career by entering the Brendons’ house without permission or a warrant, by not heading home until she talked with a counselor, and probably in several other ways based on the mistakes she had made in the investigation. 
If this was the end of her career, she wasn’t going out quietly. She had to get this guy, and nothing was going to stop her. 
________________
Chapter 7 


It was 10:00 pm, and Dorian Brendon was standing in the woods behind the Lockwood. He was thinking of what had transpired earlier in the day. He had called Dan Zinkie several times, but there was never an answer. He started to think Dan was avoiding his calls, so he drove to Dan’s house on Vine Street in Eau Claire. Dan’s car was parked in the driveway, so he went to the door and knocked. Dan answered after the second try.
“Oh, it’s you.”
“Yeah, it’s me. Why aren’t you answering my calls?”
“Listen, Bobbie and me ended up talking to the cops for over an hour yesterday. I don’t want no more trouble.”
“The cops? What did they want?”
“They wanted to know where Crystal and Ben went.”
“Well, what did you tell them?”
“I told them the truth, I didn’t want any more trouble.”
“What do you mean, ‘the truth’?”
Dorian remembered how agitated he had gotten. He didn’t like Dan’s attitude. He was not in the mood for some whiny fool to get in the way of his plans. When Dan didn’t reply quickly enough, he recalled asking more forcefully.
“I said, ‘What do you mean, ‘the truth’?’”
“Hey, don’t get all tough guy with me. I told them I hadn’t seen Crystal since the other night at your place, and the last time I saw Ben, you were sending him away from our practice meeting.”
“Did you tell them what we were doing?”
“They asked, and Bobbie and me told them all about it.”
“Do they know when we are doing the ritual?”
“First off, WE are not doing the ritual. I’m not wearing some old horror movie outfit and standing on that crazy house out at the Lockwood for anybody. I’m out, done with this crap. So’s Bobbie. I’ve gotten in enough trouble with you over the years! Anyway, we didn’t say when your little playtime on the Lockwood was happening.”
Dorian recalled how enraged he had become with Dan. Even now, as he thought about it he could feel his face heating up. That was when he placed his hand in his pocket and felt for the knife. His only thought at that moment was ending Dan Zinkie’s existence. He would have done it had Dan’s mom not pulled up at the moment he was about to strike. She exited her car and walked toward them. He remembered how awkward the episode was. 
“Dorian? It’s good to see you. You haven’t been over much lately. I bought some cookies at the store. Would you like to come in and have one?”
The opportunity to exact his revenge on Dan had been lost at that point, so he chose to just leave, but not before warning Dan.
“No thanks, Mrs. Zinkie. I have to go. I just needed to tell Dan about some stuff from school.”
“Oh, that’s too bad. I hope you stop by again soon.”
“Thanks”
Dan’s mom walked into the house. Once she was out of earshot, He remembered how he leaned toward Dan and told him to keep his mouth shut.
        “You’re pretty lucky your mom came home. If I even think you might have told someone when and exactly where the ritual is taking place, you won’t see another day! Do you understand me?”
“I understand you'd better get out of here. You should not ever come back here.”
He drove away after their confrontation, but the result of it was the fact that he was standing there alone in the woods behind the Lockwood. So much had happened that he could not explain. He had come this far, and there was no turning back. He had committed himself to this path. If all went according to plan, he would be the right hand of Balsadore by the time this night was through. Then none of what he had done would matter; he would be under the protection of his lord Balsadore.
As the thought of his ascension came to him, a strange smile filled his face. Nothing he had done would matter. He would have the freedom to play out all of his desires, and no human could stop him. He would act as Balsadore’s hand on Earth as he himself would transform into the demon’s form on Earth. It would be glorious. He would take his revenge on his parents for their abandonment, on his teachers for their lack of vision, by not seeing his potential, and on Dan and Bobbie for being false friends. His rebirth in the image of Balsadore was going to be perfect, absolutely perfect.
The woods behind the Lockwood were especially dark on the moonless night. The snow was deep in some places where the wind had pushed it into drifts around the trees. Between the drifts and the underbrush, it was hard walking. Dorian’s determination allowed him to ignore how cold his feet were becoming as they trod through the frost obstacles. He had worn the robe with the hood up. Unfortunately, though the fabric felt heavy, it offered little in the way of a windbreak. Air moved through the loose weave of the garment, sending shivers throughout the young man’s body. Now and then, the fabric of the robe would snag on low branches or the spiny bushes. He tore the garment at one point, which elicited several curse words from him.
The woods had grown right up to the walls of the sanatorium, so Dorian was not concerned with someone seeing him approach the place. At length, he found himself on the back side of the east wing. He had told Ben to prepare a means for them to climb to the roof from the exterior. It appeared that it had been a good plan as he saw the doors of the place all seemed to be chained and locked. That was a new development since the last time he and his “friends” had been there. The thought of the group of traitors he once considered his friends made him angry. They would all pay the way Crystal had. He envisioned them all being destroyed in flames. 
He had no time to think of that now; he needed to find the place Ben had provided for him to get to the roof. He began walking along the walls of the back of the Lockwood. It was slow going as the snow was deeper near the wall, where the wind had blown it into drifts nearly as high as his waist. He struggled as he worked his way around the building. Eventually, he found the spot where Ben had attempted to get the metal ladder positioned correctly on the wall. He had failed to get it high enough to reach the fire escape still mounted there. Dorian looked at the situation and could tell that he could walk the ladder up the wall by getting underneath it. 
The young man crawled under the large chunk of metal and pushed up with his back. He was able to lift it slightly and then step forward. With each lift, he moved it up about six inches. After working for a while, he stepped back to see how close he had gotten to the fire escape. It looked like he could reach it if he stood at the top of the ladder. He decided it was time to go for it. The night was wearing on, and he needed time to prepare once he was on the roof. He was a little concerned that the weight of his backpack was going to throw his balance off, but he was compelled to try. 
His first step on the ladder shook his confidence as it slid slightly to the left on the building. He stabilized himself and continued one rung at a time, moving along and up its surface. His feet were numb, and he was shivering, adding to the peril of his climb. He was three-quarters of the way up the slope of the leaning ladder when it slid one more time. He froze in place, looking down approximately fifteen feet. He took a deep breath and continued. He reached the point where the ladder was leaning, wedged against the wall. The fire escape was about six feet above. He would need to stand upright to reach the broken remnant of the ladder still attached to the building.
He first brought his knees up under himself, inching his feet into position flat on the rails of the ladder. It was at that point that he would need to release the hold on the ladder he had with his hands and stand up. The wind had picked up and felt like it was blowing him from side to side. He placed one hand on the wall and started to slide it up its surface. It was soon time for the final leap of faith and release the other hand. He placed the second hand on the building and slowly inched his way up. His legs shook under his weight, perched on the rails of a ladder that was barely leaning against the wall.
It was only another foot or so until he could reach high enough to grab the lowest rung of the portion still secured to the wall. He reached for the rung and realized he was still a couple of inches short of it. He could not see himself walking all the way back down and starting over. He would have to get on his tiptoes to grab it. He started to push up on his frozen feet. It hurt to flex them up to his toes. 
“Just one more inch and I’ll have it.”
He stretched to his limit and got a hand on the rung. He gave himself a little jump to get the other hand on the rung. That was just enough to shove the ladder sideways, and it slid all the way to the ground. That left Dorian hanging on the bottom rung, twenty feet in the air with a pile of twisted metal beneath him. In the cold night air, he broke out in a full sweat. He could feel his palms sweating even as they gripped the frozen metal.
He had no choice but to pull himself up the fire escape. He placed a foot against the wall and then the other. He hung on for dear life as he released one hand to grab for the next rung up the ladder. In this fashion, he got to the point where he could get one foot on a rung. At that point, he hugged the metal and breathed a sigh of relief. That was when he had a chance to wonder if anyone heard the loud scratching and clanking of the ladder falling. The fact that no one had come to investigate gave him some comfort. He needed to get on the roof, out of sight, in case someone came.
He climbed the remaining eight feet and threw himself onto the roof. That was when he stopped to consider the safety of the roof. He hadn’t thought about the fact that the place had not truly been maintained, and the roof could be weak in spots. Additionally, it was snow-covered, another consideration he had overlooked. 
“Dorian, you did not fully think this plan through. I need to clear a spot for my ritual.”
He began kicking snow away from a spot on the roof of the main building. That was the original stone structure. He selected a spot between the gargoyles that stood watch over the main entrance. He needed about a six-foot circle in which to draw his pentagram. He worked for some time getting the snow moved. It was difficult as the wind persisted in blowing it back at him. Finally, he was satisfied; he could create his drawing. Dorian looked at the time. It was nearly 11:00 pm. He had a couple of hours to prepare all of the components he would need, so he began setting things up in earnest.


*****


Three Hours Earlier


        Musgrave, Mary the Medium, and Spooky Boy Boo were seated on the staircase of the grand entry. They had spent the last three hours touring the building and setting up their equipment. The control room was complete, and the three monitors were collecting footage from the stationary cameras they had located around the complex. Their process was to collect as much footage in as many locations of the building as possible to use as filler around their individual footage taken as they worked in teams and alone, investigating. 
They hoped to get at least two videos out of the footage they collected at the Lockwood. If they could get a third video, it would be a rousing success. Content was the lifeblood of what they did for a living. They each had other jobs. Don was a commercial film editor, Mary had several clients for whom she performed psychic wellness sessions, and Spooky Boy Boo did announcer and voice-over work. Their main income was derived from the monetization of their YouTube channel. Once they achieved a million views per episode, they were set. 
The business side of their work had changed the dynamic of their investigations. Maintaining interesting content was so important that they often had to exaggerate their findings a little, or at least overreact to some sounds and shadow anomalies just to keep the intensity at a level the viewers found intriguing. They never fabricated evidence, just a little showmanship. Don was realizing the joy he once felt from the investigations was diminished by the constant need to film something that appeased the ever more demanding followers. Such was the life of a nomadic paranormal content creator.  
They had developed a format that their viewers had come to appreciate. They would film the arrival and the introduction to the facility. They would add footage of the three of them essentially playing around the place in a lighthearted excitement about what they were about to do. The viewers liked that part as it made them feel like friends on an adventure. 
Next, they would show some of the technical setup so the viewers could tell they were coming at the investigations from a logical, evidence-based point of view. They would then film the tour of the facility, showing the creepiest, spookiest elements that they would be experiencing alone or in pairs later. They would then do a reading of a packet of information about the history and documented hauntings associated with the building or complex. 
After that, they would investigate and finally separate for the final three to five hours to experience different parts of the building alone. It was during this portion that they would try experiments or challenges suggested by the viewers. That could be going for extended periods with no light, or being handcuffed to immovable objects for periods of time. Some of these challenges had gotten pretty intense, but that was what their followers wanted. Providing that vicarious thrill was the key to increased viewership.
        “Okay, guys, it’s dark enough for us to read our packet of information about the Lockwood. I know we have been here for a couple of hours just touring the place, it’s time you learn what all has happened here.”
        “I’ll start reading.”
        “Okay, Spooky, go for it.”
        “Nestled in the rolling fields of north central Wisconsin sits the Lockwood Sanatorium. Built on farmland donated by Josiah and Mary Lockwood. The original three-story building was constructed from timbers supplied from the Lockwood sawmills and rocks collected from the Chippewa and Eau Claire Rivers. Some have said the stones carried the spirits of Native Americans and that energy was imbued upon the structure from the start. 
        “It operated as a hospital servicing the western part of Eau Claire County for twenty years. During that time, there were at least four hundred documented deaths on the property.”
        “Mary, do you want to take over reading?”
        “Sure, this is probably the creepy part. Don always likes to give that to me.”
        “Hey, I just think your reading fits it well.”
        “Don’t try to make it a compliment, we know what you’re doing.”
        “Just read it already.”
        “Okay, okay.”
        “Over those twenty years, the Lockwoods subsidized the hospital as it lost money every year. When the Lockwood fortune neared its limit, Mary closed the hospital, climbed to the second floor of the grand entry, and hanged herself.”
        “Wait a minute, so she hanged herself right here?”
        “That’s right, Mary. She was hanging directly above us.”
        As the trio looked up at the balustrade above them, they all heard a woman’s scream. 
        “Oh my god! Did you hear that? Did you hear a woman’s scream?”
        “Yes, I did, Spooky. Right on cue, after Mary read that part of the story. Was that real, or is someone playing tricks on us? Did that caretaker ever leave the building?”
        “I saw him leave a couple of hours ago. He could have gotten back in.”
        Don grabbed for his walkie-talkie.
        “Nick, have you seen the caretaker?”
        “He came out quite a while ago and went down to the end of the left wing and went into his apartment, I think. I haven’t seen him since. Why?”
        “Never mind, let us know if he is moving around okay?”
        “Sure.”
        “That was strange.”
        “If you think that was strange, let me read this next part of the packet.”
        “Go ahead.”
        “It was said that her scream could be heard from miles away. To this day, a woman’s scream can be heard around the time Mary committed suicide on that day, the 14th of February 1921.”
        “Are you kidding me? We are here on the anniversary of Mary Lockwood’s death?”
        “That was one of the surprises I told you two about. We happened to schedule our investigation around the anniversary of Mary’s suicide.”
        “That makes the scream we heard all the more creepy.”
        “That is not all. The Central States Paranormal Group (CSPG), which we booked this through, had a group do an investigation two years ago, and they documented the woman screaming as well.”
        “That is amazing.”
        “I think there may be a few more surprises for us, Mary. Spooky, why don’t you finish the packet?”
        “Okay, if I can hold it together to do so. That scream on cue really has me wondering about this place.”
        “Go ahead, the packet has some more very interesting tidbits.”
        “Soon after its closure, Wisconsin began experiencing the spread of Tuberculosis. They needed treatment facilities. They took over the hospital and added the three wings. Over the next twenty-five years, over ten thousand deaths were recorded.”
        “Ten thousand deaths, just think of the energy released within these walls?”
        “That has to have left a residual life force here.”
        “Three of the deaths recorded were not due to the deadly disease. They were the result of misplaced love. Doctor James Zuwiki was one of the leading practitioners at the Lockwood. He was a tall man with what has been described as notable good looks. The doctor viewed the nursing staff as his own personal stable of consorts with whom he could have his way. Three such nurses, all married, fell for the good doctor. The first had the misfortune of becoming pregnant with the doctor's child. Her husband threw her out of his house, and when the doctor turned her away, she killed herself. On a chilly day in April of 1930, she climbed to the roof and bared her bosoms to the wind and then threw herself onto the front steps of the Lockwood.”
“Wow, a second suicide? We have seen in our previous investigations that when someone takes their own life, they leave very complicated energy behind. Those have been some of our most active experiences.”
“You are right, Spooky, keep reading; there are more.”
“More suicides?”
“Just keep reading.”
“Over the next few weeks, after the nurse’s death, Zuwiki became involved with two more married nurses at the same time. When they learned about each other, there was a tremendous argument. To keep the scandal somewhat quiet, Zuwiki talked the women into going to the roof with him, where they could talk it through in private. When both women plummeted to the steps in almost the exact location as the first, there were a lot of questions. Zuwiki’s story was that he tried to talk them down, but they flung themselves off nearly simultaneously. Zuwiki was never prosecuted for the deaths as they were declared accidental.
 “Zuwiki continued working at the Lockwood for one more year. A terrible accident ended his reign of emotional terror. He somehow ended up in the boiler room, where a broken valve scalded him to death. Some say his deep-seated cruelty manifests itself as a shadow figure that walks amongst the spirits of the Lockwood."
“Between the sheer numbers of deaths and the emotional and psychological upheaval experienced here, this place has a lot of paranormal potential.”
“I agree, Mary, that is why we are here. What do the two of you say we split up and each take a wing of the building? We walk to the end, climb the far stairs, and then walk back to the center on the second floor.”
“You want to do that right after reading the packet? Right when we are on the razor’s edge of fear?”
“Exactly!”
“You have a true sadistic streak. Do you realize that?”
“It will make great content.”
“Always about the content.”
“Yes, actually it is, Spooky.”
Don smiled at his friend as they both knew he was right.
        The three of them stood at the bottom of the stairs, looking down three dark hallways. If one stared into the blackness long enough, they would believe they could see movement. It probably was just a trick of the shadows. 
        “Are we ready? Remember when you reach the end of the hall, you take the stairs at that end up to the second floor, and we meet in the center above where we are standing right now.”
        “Does anyone have a preference for which hallway to take?”
        “I’ll take the east hall, Mary.”
        “Okay, I’ll take the center one then.”
        “Sounds like I’ll be taking the west wing. Good luck, all. See you in about half an hour.”
        Mary started walking slowly at first as she got used to the layout of the hall. Between open doors and furniture sitting in the way, it was sort of an obstacle course. Almost immediately, she was feeling the presence of others around her. The feeling was not scary; the spirits were not interacting, just watching. She could tell they were timid, as though they were being restricted by someone or something. She could sense their fear and frustration.
