        As she walked, she began to pick up a very faint whisper. It felt like the whispers were not to her, but about her. It was like the spirits were discussing what she was. Or perhaps who she was. The whispers were constant as she made her way down the hall. When she reached what was nearly the halfway point in the hall, she felt drawn to a room. She entered, and the air seemed totally different from before, more electrically charged.
        She could feel someone standing beside her. Out of the corner of her eye, she saw shadows darting past the doorway. The whispers grew more frequent. She exited the room and continued moving down the hall, and then all at once the whispers stopped, the shadows stopped moving, and she felt the presence of something much darker, angrier, and menacing. Her skin was alive with goosebumps, and the hair on the back of her neck stood on end. Whatever had entered the hallway was different from anything else she had sensed so far. 
        At the end of the hall, Mary entered the stairwell. Feelings of being followed, almost hunted, were nagging at her. With each step she took on the stairs, she could hear the step of her stalker. Reaching the second floor, she quickly began to move toward “Grand Central”. She was no longer comfortable being alone with whatever was following her. She was happy to see Don’s light moving toward the central area.
        “Don, have you been hearing whispers? Faint ones, I can’t quite make out the words.”
        “No, so far I haven’t heard or seen anything. This place looks the part, but I don’t think there is much to find here, Mary.”
        “They’re here, I can feel them, and I see little glimpses of them. It is still too early for them.”
        “Mary, are you certain?”
        “I’m not exaggerating. This place is totally alive. There are hundreds of spirits here, but there is a problem. There is something else here, and it is stifling the spirits. This entity controls them. It stands in the shadows, dark and intimidating, watching the others. I feel that entity will show itself, but it is angry.”
        “Well, that sounds ominous.”
        Don Musgrave was not taking Mary very seriously. Though he was a true believer in the paranormal, he was not someone who accepted the idea of demons or anything beyond human spirits. When Mary started to explain that there was something there beyond the realm of his belief, he discounted its existence. 
        “I wouldn’t take this lightly. This is more than we have dealt with in any of our investigations.”
        Musgrave stopped filming and set the camera down so he could talk with his colleague without being recorded.
        “You may be getting a little over the top. We may not get enough tonight to sell everything you are promising with the hype.”
        “I’m not hyping anything. This place is dangerous. I have never felt spirits as afraid as these are. If whatever it is that is here decides to do something, it could be big and out of our control.”
        Mary was visibly shaken. It wasn’t her words, but the way she was acting that concerned Musgrave. 
        “Maybe we need to get you out of the building for a while to let you clear your head a little.”
        “No, I think I need to stay. You’re going to need me here when things start to happen. I just need to go to the control room and try to disconnect for a few minutes.”
        “Okay, while you do that, I’ll take Spooky with me, and we will explore the tunnels. I assume he beat us back and went to the control room.”
        The pair of ghost hunters walked from where they were in the center of the halls on the second floor, known as “Grand Central”, to the control room at the bottom of the steps in the grand entry. Spooky Boy Boo was seated in front of the monitors that showed the video feeds from around the building. 
“I’ve been watching the footage of you guys as you walked back here. I want you to look at it and tell me if you see anything. Don, I’ll show yours first.”
“What are we looking for?”
“I don’t want to say, it could contaminate your viewing. Just watch and tell me if there is anything you notice.”
“Before we do that, Mary says she feels the presence of something here that is much stronger than a human spirit. She will be sitting here for a while to kind of reset her sensitivity. I thought you and I would go down to the lower level where there are utility tunnels that extend under each wing.”
        “Yeah, that will be fine. Okay, so I guess I will show you Mary’s recording first and then yours.”
        Spooky hit the play button. There was Mary, walking slowly toward the camera positioned at the end of the hallway. 
  As the three of them watched, it seemed as if the darkness that surrounded her moved like a wave. Every so often, one could make out a portion of a body or a segment of a face seemingly peeking around a corner. The images were very faint, hardly discernible from the darkness.
“Look, there is a leg, a shadow leg. Do you see the eyes all sunk in? They are almost invisible, blending with the blackness of the hallway.”
“I don’t understand. There was nothing where I was walking.”
“Don’t be quite so certain. Let’s look at your walk.”
“Okay, show me.”
Spooky opened the clip of Don moving down the west hallway. For a good portion of the video, there was nothing out of the ordinary. Then, as he was about to reach the end of the hallway nearest the camera, a huge shadow started to form behind him. It loomed over him from the floor to the ceiling.
“What is that? I didn’t see that while I was walking. That thing is huge.”
“When I saw it, I thought that was not a human spirit.”
“That is what I have been sensing in this building. It is controlling all of the entities in this building. I don’t know what it is, but it is powerful. I have been seeing this thing in my dreams this past week. It doesn’t want to scare us out of here; it wants to make us part of its collection of spirits. It wants our energy.”
Don had worked with Mary for a long time. He considered her a friend, but the fact was that he wasn’t a true believer in psychic abilities. He felt she added something to the vibe of their videos, but her “visions” were more hype than help. 
“We need to draw it out. We have to get this thing on our next video.”
“I think that is a dangerous idea. There is a real chance this thing can cause us harm.”
“Let’s not get too far out there in our fear of the unknown. How many investigations have we done? I think it is over a hundred, and we have not been harmed once. We get a stone thrown at us now and then, we get some scratches, or we get sick to our stomachs. We have never truly been harmed.”
“Don, this thing is different from anything we have seen. If I were to take a guess, I think it was once human, but it has soaked up residual energy from the building and grown into something else, something older, more dark.”
“Wow, to hear you speak of it, it sounds like quite a monster. Why didn’t it attack us already, when we were alone?”
“It may be watching us for a while before it decides what to do.”
“I’m sorry, I’m not giving it that much credit. I just want to get it on camera again and see if we can communicate with it. I want to take the Spirit Box up to the second floor and see if it will speak to us. I think right now, Mary, you stay here. Spooky and I will explore the tunnel, and then the three of us will go to the second floor to see if we can communicate with the shadow. Is everyone cool with that?”
“Yeah, sure. I can tell you aren’t going to listen to reason on this.”
Mary was upset with Don’s lack of caution. She was a little hurt that he would not listen to her advice. She was equally invested in the success of their channel, but she knew that some things weren’t meant to be messed with. The content was not worth risking one’s safety.
Don and Spooky checked their batteries and started to walk to the entrance to the tunnels under the east wing. Mary sat watching the stationary cameras they had on each floor and each wing. Nothing was happening. As she sat there, she started to calm down. The energy in that room was much lower than in other parts of the building. There were no cameras in the tunnels, so she had no clue how the investigation was going for Don and Spooky. She fought the urge to call them on the walkie since calling them would probably scare them. That happens when you’re so focused in the dark. It had happened to her several times; once in a while, as a prank from the others. 
Mary checked the time on the computer; it was 9:30 pm. The guys had been gone for about thirty minutes. She assumed they would be at least another hour. She was getting kind of bored sitting there alone, just watching the camera feeds. Her senses had been on high alert earlier as she walked through the building. The adrenal high she had been on was crashing, and the inevitable drowsiness was beginning to overtake her. She could feel herself drifting as her eyes frequently blinked as she stared at the screens. Mary was nearly asleep when it happened. She barely caught it between long, sleepy blinks. There was movement.
On the second floor central wing, someone walked quickly past the camera. It took a second or two for it to register in her mind. Once she realized what had just happened, she sprang to attention. Fully awake, she looked hard at the feed she had seen it on. Nothing was happening; she needed to replay that section. She stopped the recording and rewound forty seconds. She then played it back with her finger ready to hit pause. There, there it is, she paused the playback. A figure in full stride was in the center of the frame. 
It appeared to be the full form of a man in overalls with a slouchy cap. The face was not completely visible as the whole figure had a mist-like quality, rendering it translucent. She had never seen a full form spectre before. Her heart was racing, and her breathing was coming in staccato puffs. This was big, huge. This would break the internet when it aired. People investigated their entire lives and never saw a manifestation like this.
Mary grabbed for the walkie-talkie. She needed the guys to see this immediately.
“Don, Don, come in!”
She was abrupt and loud. The perfect scenario to startle the person on the other end of the call. Both men jumped, shouting expletives. Don grabbed his chest, and Spooky leaned over with his hands on his knees. Don responded.
“Whoa, you know how that affects us when we are trying to be quiet in an investigation. You just scared the you know what out of us.”
“Sorry, I just saw something on the second floor feed that you need to see right now!”
“What is it?”
“You need to see it, it’s amazing. I’ve never seen anything like it, much less gotten a recording of it. Hurry up, you have to get up here and look at this!”
“Okay, we’re coming.”
The two men abandoned their walk through the tunnels and headed back toward the stairs to the first floor. Once there, they jogged down the hallway to the control room. Don stepped in, breathing heavily.
“Okay, what was so amazing?”
“I was sitting, actually getting pretty bored. It was getting difficult to stay awake, but then out of the corner of my eye I saw it. You won’t believe it, even though it’s recorded.”
“Okay, enough with the buildup, let’s see it.”
Mary brought the clip up and hit play. Spooky and Don looked in total astonishment.
“Run it again.”
They all watched it once more.
“That is something I have never seen before. That’s a full-body apparition. We’ve never caught anything like that in any of our investigations. That is going to make our year. We can count on this video going viral. We will be gaining a ton of followers. This is very exciting. Excellent catch, Mary. Did you see anything else while you were watching?”
“I did not see anything while I was watching, but as I said, I got a little bored. I could have missed something.”
“We are going to have to look at every second of these recordings very closely. We have our work cut out for us. There are so many things we need to do. I had talked about a spirit box session upstairs. I think we need to get that done right now.”
“How do you want to go about doing that? Are you thinking at full volume or are you talking about the Estes Method?”
“We may try both, but to start, I think we want to go full volume. I want to hear that shadow’s story. I’m willing to do about anything to get it talking. From what you have said, Mary, I think a little antagonizing would get a reaction.”
“I was afraid you were thinking of doing that. You have to believe me. Don’t get this thing upset. It is more powerful than anything we have come across before. Like I said earlier. I think it was human. If I were to guess, the individual was very evil in life, and that evil has grown in death. It could be Doctor Zuwiki, the guy involved with the nurses who were killed, or died falling from the roof. I think he was the embodiment of evil in life, and that anger and spite for mankind followed him into death.”
“Fine, but how does that make him the shadow?” 
“Spooky, there is plenty of negative energy all around this place, so much useless death creates an angry, aggressive aura. It needed an outlet, and Zuwiki accepted its power, taking it as his own. Over time, he has grown into this thing that we have seen and all felt.”
“Mary, I have always trusted your senses, but it sounds like you are making conjectures here that are outside of your expertise. I think we all can agree that between the scream, the full body apparition, and the footage of the shadow, we have a very active place here. We need to take full advantage of it. This one video could make us enough to cover the expenses for us all for the next year. Think of what I’m saying. We would have an entire year to do with as we pleased. We could do many of the investigations we decided were too expensive.”
“YOU CANNOT SPEND THAT MONEY IF WE DON’T MAKE IT OUT OF HERE!”
“Mary, give Don a break. He is just trying to make sure we are financially solvent. We started this channel with no money and no idea of where we were going. Look how far we’ve come. He has managed that. I think we can trust his judgement.”
“I have trusted both of you from day one. I understand the importance of the funding of our little team, but there comes a time when our safety outweighs our finances. I’m not joking or exaggerating the power of this entity. We are in trouble here. I cannot state it any clearer!”
“Okay, Mary, how about we compromise. We will do the spirit box, but we will not be disrespectful to your shadow monster. Can we agree on that?”
“Don, we are in this together. I am afraid of tonight’s outcome. I hope I am wrong, but my senses are saying we may not leave this place!”
Don looked at her like she had lost her mind. He was concerned that a spirit may be attached to her, causing her over-the-top behavior. There had been times during their investigations when they had to use the help of a shaman to cleanse them of unwanted attachments.
He decided not to confront that problem for now; he just could not trust her senses as he normally would. He would appease her concerns the best way he knew how, until they could get help in clearing the unwanted influence. It was nearly 11:00 pm, and they needed a break to calm their nerves and concerns. 
“Let’s take a thirty-minute break, eat something, and then do our spirit box session.”
The trio retrieved the food they had brought from their backpacks. They acted differently from other investigations. Usually, this was a time of conversation and camaraderie. On this occasion, they separated and spent the time quietly.


*****


        Nick Carter had been the Paranormal Posse’s support guy for the past three years. He had gone to school with Spooky Boy Boo, whose real name was Alan Parker, from first grade through graduation. He had always enjoyed working with video projects, and when the team needed someone who could edit and record the drone shots, he was their man.
        He took on those tasks, plus driving the RV. More recently, he started a tradition of cooking breakfast for the team on the mornings after they spent all night investigating. He had just finished mixing pancake batter. He liked to have that done the night before because he thought it made better pancakes after it had cured in the refrigerator overnight. He decided to take a few drone shots before going to sleep, until they were scheduled to come out in the morning.
He grabbed the drone remote and the small camera monitor and stepped out of the RV. The drone was stored in the storage compartment under the RV. He pulled out the drone and did a quick check on all of its functions. Once he was satisfied with its readiness, he got it in the air. With no moon, the visual was a bit surreal. There were lots of long shadows, areas of total darkness, and a great contrast of objects against the snow. 
His first shot would be flying over the entrance and up the driveway. As the drone reached the entrance and hovered over the gate, Nick noticed a van parked nearby. He flew closer to see if he could determine what it was, as it appeared to be a work van of some sort. The drone had to get very close for Nick to make out the writing on it in the dark. It read “Blanchard Veterinary”. He saw someone moving around, and then the van quickly backed out and drove away.
Nick thought that was pretty strange. It was after 11:00 pm, so why would the vet be hanging around the place? He tried the walkie-talkie to let Don know about the van and the guy. 
“Don, this is Nick. Come in, please.”
All he received was static. The communication was being disrupted by something. Maybe Don was in the basement or around the old wiring system. Nick figured he would continue his work and try again later.
He brought the drone up the driveway and circled the Lockwood. As he watched the monitor, he thought he saw something moving on the roof. He brought the drone around a second time. Whoever or whatever was on the roof scurried behind something so he could not get a good look at them. He really needed to tell Don about someone being on the roof.
“Don, can you hear me?”
No response other than static. 
“Boo, can you hear me?”
Only static.
“Mary, can you hear me?”
No response from any of them. Nick considered just going in, but that could ruin their video shoot. He brought the drone in and put it away. He took the monitor and the remote inside the RV and hooked the remote up for charging. He considered his options; he could not leave them in the building without them knowing about a person on the roof. Maybe their cell phones would work. He hit the speed dial for Don Musgrave. The phone went directly to voicemail. Now he was not just needing to tell them about the guy on the roof and the van, but he was worried about whether they were okay inside. He sat thinking of other options, but arrived at only one.
He decided to go in. He rummaged through his storage drawer and pulled out the small baseball bat he carried just in case they ran into trouble. Many of the locations they investigated were located in less-than-safe areas of larger cities. He readied himself to walk into the creepy building. Though he had been a team member on over a hundred of these investigations, he never spent much time in any of the locations. He wanted no part of the dark and dusty buildings or the possible spectral inhabitants they hosted. He certainly never entered these places at night. This was going to be a real gut-check moment for him. He opened the door of the RV and stepped into the cold night air, and was met with something hard and heavy crashing into the top of his head.
 Nick went down on the ground, and a pool of red quickly spread around the now limp form of Nick Carter. The attacker reached for the wrist of the young man on the ground. There was no pulse. The assailant whispered an inaudible curse. They had not wanted to kill Nick. It seemed they had other plans for him that were no longer possible. They kicked the body over onto its back. In the darkness of the moonless night, a faint glint of the knife blade was all that betrayed shadows as it rose above the hooded figure. It came down swiftly, taking the right eye from Nick’s lifeless form. A second thrust and the left eye was dispatched.
The dark figure stood up and seemed to be surveying their work. Apparently satisfied with their work, they moved to the front door of the Lockwood, humming some kind of song. Once there, they picked up a length of chain from the large cement pad surrounding the doorway. They wrapped it several times around the door handles and clicked a lock on it to hold it in place. This was the last door still left unchained from the exterior. The building was now sealed. Unbeknownst to the paranormal investigators, they had now become prisoners of the Lockwood. 
The hooded figure moved away from the door. They stopped to take one last look at Nick Carter’s now mutilated face before disappearing into the darkness.
________________
Chapter 8


        A van drove down County Trunk Z. As it neared the gate of the Lockwood, the headlights were turned off. The driver turned into the Lockwood driveway, stopped at the gate, and got out of the vehicle to open it. He drove the van in and then got out to close the gate. The driver got in and continued up the long drive to the complex of buildings. He drove off the driveway into the snow-covered secondary path that went around behind the building. The van struggled to make its way to the gravel lot in the back. He shut off the engine and sat in the darkness for a few minutes. 


*****
                                             
        Sally Thomas was now more desperate to find the kids and bring a killer to justice. She was regretting coming to the Bendons’ house. Sally had found nothing of consequence to prove that anything had happened there. She hoped the forensic team might find something to help with the case. When Sally had finished looking through the house, it was nearly midnight. She was nearing exhaustion. Her day had started at 4:00 am, and she had been constantly on the go and on edge. She hadn’t eaten and really would like to just sit down, but she had the outbuildings to check. 
She left the house through the front door and looked toward the garage. The deputy had already spent time on that during her previous visit. There was a large equipment shed and the log cabin she had seen from Dorian’s window. Sally opted to check the large shed first. As she approached it, she noticed that there were footprints that had to have occurred between the snowfalls. They were still noticeable but partially filled in from the most recent snow. She stood in the cold, trying to think of the timing of each snow event. It was now the morning of the 15th. It snowed on the 13th and then yesterday, the 14th. So whoever walked to the shed had to have done that after the snow on the 13th and before the snow on the 14th. So it was after 2:00 pm on the 13th and before 7:00 am on the following day. That only narrowed it to a fifteen-hour window.
Sally wasn’t sure if that meant much other than it could only have been Dorian if it was one of the Brendon’s tracks. Outside of the family, it could have been anyone else. She followed the tracks to the side door of the shed. The deputy was surprised to find it unlocked. It was quite large, probably forty feet by sixty feet. She felt around for a light switch on either side of the side door. Sally found it and flipped all of the switches. The entire building lit up. There was a tractor with a plow and what appeared to be the rigging to mount a lawnmower. There was a good-sized boat, a couple of four-wheeler type vehicles, and two vintage cars. Sally was no car expert; she just knew they were old, like from the thirties or something like that.
She noticed there was still a small puddle and snow remnants where the tractor had been driven into the shed. It could also have been returned to the shed after being used for something, but what? After looking around the building, she decided to look into where the tractor had gone. If there was a snow trail in the shed, there should be tracks outside. She had approached the shed from the side, so they should be out on the front of the building.
She exited through the side door and had to navigate a drift that had formed extending out from the corner of the structure. Once through the obstacle, she could see the tracks from the farm vehicle. They made a large loop down by the tree that had the burn marks and back up behind the shed somewhere. Sally was about to walk in that direction when her radio went off. Again, the voice surprised her, making her jump. She took a breath to settle herself.  
        “Deputy Thomas, this is Tom Clark.”
“Ahh … yeah, go for Thomas.”
“I thought you would want to know this as soon as possible.”
“What is it?”
“I think we found Dober Grumby.”
“Hold him there, I’ll be right over.”
Sally was excited at last; she was going to face that bastard. She started for her vehicle almost on the run.
“Wait, there is no need to hurry, Deputy.”
Sally stopped, confused by Clark’s statement. She responded, a little out of breath.
“What, ahh … what do you mean?”
“He is dead. We found him under the floorboards of the back room of the cabin. His throat was slit and his eyes … well, the killer gouged them out like the others.”
“What? That is not possible, Grumby is doing this. He couldn’t do that to himself. The body you found has to be someone else.”
Sally was frantic; she was positive Grumby was her man. All they had to do was find the creep. Clark had to be mistaken; the body must be someone else.
“No, we have positive identification. Two of the deputies investigating here know Grumby to see him. They both identified the corpse as him.”
“No, no, then who is doing this?”
“That’s a question I can’t answer, at least not based on what I’ve found so far.”
Sally walked through the snow, exhausted and on the verge of collapse. Tears froze on her cheeks as she held her hand to her forehead; thoughts spun around and around. She couldn’t have been this wrong. In a weak, raspy voice, she asked her next question.
“Wh … wh … when was Grumby killed?”
“It is a little difficult to say because of the cold weather he has been preserved. However, based on the level of freezing and frost burn, he has been dead for at least two days, but it could be much longer. We will need the coroner to do several tests, most of which are not very reliable, to get a better approximate time of death.
The detective was nearly despondent, all of the work she had put in, the loss of her fellow officers, one of whom she considered a mentor, it was all just too much. She spoke slowly and quietly.
“We are back at square one. I’m over here, at the Brendon estate. I guess I could use some help determining if there was any type of struggle or other violence here. I don’t see any, but something had to happen here. I can tell something happened in the driveway. With all of the snow, I can’t tell what all went down.”
“We need to wrap things up here. The coroner has arrived, so it should take about another hour. Are you going to be okay? It sounds like maybe I should send one of the deputies over for ahh … backup.”
“No, I have some outbuildings yet to check. Give me a heads up when you are heading over.”
Clark had already determined he should send someone anyway. He thought Thomas sounded like she could use someone to support her right now. He understood how emotionally involved an officer could get when they are trying hard to protect the public. When fellow officers and young people are involved, it gets pretty difficult to keep your perspective.
“Will do. Looks like the deputies here are wrapping up. I’ll let them know where you are and what is going on.”
Sally responded. Her thoughts were very distracted. It felt like the puzzle she had been working on had just been knocked all over the floor. She had no clue how the pieces fit together as she started over.
“Yeah, okay.”
“Hang in there; this is a strange one. Out.”
“Yeah, yeah, strange. Clear.”
Sally stood for a second, searching the sky for guidance. She fell to her knees in the snow, feeling drained of energy and hope. The clues were spinning in her head. The raccoon, the table at Dober’s was stained, but no human blood, three kids were missing all of it within a mile of the Lockwood. She was dumbfounded. What was Fred trying to tell them? If he wasn’t naming Dober, who was he trying to name? D. O., whose name could that be? Dorian Brendon? It could be the kid involved in the occult. That would make sense; he sounded a little whacked out, and his friends said he was over the top with the idea that he could summon a demon.
DO … Dorian! Yes, that had to be it. Sally felt a little rush of energy as she began to piece the puzzle back together. She needed to look closer here at the estate. If Dorian were doing this, there would be some form of evidence. The cabin! She had not investigated the cabin yet. She trudged through the snow until she hit the partially filled tractor tracks that seemed to be headed to the cabin. The walking was much easier on the track of the implement. It was about one hundred yards behind the large machine shed she had already inspected. It was surrounded by the trees of the same woods that ran through Grumby’s property and on up to the back of the Lockwood. 
She was now standing in front of the cabin. The cold air was starting to force her back to clarity. What if Dorian were in that cabin waiting as he had been in Grumby’s cellar? She could be walking into a trap. He could have been watching her the whole time she had been there. She should have told Clark to send another car over for backup. Her mind wandered to the ineffective handling of backup at Grumby’s. The tears started to blur her vision. She had to shake off the emotions; she had a job to do!
She followed the tractor tracks; it seemed as though they led behind the cabin. There seemed to be marks of something very large being dragged behind the tractor. There were large divots taken out of the ground where it had been pulled. She could feel those more than see them. She had to be careful not to twist an ankle in the ruts that had been formed. When she was behind the cabin, she trained her flashlight on a hulking shadow that was back there. As the light carried across it she could tell the twisted, burned mess of metal was a car. It had been rolled, and then it had hit something, probably the tree along the driveway, and then caught on fire.
Had Dorian had an accident in his own driveway? Had he hauled the car back here to hide it from his parents? It looked bad. It would have been miraculous if anyone had gotten out of the wreck before it caught fire. There was a remnant of a license plate. The paint had been burned off, but the relief of the stamped numbers and letters was still there. The deputy got close enough to feel the numbers like reading Braille. Once she had it completed on her notepad pad she tucked it away. She would do a plate search when she got back to her vehicle. If her memory was correct, the numbers she wrote were not a match for the two cars she had placed on the BOLO. 
Based on what she had seen at this place, there were plenty of cars, so it would not be a surprise if this was another vehicle owned by the family. She needed to get a look inside the vehicle. That was going to require her to climb a little of the wreckage. She needed to be careful; the killer, whoever it was, could easily be watching her. Once on the pile of car wreckage, she could get a light inside the passenger area. As she moved the light slowly around it caught a horrific sight that nearly sent her tumbling to the ground as she jerked back away from the view.
She shook herself back into control. Aiming the light back into the car, she looked upon the gaping, scorched skull of the presumed victim of the wreck. She could not tell if the body could be Dorian or one of the other missing kids. The puzzle had just presented another oddball piece that did not fit.
“Thomas to dispatch.”
“Go for dispatch.”
“I have a wrecked car with a deceased victim hidden behind a cabin at the Brendon estate. I need backup and forensics.”
“Another one? I’ll get backup there as soon as possible. Everyone available is over at the Grumby place.”
As dispatch was talking, Sally saw the red and blue lights of a sheriff’s deputy cruiser coming up the Brendon driveway.
“Dispatch, backup is already here.”
“Okay, tell them to call with a check-in so I know who is where.”
“Will do. Out.”
“Dispatch, clear.”
Sally walked the tractor track back to the large shed and then toward the arriving officers. They pulled up behind Thomas’s vehicle and stepped out. It was Deputies Bernedette Hart and Hank Stussie.”
“Hey Sally, how are you doing? Are you holding up okay? That deal over at Grumby’s was a mess.”
Sally really didn’t want to answer the question. She was operating on sheer grit by that point. She had no energy left, emotionally or psychologically, for reflection.
“I ahh … I … yeah, I’m okay. 
She needed to change the focus from herself.
“We can’t say as much for Fred and Larry. I was certain it was that freak Dober, but Clark said he was positively identified. Is that right?”
Even though she had asked her friend Bernedette, Hank Stussie answered Sally’s question.
“Yeah, I knew old Dober. I helped identify him. He was a mess, the same way as Fred and Larry. Who does this kind of thing? Why do they? I’ve been working this job for fifteen years, and I have never seen anything like this. The whole eye gouging this is really sick.”
Sally thought of the long road she had taken to get to where the case was at that moment. In almost a whisper, she responded.
“It all started with a raccoon.”
“What did you say? A raccoon? What do you mean?”
Thomas looked up, almost surprised by the question. She wasn’t sure she had mentioned the raccoon out loud. 
“I ahh … I first heard about it from Barney at the Lockwood, and then another one popped up at a farm nearby. Raccoons with their eyes gouged out.”
“So you think it is the same person doing this? They moved on to people? Did they slit the raccoons’ throats as well?”
Sally replied in a sort of detached, matter-of-fact voice. 
“No, that is new. As is what I found down there behind the cabin.”
The two deputies looked in the direction Sally pointed.
“I don’t see a cabin over there. Are you saying there is another cabin besides Dober’s? Or are you pointing over at his place?”
Sally looked a little perturbed by the question. Why would she be pointing at Dober’s place? 
“No, not Dober’s, right here. There is a cabin located behind the large machine shed. Behind that is another body.”
“Are you kidding me? Another body, this can’t be happening out here. This was such a quiet spot. Nothing like this has happened here before.”
Sally had given up trying to figure out why. All she wanted to do was determine who was doing this and get them in jail.
“Well, it’s happening now. Go ahead, see for yourself. We still need to clear that cabin.I didn’t try to enter it alone. Too much has gone down tonight to try that, I guess.”
“That was good thinking. We can’t afford to lose any more deputies to this maniac. I assume the sheriff will call in the State Police to back us up tomorrow.”
“I doubt the sheriff will allow me to be involved after tonight. I want to find whoever is doing this right now!”
The two deputies just looked at Sally. They could tell she was at her limit.
“Let’s go see the victim and check the cabin. Bernedette and I will take the lead on this, okay?”
Sally just shook her head yes and waved them on to lead. She followed, feeling like each step was a day’s work. She followed the other deputies to the cabin. They walked around the building to the rear. There, the two looked over the wreckage and examined the body of the victim.
“The victim was most certainly in the car when it was burning. I can’t tell if they were driving.”
“That will have to be for the forensics and the coroner to determine, Hank.”
Sally watched the other two check on the wreckage. She was getting anxious to keep things moving. The body count was out of control. They needed to find the killer immediately; they could not change what had already happened.
“We need to clear this cabin and move on with our investigation.”
“Is there just the one door?”
“I think so, and a window on either side.”
“Bernedette and I will go in tandem. You watch the exterior.”
“Got it.”
“Ready, Bernie? One … two … three! Go Go Go.”
The deputies opened the door and moved in sidearms drawn and flashlights pointed in. They checked left and right and circled the interior.
“Clear.”
Sally had watched the windows on entry; there was no movement. She walked in, and the deputies looked at what amounted to old lawn equipment and boxes of stored household items. It was a complete dead end. The trio walked back to their vehicles.
“I’m doing a plate lookup on the wreck. The forensic team should be here in a few minutes. We need an APB put out on Dorian Brendon. He could be armed and dangerous. He is now our prime suspect.”
“I’m on that.”
“Thanks, Bernie.”
Sally went to her vehicle and got in. As she sat there, she took a deep breath. Her legs were aching; it was the first time she had sat down since driving over to the Brendon estate. Her head was hurting, partially due to exhaustion, and partially due to lack of caffeine. She got on her computer and punched in the license number. As she went to hit the last digit, she paused. What if the car was one of the missing Osborn kids? That could be Crystal or Ben in that car. If it was Dorian, she was down one more suspect. Wishing it wasn’t him was equivalent to wishing it was one of the Osborn kids. That was a terrible choice. 
She hit the final digit and waited a few seconds for the registration to come up. Sally had never felt as much trepidation about seeing the owner of a plate before. As the answer popped on the screen, her worst nightmare came into reality and slapped her in the face. The car was registered to Max Osborn. She didn’t know which of his children it was, but she was now certain it was one of them. 
The frustration of failure was weighing heavily on the deputy. So far her investigation had resulted in the death of two deputies, the probable death of one of the kids she was looking for, and the likelihood of her losing her career. Beyond all that, she had no idea where she stood with Barney. Her life was at one of its lowest points, with no end to the downward slide in sight. All she had left was hope. 
Hope is an interesting emotion and cognitive mindset. It exists only when one can suspend reality and logic. It is what’s left when everything else has failed. In her current situation, all she could muster was hope.
She was going to have to call the Osborns about the car and the possible victim. There was, of course, a slim chance that, for some reason, Dorian Brendon was driving the car, but if that were the case, who pulled the wreckage behind the cabin?
Telling a parent their child has been killed in a car wreck is a terrible chore. No parent is prepared for that kind of shock. One minute, you have a special person you have nurtured and watched grow up, and the next, they are snatched away from you without even an opportunity to say goodbye. Sally had done that on two occasions in the past. Each case was heartbreaking. The Osborns were in a special situation. She had very little respect for their commitment to parenting. The father’s lack of knowledge about his own children was disturbing. The fact that the wife was not even aware they were missing was beyond acceptable.
Sally thought for a moment about her judgment of the Osborns. Maybe she was in no position to have an opinion. In some ways, her reaction was jealousy. She would never have the chance to raise a child. She had given that opportunity up when she opted to go into law enforcement. The Osborns had taken a risk she chose not to take. She had to admit that it brought into question who should be judging whom.
The bigger concern was where Dorian Brendon had gone? If he was really planning to “summon” a demon at the Lockwood, when was he going to do it? Was he there now? Finding the Brendon kid had to be their number one priority, but how?
Sally was at a loss as to what her next step should be. She was already out on a limb for not leaving the investigation after the message from the sheriff, which dispatch had passed along to her. Part of her wanted to just go home, climb into bed, and never leave. She was feeling defeated. As she sat there trying to generate enough energy to speak with the other deputies, the weight of what had happened that day began to crash down all around her. Once again, she was struggling to hold back the tears. There was something else. It had been bothering her since she had first entered the woods to get to Dober’s cabin. It wasn’t something she could describe, but it was there, and she could feel it.
Her thoughts were interrupted by the tapping on the window of the car. It startled her. She whipped her head around to see Bernedette standing outside the door. She tilted her head back with her eyes closed to just regroup before opening the door.
“Sorry, did I scare you?”
“No, I was just a little startled. My mind was on our next steps.”
“We have to find the Brendon kid, right?”
“That is what I believe, the problem is where to look. I think we have to go over to the Lockwood."
“Why would we go there?”
 “Dorian Brendon is into the occult. He thinks he can summon a demon if he does it on the roof of the Lockwood. He believes there is paranormal energy in that place that would assist him in bringing forth something called Balsadore.”
“Slow down, this kid is trying to do what?”
“I know it sounds crazy, this whole affair is crazy. That might be my problem. I’ve been trying to investigate it logically. There is no logic to what is happening. We are dealing with all kinds of crazy. I’m worried it is going to make me crazy before it’s over.”
“Just let me fully understand what is going on. The Brendon kid thinks he can summon a demon. He might be doing it at the Lockwood. That place is totally creepy, but why there?”
“I told you, he thinks there is ghost energy there that will aid in his … his … I guess it's called a ritual.”
“The kid’s nuts!”
“If I’m right, he is beyond that; he is our killer. Fred scrawled D O in the dirt beside him before he … “
Sally had to catch herself. She was still reeling from the sight of her mentor, bloodied and mutilated in Dober’s cellar.
“Before he died. He had to be trying to tell us the name of the killer. D O, I thought it was Dober, but it had to be Dorian Brendon. Now with the body in the car hidden behind that cabin, I’m certain he is the killer.”
“That would make sense, but you just said we have to be careful of accepting or expecting the logical conclusion.”
Sally looked at Bernie, somewhat perturbed that she had thrown her own words back at her.
“Well, either way, we need the coroner and forensics here ASAP. I’m not comfortable leaving until I know more about that corpse in the car. We need an identification. I can’t head over to the Lockwood alone; that place is too big. So we wait. If the body in that car is Crystal Osborn, I’m afraid Ben has already met the same fate. There is only one person I fear for right now, and that is Barney over at the Lockwood.”
“Barney? Do you mean Barney Doyle?”
“Yeah.”
“You know Doyle starts with DO.”
“Don’t be ridiculous. Barney wouldn’t hurt a flea.”
“There was that episode when he was a teen that never seemed to be resolved. People talked back then that Barney pushed him.”
“His brother was twelve years old, and he was blind. He knew better. He never should have been out by the road like that.”
He should have been able to hear that semi coming. If he did hear it, then someone had to have pushed him. It wouldn’t be the first time sibling rivalry went too far.”
“You’re making quite a leap from an accident that happened in his teens to being a maniac mutilating his victims today.”
“I suppose I am. It’s just that I don’t understand anyone living out at that creepy sanatorium. If ever a place could make someone loopy, that is the place.”
“You’re just getting carried away.”
“Maybe so, maybe not. I just think we shouldn’t be so quick to rule him out.”
Sally shook her head. Barney couldn’t have anything to do with this horror show. Barney was good. He was good …good. She thought about what Bernie had said, and a trickle of sweat rolled down her spine. There was no way Barney could do such a thing and still look her in the eye and say he cared for her. For a person to do that, they would have to be terribly cold-hearted. The Barney she knew couldn’t do anything like that. 
As she told herself it could not possibly be Barney, way down deep in the pit of her stomach, there was a seed that had just sprouted its first root. It was sinking that root deep into her soul. It was growing quickly, like the way a weed can appear overnight. It was there in her gut, spreading and festering. Why had Bernie planted that weed? It was a cold and harmful thing to do. 
Sally was more anxious for the team to get there and get that body identified. Though she was not a religious person, she began to pray that Barney was safe and had nothing to do with any of the atrocities occurring all around the Lockwood.

        “Don, where did you want to set up the spirit box?”
        “Let’s start at the top of the stairs in the area they call ‘Grand Central’. I think there are three old chairs up there. We can sit in a circle. Just to see if we get any matches, let’s run the ovilus at the same time. Mary, you handle the ovilus. Boo, you run the handheld camera. We need two stationary cameras. One over by Mary so she can show the camera the words on the ovilus, and one by the spirit box. That’s so we are sure we catch any words or phrases we don’t make out while we listen.”
        “Sounds good. Who will be explaining the equipment to the camera for the followers?”
        “Spooky, why don’t you explain the spirit box. Mary, are you okay with explaining the ovilus?”
        “Okay.”
        Mary was still struggling with the whole idea of trying to communicate with the shadow figure. Respectful or not, this thing was malevolent. Anything it said would be either untrue or misleading. In spite of her misgivings, she was there to support the team. That didn’t change the fact that, as a medium, her third eye saw nothing but trouble ahead.
Don was excited to get their attempt to communicate with the shadow he saw on the recordings underway. He led the trio up the stairs and gathered the three chairs while Spooky set up the stationary cameras. Don and Mary positioned themselves and made sure the cameras caught a full view from either angle while Spooky ran down to get the handheld camera.
“Are we all set?”
“Spooky gave a thumbs up, and Mary nodded.”
“Go ahead, Spooky. Let’s start.”
Spooky held the camera away from himself and spoke into the lens.
“Okay, we will be attempting to communicate with the shadow figure we caught on camera earlier.. We have two devices we will be using. This is a bit of an experiment to see if the answers we get on two different pieces of investigative equipment match. Don is using a spirit box. This tool rapidly scans AM/FM radio frequencies to create white noise, allowing spirits to communicate through snippets of sound. If we pick up phrases, that means the spirit is using several frequencies to speak to us. Mary, can you explain the tool you’ll be using?”
“Ahh … Sure, Spooky. This is the ovilus we use to help communicate with spirits by converting environmental changes, like EMF, temperature, and pressure, into audible words or visual text. It has a built-in database of several thousand words or phonemes. So spirits can use the database to respond to our questions or just speak their own mind to tell us what they need us to hear.”
“Okay, I think we are set to start. We will be asking several questions to get the shadow figure or anyone here to communicate with us. Hello, my name is Don. I’m here with my friends Mary and Spooky. We would … ”
Before Don could finish his statement, there was a loud bang from the east hallway, and then what sounded like someone running right at them. Mary screamed, immediately stood up, knocking her chair over, and backed away from the noise. 
        “Whoa, it sounds like we have someone here to speak with. Can you tell us who you are?”
A loud scream came from the spirit box.
“What was that?”
“It sounded like a man screaming.”
“Who are you? What do you want?”
A deep voice came from the spirit box.
“You don’t belong … here.”
“I know what I heard. What did you hear, Spooky?”
“I think I heard: You don’t belong here.”
“That is what I heard, too.”
“What does it mean?”
“It doesn’t sound like it wants us here, or maybe it’s trying to help us by telling us we shouldn’t be here.”
“I agree, Spooky.”
“I don’t think it is trying to help us or warn us. It is telling us why it is upset with us.”
“Mary, you really believe this thing means us harm, don’t you?”
        “Yes, I do. I can feel how powerful this thing is. It can hurt us.”
“That is disconcerting.”
Don looked at the camera for the next statement. 
“For those of you who are long-term followers, you know our process of investigation. We are in this building until 6:00 am. We are not quite to 1:00 am. So we will be here for another five hours. During that time, we will spend about two hours investigating alone. We think the entity that we are communicating with right now has shown itself as a shadow figure. It was following me when we did our solo walk through of the three wings of the building.”
“Why do you believe we don’t belong here? Isn’t this a place of healing? Aren’t the doors open to anyone?”
Mary jumped as the ovilus announced a word in its monotone, electronic voice.
“DEATH.”
“What did that say?”
Mary held the ovilus up to the camera so the word could be seen.
“It said, death!”
“Why did you say death? Whose death are you talking about?”
“You will not ……ldjhfh.”
“I didn’t understand the last word you said. Can you repeat it?
“The ovilus has a word, but no vocal response.”
“What is the word?”
“Trapped, look here is the screen.”
She pointed the small screen in front of the camera. 
“Who is trapped? Are you trapped here?”
As Don asked the questions, three doors in a row slammed shut in the west wing. All three of the investigators jumped, shouted, or screamed. They were all standing now and looking around at each of the hallways. The noise had to have come from the west wing. They stood staring down the hallway. Until Mary interrupted their silence and spoke.
“What the hell was that?”
“I think doors were slamming down the hall!”
“We’ve never had that much activity all at once. Do you realize the amount of energy it would have taken to close that many doors? I think Mary may be right about how strong this entity is.”
“Was that you? Are you the shadow figure? You said we don’t belong here, and then we got the word “trapped”. Are you angry that you are trapped, and we can leave? Is that why we don’t belong here?”
The three of them were all standing, watching all around themselves, waiting for some kind of a response. One minute passed, then another, and so on until nearly an hour went by.
“It was so wild to start with, and now it has gone totally quiet. What happened?”
“I can feel it, they are right here, all around us. They are just watching us.”
“I think we need to change things up. They are more likely to interact if we are alone. Each of us should take a different floor and see if we can communicate with a spirit here.”
“That is not a good idea.”
“Mary, I understand you are concerned about this shadow figure. It has already had chances to harm us and hasn’t. We need the content. We came here to get it, and we are going to do so.”
“Fine, I don’t think anything I say will matter anyway. We are not leaving here.”
Mary was nearly despondent as she spoke. Her premonition was very dark, and she felt they had already crossed the line of no return. There was no escape at this point. Spooky looked at her and felt a shiver go up his spine. He had never seen her speak with such despair.
“I could use a little more positivity here.”
“It will be fine, Spooky. I think Mary is dealing with some issues related to nightmares she has had this week.”
“Maybe she shouldn’t be alone. What do you think, Mary?”
“I’ll be okay. If this is going to happen, I want the chance to communicate directly with them for one time before … ”
Mary’s voice trailed off, and she didn’t finish her statement. She just started walking toward the stairs.
“I’ll take the first floor. You two can decide what you’re doing.”
The way Mary walked away was out of character for her. Don wasn’t particularly upset with it, because the controversy would make for good chatter in the comments. When it came to keeping followers, he knew that any storyline that got people talking was good for the channel.
“She really thinks we are in trouble here, doesn’t she?”
“Yes, she had a nightmare about that shadow figure, and she thinks he, or it, can actually kill us.”
“Are you certain she is wrong? Her psychic vision has been right on a lot of things since we have been together.”
“Have we ever received actual injuries from spirits?”
“I heard what you said earlier. We have had small stuff happen, that’s all, but she has never acted like this before. This time could be different.”
“Okay, I have one teammate acting over the top. I don’t need a second one. I need to count on you, Spooky.”
“Alright, alright, let’s do this thing. Are we doing floors two and three, or is one of us going to the tunnels again?”
“I think the third floor and tunnels.”
“Okay, well, I’ll take the tunnels, I guess, that way I can get to Mary if she is having issues.”
“That sounds good. Stop at the control room and grab the other ovilus. I want us all to try to communicate with whatever is here.”
“Will do.”

        Dorian Brendon had found his way into the Lockwood from the roof. There was a hatch on the roof that opened onto the stairwell at the end of the west wing. That allowed him access to the whole building. He was waiting for the witching hour for his ritual. He had the location where he intended to perform it prepared. He was upset that the building was being used by whoever was with the RV out front. These people were attempting to disturb the paranormal energy that he was counting on to assist in his efforts to bring forth Balsadore.
        He could hear the sounds of someone at the end of the hallway. These people needed to be gone. He had not done all that he had done in preparation to be Balsadore’s hand on earth. He had done what he had to make himself worthy of his demonic lord. If he had to do more to ensure the success of his mission, so be it.

        Mary was sweating as she walked to the first floor. It was as though she was walking her last steps to the gallows. She knew beyond a doubt she was never leaving the Lockwood. She was doomed to become a resident for all time. It was not death that she feared; it was an everlasting haunting trapped under the control of the shadow figure. She was still uncertain what it was, but it was in charge of the building. It had been playing with them for its own entertainment. When it became bored with them, it would dispatch them quickly. It was too much like her nightmare.
        She made it to the end of the west wing hallway on the first floor. That was where she would attempt to speak with one of the human spirits in the building. She hoped the others would keep the shadow figure busy enough that she could get the interaction she hoped to experience at least once before she met her fate.
        She pulled the ovilus from her backpack and began.
        “Hello, my name is Mary. I would like to communicate with anyone willing to talk with me. You needn’t be afraid of me, I mean you no harm. Can you tell me your name or what job you performed here?”
        Mary waited for a response, but there was nothing.
        “Please speak with me. I have spent my adult life investigating the afterlife. I want to know more. You can help. I can feel all of your presence around me. Won’t one of you talk to me?”
        “Danger.”
        “There is danger, you are right. I am aware of it, but I can’t leave. I already know we will find out the doors are all chained and locked. We're prisoners here. The others don’t know that yet.”
        “Run.”
        “What good would running do?”
        “Knife.”
        “Knife? Who has a knife?”
        “He’s coming.”
        “Who is coming? What are you talking about? What is your name?”
        Mary was concerned about the nature of the words she was getting on the ovilus. They seemed so specific. Danger, run, knife, and he’s coming. The words she had gotten from the device in the past were much more random and less cohesive as a train of thought. This sounded like a spirit was warning her about someone coming to harm her with a knife. That did not make sense to her.
        She believed the shadow entity could and would harm them before the night was through, but the paranormal wouldn’t use a knife. It was much more likely they would be pushed on the stairs, or maybe have something fall on them. The force it would take to wield a knife was not in the realm of the paranormal she had studied.
        Suddenly, she heard noises coming from the stairway at the end of the west wing. It was only about thirty feet away from her at that point. It sounded like someone running down the steps. It had to be Don or Spooky Boy. It was too audible to be the remnants of a spirit running toward her. She decided to head to the stairs to see why one of the guys was running in her direction so quickly. Perhaps there had been an incident with one of them.
        Just as she got to the door that opened into the stairwell, it burst open toward her. She was startled, and before she could react, the hooded figure was on her. There was a hand across her mouth stifling the noise, and a knife swung up above her and then came down and across her throat. She could feel the cold steel slice into her deeply. Her body immediately went into shock as she grabbed at her neck, attempting hopelessly to hold back the gush of blood.
        Mary’s legs buckled as her strength waned due to loss of blood. She hit the floor still partially conscious. She watched the hooded figure bend down over her, once again flashing the knife. Her last sight was the blade moving toward her right eye, and then she was gone. The hooded figure continued their gruesome labor, removing both eyes. The figure grabbed Mary’s jacket and wiped the blade of the knife clean. 
        Loud static came over Mary’s walkie-talkie, and then the voice of Spooky Boy Boo.
        “Mary, are you doing okay? Don is a little too carried away with this whole shadow thing. Sorry, he’s not listening. Do you really think we are in danger?”
        The question hung in the air above the lifeless form of Mary the Medium. The hooded figure picked up the walkie-talkie and, in a gruff whisper, responded.
        “Yesssss!”
        Spooky was thrown by the rasping male whisper he received. 
        “Mary, are you there? Are you okay? Who was that on the walkie? Did you hear it? Mary? Mary?”
        There was no answer, and there wouldn’t be, at least not in human form. The hooded figure walked into the darkness of the west wing, humming a children’s song.
        Spooky was not sure what had happened. The voice that responded was not Mary's. It could be that the spirits blocked the transmission and answered without Mary ever getting the original. He had that happen before. 
        He was in the tunnels. That was not the most comfortable place to be. It smelled like mold and mildew. It obviously was dark; the darkness he was experiencing seemed different. It was as though it swallowed the light from his flashlight, and he could barely see right in front of himself. As he walked, he could hear the water splash under his feet. He stopped to look around, but the splashing did not stop. He looked back to see who was following, but nothing was there. He spoke into his camera for the followers.
        “This place is beyond what we have experienced before. I think this is where Don and I turned back before. From this point on, I’m not sure of the direction to go. It looks like there are at least three different directions the tunnel goes up ahead. According to the schematic we looked at before our investigation, the tunnel should go through the basement of the original Lockwood hospital. That is the three-story rock building from which the wings extend.
“It then only goes halfway down the other two wings. The boiler that killed Zuwiki is in the center wing tunnel. That was the doctor who was involved with several married nurses. One of them committed suicide when he would not accept the child. She jumped from the roof of the original stone building in the center of the complex. Two others met the same fate, but it was unclear how they came to fall from the same spot as the first. The doctor could have pushed them. He really sounds like a cruel individual. The description of his death sounds horrific. The steam boiled the skin off his body.  We will keep moving and continue our investigation there.”
He held his flashlight up as he considered which direction he needed to go for the center tunnel. He stood for just a moment, and a figure flashed from one of the tunnels into the next one. Spooky stood stone still. Had he really just seen that? 
“Ha ha ha.”
“Was that a laugh? Who is there?”
Spooky was seriously on edge. He had heard sounds and whispers before, but this was different; it was taunting him.
“Arrghhhhhhhhhhhhhhh!”
Spooky nearly jumped out of his skin. That scream echoed through the tunnels. He really could not tell where it had come from. It could have been behind him or in front of him. It reverberated all around him. He wasn’t sure what to do. He was not ready to walk into whatever had screamed. The problem was that he didn’t know which direction was toward the scream and which was away from it. He had to ask himself, can you truly run from a ghost? 
He decided to move forward, hoping the scream had been meant to scare him out of the place. That would mean it wanted him to run back to where he came from. That is, of course, assuming a ghost could think strategically. 
Spooky was starting to scare himself out of thinking straight. As he moved through the tunnel, he kept looking back repeatedly. Each time he stared into the dark, he thought he could see something. Was it all in his head, or was there something chasing him through these damn tunnels? He was regretting offering to take them. He just wanted to be out of there. 
Spooky was almost running as he tried to make his way out of the tunnels. His obsession with looking back to see if anything was chasing him ultimately caused him to fall. He hit the wet, dirty floor in a full sprawl, dropping his flashlight in the process. 
“Great! Now I’m wet and smelly, my knee is sore, and my hands are gross.”
 The light was several feet away from him as he got back to his feet and started to wipe the muck from his hands on his pant legs. The light shone across the floor in front of him as it lay on the wet concrete. He was looking down as he wiped the persistent gunk off and attempted to straighten himself out. The stream of light was interrupted as though something crossed between him and the light. He jumped and looked up into the blackness of the tunnel.
“Who is there?”
He heard the squishing of the moist floor around him. He could tell he was not alone. He needed his flashlight. Taking a step toward it, he felt a sharp sting behind his right knee. The leg collapsed, and he fell again. The searing pain caused him to grab his knee. It was soaking wet. What was that? He held his hand close to his face and, through the dim reflected light, realized it was blood. He had been sliced across the area where the hamstring attaches to the fibula, severing the popliteal artery in the process. The result was a steady stream of blood gushing from the wound.
Spooky was already lightheaded and reeling from the shock of the attack when the knife whizzed past his ear and sliced his throat.
“Wha …ggghhhhhh.”
Lying in the damp, moldy muck covering the tunnel floor, Spooky’s life ebbed from the lacerations. His vision was dimming as the final injuries were inflicted upon his eyes.  
A hooded figure stood in the darkness with the light from the flashlight casting a glow across the tortured countenance of the now dead Spooky Boy Boo. Seemingly satisfied with their handiwork, they walked on down the tunnel singing a children’s song to the tune of Kookaburra.


“Raccoon sleeps in a hollow tree
While the sun shines on you and me.
Sleep, little raccoon,
Sleep, little raccoon,
Warm and cozily.


In the darkest part of night
Raccoon has the best eyesight.
Look, little raccoon,
Look, little raccoon,
My, your eyes are bright.


Raccoon hardly makes a sound
When he prowls all around.
Hunt, little raccoon,
Hunt, little raccoon,
Find food on the ground.” 


The figure in the hood took their time walking through the tunnel to the basement of the original central building. They walked up the stairs to the first floor. It was just them and Don Musgrave in the building. At least that is what they thought. It was time to rid the building of the last intruder. 
The control room set up by the Posse was located at the bottom of the large staircase that led to the second floor. The hooded figure stepped in to look at the monitors that were recording the stationary camera feeds. They could see Musgrave on the third floor. The investigator was working his way down the east wing hallway. He was checking rooms as he tried to communicate with the spirits.
The figure moved silently and deliberately up to the second floor. They had a task to finish. As they walked the halls of the Lockwood, memories flooded through their mind. A childhood interrupted, unreasonable expectations, and the loss of their most precious loved one. It was time for everything to come full circle. Time for redemption.


*****


“This is Don for Mary.”
Musgrave was standing in the middle of the east wing hallway on the third floor. He was attempting to check in with Mary the Medium. He felt he had steamrolled her earlier. He knew she was afraid for the team, but he had the business aspect of their relationship to consider. Still, he needed to make sure he mended any hard feelings before they created long-term problems. He had no idea that his paranormal skills might be more effective for communicating with his now deceased teammate.
“Mary, please respond.”
No response was forthcoming.
“Come on, Mary, please respond.”
Musgrave looked at the walkie and wondered if it was working correctly. It could be working, but Mary was refusing to respond. She had been very frustrated with him. He decided he needed to try calling Spooky.
“Hey, Spooky, can you hear me? I need to know if the walkie is working.”
Once again, he received no response, and once again, the recipient of his call was no longer among the living. Don spoke into the camera.
“Looks like the walkie is not working. I guess I’m on my own. This place has played some tricks on us already. I haven’t seen the shadow figure yet. I have tried to contact him with the spirit box, but there has been nothing so far. Let’s give it another try.”
Don pulled out the spirit box and started the rapid scanning of radio waves. 
“Is there anyone with me right now? If you want to communicate with me, you can use this device.”
A loud scream-like sound came from the spirit box. Don looked at it in surprise. For a scream to last as long as that one, the spirit had to be using multiple radio station waves.
“What are you screaming? Are you trapped here?”
“Murder.”
“Murder? Who was murdered? Can you tell me when that happened? Can you tell me your name?”
“Now.”
“What is happening now? Are you saying there is a murder now?”
“Get out.”
“Why do you want us to leave? We mean you no harm. We just want to communicate with you.”
“He … is … Coming.”
Musgrave was getting a little nervous. He had never had a session of the spirit box as specific and coherent as the one he was experiencing. He looked into the camera to speak.
“This is amazing. I haven’t had a spirit box session like this before. It seems to be warning me that something bad is about to happen. I’m going to turn the spirit box off and try to get vocal answers.”
        Don shut off the device and set up a couple of REM pods (Radiating Electromagnetic Field Pods). 
“A REM is a tool that detects disturbances in its electromagnetic (EMF) field, signaling potential paranormal activity with lights and sounds. I will place them in the hallway on either side of me. If something comes down the hall from either direction, the REM pod will sound off with loud beeps.”
Don set the second pod up and returned to his spot between them. He pulled out a recorder and began to ask questions. 
“Whoever was answering my questions earlier, I would like to hear your actual voice. Can you tell me if you are here right now?”
Musgrave waited for a response.
After a couple of minutes, Don asked a few more questions.
“The little box I am holding can record your messages. Is there something you want me to know? Help me tell your story. Who are you? Why are you still here? Talk to me, I will be able to hear you on this recording device.”
Musgrave stopped the recorder and rewound the recording to listen for any answers. As he was about to hit the play button, there was a loud bang at the end of the hall. He quickly pointed his flashlight in the direction of the noise. He could not see anything. He stared into the darkness, searching the shadows for signs of movement. There was nothing there. He was about to go back to listening to the recording when the REM went off. 
Don jumped and spun around to look at the device. 
“Are you right here with me? Who are you? Tell your name.”
Don was getting very anxious. He started to think about Mary’s warnings. Maybe she was not delusional about the risks of this place. He looked at the recording device in his hand. He was apprehensive about listening to what might be on it. He had never felt that way before when he investigated. This building was different; it seemed more evil, as though there was something there that didn’t just want to scare him, it wanted blood. He slowly pressed the play button and listened. He first heard his questions
Is there something you want me to know? Help me tell your story. Who are you?
“Zuwiki”
 Why are you still here? Talk to me, I will be able to hear you on this recording device.”
“You can’t leave!” You … are … mine!”
All of the blood drained from Don’s face. He had never heard a voice that oozed so much wickedness and malice. It chilled Musgrave to the core. He felt threatened and very vulnerable. He needed to talk to his team members and get back together, now! 
“Mary, Spooky, Nick somebody, answer the walkie. Damn it, what is going on? It feels like I’m alone and surrounded right now. Is this building blocking the walkie-talkies? I don’t understand what is happening.”
Musgrave was a man used to being under control. He had done so many of these investigations that he rarely felt intimidated by the paranormal. The fact was, he thought much of it was just the overactive imagination of people walking in the dark. He tried to rationalize what was happening.
“Let’s stop and take a deep breath. I’m letting the atmosphere get to me. This is the same place it was when we walked through it earlier in the light. Most shadows are just shadows, most noises are just building creaks or rodents moving around. The loud bangs are caused by stuff falling from the ceilings and walls.”
The little “let’s get back to being rational” speech he was giving to himself was starting to calm him down. He took one more deep breath and shook himself. He turned his camera back on and prepared to continue his investigation. If there were going to be noises, he wanted to capture them for his audience. Content was king.
“Okay, we are just fine. We’ve been hearing noises down the hall, we received some menacing responses on the EVP, and the REM went off just a few minutes ago. We’ve had some very intelligent responses on the spirit box. It seems obvious we are not alone here. I think we should …. HEY, WHAT THE …”
Musgrave was shoved from behind and nearly fell.
“YOU DO NOT HAVE PERMISSION TO TOUCH ME!”
Don was struggling to keep himself from panicking. He had never been pushed like that. He had felt two hands on his back and then a forceful shove. The energy it would take a spirit to do that was beyond what he could imagine.
“That’s it, I’m out of here.”
Musgrave grabbed his equipment except for the stationary camera at the end of the hall and started to walk quickly toward the center of the complex, where he could take the main staircase down to the control center. Just as he started to leave, he looked back and standing in the hall was a hooded figure in what looked like a long robe. 
Musgrave increased his speed to a full run. He wanted out of the place as fast as he could get it done. When he looked back, the figure was no longer there. He kept running. He had about fifty feet to the stairs when he felt a hand on his shoulder and heard whispering.
“Youuuu … don’t ... belong … here!
Musgrave was breathing heavily, and through the puffs of breath, he spoke to whoever was talking to him.
“Okay … okay … I … ahh … I’m … going … just … ahh … let me leave!”
Sweat poured into his eyes even though the building was freezing. He was fumbling with the equipment and finally just dropped it all in an attempt to get to the staircase. He made it there and reached for the handrail when he was stopped dead in his tracks by a huge looming shadow that seemed to just appear in front of him. He jumped in surprise, losing his footing and lunging out over the steps, landing about halfway down and tumbling the rest of the way.
He lay at the bottom of the stairs between the second and third floors. He could not feel or move his legs. He was unaware that his neck was broken. His head throbbed, but his body felt numb. He could hear footsteps coming up the stairs.
“Mary, is that you? Spooky, are you there?”
He was hoping his friends had heard the fall and come to help him. Instead, he saw a hooded figure approach. The face was hidden in the shadows of the hood. 
“Who are you? What are you doing here? Are you human or … ”
His question was cut short by the sweep of a knife across his throat. The figure leaned down toward his face. Don’s eyes widened as if recognizing the person. He could not speak as the blood gushed from his neck, taking his last minutes on earth with it. The look in his eyes changed from recognition to pleading. He still wanted to live.  Those eyes were still begging for mercy as the knife gouged them one at a time, causing a muffled gurgling scream to be the last utterance of Don Musgrave.
