Chapter 9


        The van was cold, and he had been tied up for hours, bouncing around in the vehicle. His head was in so much pain, he could hardly think. He had no idea where he was, and no way to call for help. Ben Osborn felt hopeless and destroyed. Even if he received assistance, what good would it be? He had no eyes to see or tongue to speak. He wanted to cry, but that too had been taken from him. He felt he had been used and thrown out, like so much garbage. He just wanted to die and end the misery.
        When the van door opened, he thought death was indeed the next step. He felt pressure all around him. Not really like hands, but the ropes on his wrists loosened and fell off. He was free, but to do what? He could not see, and he had no frame of reference as to where he was. He could not ask the identity of the individual helping him or why they were doing it. He felt that pressure again, urging him to crawl out of the van. He could feel he was outside. The cold air heightened the pain where his eyes had once been. It seemed like he was being pushed, but he couldn’t feel hands. He walked cautiously one step at a time. He heard the sound of metal rattling. Like corrugated steel was being moved, and it made that fake sound of thunder.
More pressure as he was forced inside a building, and then up the stairs, so many steps. Each one was a stumble and slide as he went without sight. He reached to his sides to find a handrail, which helped as he continued his upward ascent. Finally, he was climbing a ladder to what felt like a hatchway. He could feel the wind and then a whisper.
“You’re on the roof, stand still, I’ll call you when it’s time.”
Ben thought. Time for what? Why was he on a roof? The thought of a roof reminded him of getting caught. That ended so badly; his heart started racing as he thought of how he was tortured and mutilated by the person in the hood. Was that who had brought him up here?


*****


Dorian Brendon had returned to the roof a few minutes earlier. It was now 2:55 am. He was nearing the time he would call forth his lord, Balsadore. He had finished his preparation; he felt the building’s spiritual inhabitants were ready to provide the energy he required to call out the demon. Brendon walked to the pentagram he had created there and placed a ritual rug upon which he knelt. Beside the rug was the bucket he had prepared with the sacrificial blood his lord would require. He dipped his right forefinger into the bucket and used the blood-covered digit to draw a crossed circle on his forehead and two lines with arrowheads on each one, one pointing up and one pointing down. He opened the book and turned to the incantation he had practiced so many times. He then plunged both hands in the bucket. He raised the bloody hands above his head and began his ritual chant.


“From the pit of eternal flames we call thee to rise!
Dark Lord, hear my plea, I beseech thee to answer my call.
Absolutus etcum resurectude eterndid Rex!
Come forth from the shadows and rule the light.


“Vengeance is thy name. Thy power is beyond the mortal world.
Angrious motum greco santorum.
Angrious motum greco santorum.
Take me as your right hand to do your bidding.


Rip the veil of shadow and walk this unworthy plane.
Bring forth your power and vanquish the wicked.
Swallow the world in your dark glory.
Et pra demeanon exulto et provenius!


        Brendon’s words echoed into the frigid night air. The moonless sky winked with stars peeking between the clouds. Other than the boy’s chant, there was a strange silence. In the building below his feet, three bodies lay seeping the last of their blood onto the floors of the building that was once dedicated to healing. 
        Ben could hear the boy that he once considered a friend. He recognized portions of his incantation. He realized he had to be standing on the roof of the Lockwood. If he could get his hands on Dorian, he would choke the guy for what happened to him. It was his fault that he had been out there the night he was assaulted. Dorian had sent him there. But if he was out there,doing the ritual was Crystal with him? What about Dan and Bobbie? They were all supposed to be there.
        As Dorian waited for his chant to take effect, the wind picked up, blowing snow in little swirling mini-tornadoes across the roof. Then Dorian could swear he felt the roof shudder. It had to his dark lord Balsadore. Excitement filled the boy’s body as he was certain his incantation had worked, and he indeed called forth a demonic presence.
        His eyes were focused on the center of the pentagram. He thought to himself, if he had done everything correctly, he would see the demon rise from that spot. A swirl of snow circled the center of the drawing. As Dorian watched, he could swear the snow was gaining speed. Then the snow darkened as it was enveloped in a shadow billowing up from the roof. It grew until it towered over the boy. His heart was pounding as he watched what he was sure was the demon materializing before his eyes. He waited for the beast to acknowledge him as his right hand on earth, but there was no sound. When the shadow had finished its rise, it hovered over Dorian as if inspecting the slim little figure before it. 
When it appeared, there was no communication coming forth from the demon; Dorian spoke.
“Dark lord, I called you forth. I offer myself as your right hand on earth. Please allow me to serve you. There is much to do here. I have enemies over whom we must prevail.”
The shadow hovered, offering no response, but it closed the space between itself and Dorian and then vanished. Dorian stood confused by what had just happened. Had he failed? Was Balsadore invisible? Did he have the power of his demonic lord? How would he know? Maybe he should test his powers. How could he do that? He went back to the book, hoping he had missed something about the type of power the hand of Balsadore would wield. He couldn’t believe that, after all the time he had spent in preparation, he had not thought about what he would be able to do once he achieved his goal. 
Dorian’s mind was so cluttered with his confusion that he was oblivious to what was happening around him.
Ben was still standing by the hatch. He was afraid to move for fear he would step off the roof. It was not so much that he feared dying; it was more an issue of wanting to exact some form of revenge on Dorian. Ben felt his life was over, but he did not want to let Dorian off the hook for putting him in this situation. And then the whisperer was back.
“Come with me, I will take you to him.”
Ben wondered who the whisperer was referring to. Who were they taking him to? Was he talking about Dorian? Or had Dorian been successful in his demon summons, and was he going to Balsadore? If it were the latter, then he was certainly dead. Dorian must have assumed he was no longer a friend. Ben knew quite well that Dorian wanted revenge on everyone he felt had wronged him, no matter how minor the transgression might be. Balsadore was his super weapon, to be used to destroy those whom Dorian singled out.
Ben went where the whisperer directed. He walked slowly, assuming at any moment he would be falling to his death. He realized how foolish his caution was. In a way, falling would be okay. His life felt like it was already over. It would end the pain; he needed that searing pain to stop. His acceptance that death would be a blessing gave him the courage to walk at full stride 


*****
It was 3:15 am. Sally was standing beside the wrecked vehicle behind the cabin at the Brendons’ home. She was numb from the cold and the exhaustion of the day. She needed to know who was in that car. The coroner and the forensic team had taken nearly an hour and a half to extract the corpse from the crumpled, burned vehicle.
They had spent another half hour examining the body. Sally figured they had been at it long enough to give her some answers.
“You guys have been at this for two hours. What can you tell me about the victim?”
“You know I will issue a full report after I have a chance to do a full examination at my office.”
“Listen, Doc, I have three kids missing out here. I need to know if we just found one of them. If we did, that means there is at least one other kid in danger and maybe one that is running off the rails, so I need to know what you can tell me now.”
“Well, I can’t tell you much, but based on the pelvic configuration, I’m certain the driver was a female.”
“A female, can you give me an age range?”
“Again, I will know more once I get her back to my office.”
“Give me your opinion, based on what you have seen so far.”
“She looks young, I’d say under twenty.”
Sally kicked the snow as she turned from her conversation with the coroner. That pretty much meant she was looking at Crystal Osborn. She turned to Tom Clark.
“Tom, are we sure she was driving when the vehicle wrecked?”
“It looks like it. I can’t be totally certain until we do a full breakdown of the information, but my initial thought is that she was in the car alone.”
“Does it appear there was anything done to the car, the brakes, or the steering?”
“No, it looks like the car was in good shape, but that is an opinion. All I can say is that there is nothing suspicious that is easily visible tonight. You have to understand there is only so much we can do, given the circumstances. In the light of day, we should have a better idea of what exactly happened. At first blush, it looks like she ran into the tree and then caught fire.”
“If it wasn’t due to tampering, why did the car run off the driveway into the tree?”
Sally had a thought as she asked her last question. What if it wasn’t the car that had been tampered with? She recalled the missing drugs from the veterinarian’s van. She needed to talk to the coroner again. She headed up to where he had parked his van. 
“Before you leave, I have another question.”
“Okay, I am ready to take the body back to the office. What do you need?”
“Is it possible the victim had been drugged?”
“I would not be able to tell you that until I have the chance to do some tests … ”
“Let me guess, back at your office.”
“Deputy, I understand you’re being anxious for information. My work is a science. I need to have the time to truly test everything, so that what I tell you is accurate and not a guess.”
“Okay, okay, Doc. I’ll let you go so you can get the facts.”
“As soon as I know anything I think will be helpful, I will call you.”
“Okay, thanks.”
Sally needed to go to the Lockwood. That was where she believed Dorian Brendon was going to attempt to summon a demon. As crazy as that sounded, the kid believed he could do it on the roof of the old sanatorium. She realized she was not just tired. She was physically and mentally exhausted. So much had happened that day, and she was now nearing twenty-four hours on the job. She did not dare go to the Lockwood alone. Her thinking speed was off, her emotions were on edge, and her frustration and anxiety had peaked hours before. She was uncertain how she would react when she finally confronted the perpetrator. 
Perpetrator, the word lingered in her mind. She realized it was a singular assumption. She had conflated the murders and the kidnapping. What if two unrelated persons performed two different criminal acts? That seemed far-fetched. The coincidence element was always suspect in her line of work. Why would two separate acts be taking place around the Lockwood? It was more likely that the acts were related in some manner. It had to be Dorian Brendon. Fred had scrawled the D O in the dirt. Crystal Osborn was found dead in a wrecked car that had been dragged behind the cabin at the Brendon house. The kid was loony enough to think he could summon a demon to vanquish his enemies; it all added up. 
One thing that continued to gnaw at her was the raccoons and then the people. Why was he killing and gouging out eyes? That didn’t fit the whole scheme. How would killing random deputies at Dober Grumby’s place be related to this whole demon summoning thing? She then realized applying logic to a crazed killer was not a worthwhile endeavor. At that moment, Tom Clark’s voice seemed to be calling her from somewhere in the distance.
“Earth to Sally. Are you awake or asleep with your eyes open?”
“What? Huh, what do you mean?”
“I’ve been talking to you, but you’ve been somewhere else. You haven’t heard a word I said, have you?”
“Ahh … no, I guess my mind was on other things. What were you saying?”
“I should have told you earlier, the blood actually did show human traits.”
“What blood?”
“The blood around the table at Dober Grumby’s place. The first tests came up negative, but we went back and pulled samples from other stains on and under the table. We found human blood.”
Sally’s brain was too tired to do the mental gymnastics of piecing that information into the puzzle she had before her.”
“You’re going to have to help out here. What does that mean?”
“Well, I suppose it means you can assume whoever was torturing raccoons had moved up the food chain and started to do the same to humans. Now, before you get back on the Dober train. His blood was one of the samples.”
Sally gave Tom a sort of blank look. She was so drained by the investigation and all the deaths that she was rendered nearly unresponsive. Tom figured she needed a little help thinking through the information.
“So, Dober was not the killer; he was one of the victims. Whoever did this killed Dober and used his cabin as their own little torture chamber. There was something else that was strange.”
“Now you think something is strange? This whole thing has been strange from the beginning.”
“I guess that is true. But when we really got looking at the cabin interior, we noticed what seemed like a scarecrow figure seated by the fireplace.”
“A scarecrow?”
“Well, maybe more like an effigy. It was dressed in jeans and what looked like what once was a lab coat. It was old and worn, pretty dirty too. It could have been a shop coat of some kind.”
“So what does that mean?”
“That one is for you to figure out. It seemed to be set up to be the audience for whoever was doing the torture and mutilation.”
“Is there anything else? I’m not confused enough; there must be something more, like you found signs of aliens.”
“Good to see you haven’t lost your sarcasm in all of this mess.”
“It’s probably my last day on the force, so I might as well go out swinging.”
“Hang in there. I don’t think I have ever known a law enforcement officer more committed to finding the truth in a case than you.”
Sally gave Tom a tired, sad smile as he walked away. He was kind, but she knew she had stumbled quite badly on this case. It was time to finish it, and in her heart she knew that finish was at the Lockwood. She needed another deputy to go with her. She was standing beside her vehicle, looking around the scene at the Brendon estate. There were now three deputy teams, the forensic team, and the coroner, who was now leaving. Most of these people were going to be staying until the sunlight to finish the investigation. There had to be one of these deputies she could talk into leaving with her.


*****
        Standing on the third floor, looking down from the top of “Grand Central”, a blood-covered, hooded figure was consumed by memories he had blocked for so many years. 
        “Go ahead, get in the car. We will be taking you to our house. We have a room that you will be staying in while you are with us. How long that is going to be is up to you. We won’t be adopting you unless it looks like it will work out. I tell you all of this so you understand we will always be honest with you. What we expect is obedience and your best and hardest work. Now, if you understand, we will go to our house.”
        “I understand. What do I call you?”
        “For now, just call us Mr. John and Ms. Wendy. We will be calling you Irish. You have not yet earned the right to be addressed by your real name.”
        The young boy got into the car. He was not sure what Mr. John had in mind for him to earn the right to be called by his real name. This was different than the other foster homes he had been in. They usually talked all nice and friendly at first. Then they ignored him for the most part. They wanted him more for the money they were paid to care for him. He was more of a burden than a family member. These people seemed to be throwing out a business deal rather than the offer of a permanent family. 
        As he rode to the home of the couple in the front seat, he considered his situation. He had been in five foster homes since he lost the last of his family. He was what the system called eligible. There was no father of record, and his mother had died of a drug overdose after abandoning him. He was alone. Though he was considered available to adopt, he had floated through five foster homes since he was seven. Those had ranged from nice, but unprepared to deal with him to abusive and in the foster program for the money. None had provided a home; at best, they had been room and board.
        This new couple made no pretense at offering love or warmth. They were cold and had expectations. If he met those expectations, they may consider a long-term arrangement. Luckily, the boy was gifted with an above-average intellect. Between that and the amount of experience he had on his own, he knew how to play the game. He was tired of bouncing around. He needed a place he could call home, no matter how flawed it may be. He wanted to get an education and get on with his life. 
        If that meant pretending to dance to their tune, he could do that as long as the tune was not too far off tempo. Since he knew how to play the game, he also knew how to sabotage it. He could act out in ways that made it impossible for a couple of the families to keep him. He had the will to outlast threats and punishment to get what he wanted. At ten, he was a practiced manipulator, skilled in the politics of power. 
For all of his hard-fought worldliness in human dynamics, he lacked the ability to feel empathy or true love. Perhaps he was an excellent fit for the frigid couple with whom he was now paired. Time would tell. 
He watched out the window as they drove through the southeast side of Eau Claire and out Hwy 12. They passed the Gemini Drive-in theater. “Raiders of the Lost Ark” was playing. Some of the kids he had met talked about seeing that new movie. He had never been taken to a movie theater or a Drive-In. 
“Is there any chance we could see that movie? I’ve never been to one.”
That was when he first noticed Mr. John’s eyes. In response to his question, the man had stopped the car and spun his head around to stare directly at him. His eyes were black and soulless. His gaze was filled with contempt and judgment. He would learn to hate those eyes. After leveling that horrid stare, Mr. John spoke.
“We don’t waste our time on frivolous entertainment. A person is better served reading or working. Improving one's mind and body is far more important than filling their head with fantasies like that.”
The boy looked at Mr. John. The man’s face was filled with elitist pride in his criticism of the value of movies. The eyes closed just enough to appear to be looking down at the boy, signaling his superiority. There was no need to attempt to question Mr. John’s assessment of the form of entertainment. The boy could tell it was a point of pride, and his opinion was not going to change.
Shortly after passing the Gemini, they turned onto Markgraff Road. Two miles down that road, they entered the driveway of a farmette. This would be the boy’s home until further notice. He crawled out of the car and looked around. There was a small barn and two other outbuildings, a chicken coop, and a pig barn. The place was run down, but tidy. 
“Grab your bag and follow me.”
The boy followed Mr. John into the house. It smelled of boiled cabbage and cigarette smoke. Neither of which was very pleasant to the boy. He followed his foster “parent” up a narrow staircase to what had been the attic of the house. There was no true second floor. The space had been enclosed with plywood and painted white. There was a braided rug that covered the rough boards of the attic floor in front of the single bed, which was covered with an old quilt. There was no closet, but there were pegs along the wall opposite the bed and a small chest of drawers. 
“This will be your room. You are responsible for keeping it looking like it does right now. There will be no nailing up pictures, no drawing on the walls or floor. There will be no food allowed up here; we have enough trouble with mice; we don’t need to be inviting them around the house. The bedclothes are to be stripped and brought downstairs every Saturday morning for washing. Your clothes will be washed twice per week, so keep them from getting dirty. Unpack and meet me in front of the house. I will teach you your daily chores outside.”
Mr. John exited the attic, and the boy sat on the bed for a minute just to take in all that he had seen and heard so far. He was in a very regimented home. Was that going to work for him? He had to think about it; he didn’t have time at that moment to do that since he was expected outside. He quickly unpacked his few belongings, which fit into two of the drawers of the chest. He then went downstairs. Ms. Wendy was in the kitchen as he walked past. She did not speak to him. Outside, Mr. John was waiting. His eyes squinted at the boy as he spoke.                                                 
        “That took too long. You are going to learn that when I say I will be waiting for you, I expect you to move at lightning speed.”
        “Sorry, I was just looking at the room to make sure I had put everything away correctly without disturbing anything.”
        “I don’t want excuses. Excuses are for the weak. Learn to do things right the first time so you don’t waste time rechecking your work.”
“Yes, sir, I’ll try.”
Mr. John’s eyes glared with disdain for the boy.
“NO! You won’t try, you will do it right the first time!”
The boy was a little shocked by the forcefulness of the man. He wondered just how often Mr. John did not do things right the first time. He was sure, by the looks of the farmette, there were plenty of mistakes made running the place. He chose to bide his time before saying anything that might rile the guy. He already hated the man. He had plenty of cause, but the eyes were enough of a reason.
“Follow me. You will be responsible for feeding the chickens and the hogs twice a day. I will show where the feed is and the measurements for each.”
It took about an hour for the two of them to get all of the chores completed. When that was done they entered the house.
“Take your shoes back outside and wipe them in the grass. Once that is done, use the rags from the bucket beside the front door. Once the shoes are clean, remove them. We do not track barnyard muck into this home. Is all that I’ve explained to you completely clear?”
“I think so. …”
The boy stopped and immediately changed his answer before Mr. John could respond.
“I mean, yes, I understand.”
“Good”
“Ms. Wendy should have our dinner ready in about fifteen minutes. Come with me, I will show you where the bathroom is. You will wash your hands before every meal. You will then report to her in the kitchen to set the table for our meal.”
The orders and rules were endless from that day in 1981 until the boy was ready to leave for college.
Life was not enjoyable at their home. There was never a relationship one could characterize as a family. They were an efficient unit. If the boy was ever deemed out of line, he knew the consequences would be harsh. Mr. John kept a broken rein from a bridle hanging in the porch entryway. The boy felt that piece of leather across his lower buttocks at least once a week. His transgressions could range anywhere from dropping a glass while setting the table to forgetting to change the water in the chicken coop. There was never room for a mistake. Each one, no matter how small, was dealt with in the same manner.
The boy was never allowed ownership of any type. Everything, including the clothes on his back, was the property of Mr. John. He made that clear regularly. The boy would have no belongings of his own until he could pay for them with money he earned from a job. That became painfully clear when Irish started feeding a stray cat. When he was twelve, he had discovered a stray cat behind the pig barn. It was sick and had obviously been in a fight. Its left eye was missing, and it limped on its front right paw. 
“Hey there, little guy. You look like you’ve been to war. Are you hungry? You wait here, I’ll bring you some food, okay?”
The cat was not certain it could trust Irish. He was not willing to allow him to pet it, but the soft words seemed like he was not going to harm him. As Irish ate breakfast, he snuck some of his eggs and sausage into a napkin in his pocket. Before getting on the bus, he ran out and fed his new friend. The cat walked toward the food tentatively. When it felt comfortable it was meant for him, he gobbled it down.
This went on for a week, and by the end of the seven days, Calico was Irish’s best friend. He would rub on the boy's leg the minute he arrived and allowed Irish to pet him while he ate. They became good friends and Irish was able to keep Calico a secret for nearly a month. Then one day, Mr. John got suspicious and followed Irish out after breakfast. When he spotted the boy feeding the cat a portion of his breakfast, he was enraged. 
“What do you think you are doing? You are throwing good food, my food, away on a ragged, mangy cat. I will not have my food wasted in such a manner. It is obvious we have been too good to you. Your meal portions shall be cut in half until you can prove yourself worthy of more. As for this piece of refuse, I will dispose of it.”
“Don’t touch Calico, he is mine.”
“Yours? You don’t own anything, especially this furball. He has been eating MY food. If he belongs to anyone, it is me. I decide what happens to my property! I can dispose of my property any way I choose.”
Mr. John trapped Calico in the corner of the barn. He snatched the cat up by the loose skin on the back of its neck and, with his bare hands, grabbed its head and twisted it until the cat's body went limp. He threw the animal at the boy's feet. Calico lay there with its eyes open, giving Irish a gruesome, dead stare. 
Tears pooled in the boy’s eyes, but he showed no signs of crying. Mr. John manhandled the boy and marched him to the porch, where the leather strap was stored. Twenty-two swipes of the old piece of horse rein whipped through the air, landing with a snap on the boy's backside. Irish refused to cry or give any sign of remorse. When it was done, he stood straight up, revealing none of the pain he was feeling, and stared at Mr. John with hate and disdain. 
By the time he was a freshman in high school, the punishments expanded to include any underachievement of grade expectations. These were rare, as the boy was extraordinarily bright. When they happened, they went into his memory as inspiration for the day he could pay these people back for the abuse he had endured over the years. The relationship between him and the foster/adoptive parents was never remotely loving. At best, there were days of peaceful coexistence.
The years rolled on, and the expectations grew. The day came when he graduated from high school. Mr. John sat him down to explain what his future would hold. Those eyes framed by furrowed caterpillar brows stared at him again. Those black, lifeless, cold eyes betrayed the greed he was about to unleash on the boy he called Irish.  
“You will attend college and get a degree in engineering. That way, you can get a good job. You will provide Ms. Wendy and me with a monthly payment for raising you.”
 Irish sat looking at his “father” in disbelief. He had just learned that this man felt he was entitled to own him for life. Even now, after all he had done, Mr. John was still not granting him the decency to call him by name and view him as a full-fledged human being. What had he done to deserve a portion of his income? 
Irish had worked for this man for eight years, ultimately doing all of the outdoor work. He had earned them money as essentially their slave for the majority of the time. Irish had more than paid his way. They weren’t even funding his college; that was all being paid through scholarships and grants Irish had earned. What right did he have to tell him what degree he would pursue in school?
Irish did not speak. He would bide his time. He had the summer to solve the problem. During that summer, Irish’s foster parents did something surprising. They brought a new boy home to stay with them. Irish wanted to warn the twelve-year-old about the type of home he was entering, but it seemed the boy had been knocked around for some time. And was just happy for the structure.
The kid was interesting; he wore glasses that were as thick as the bottom of glass soda bottles. Irish never had the chance to get to know him very well, but that was another story. When the summer ended, Irish went off to college, but not before sealing the fate of his foster parents. He would once again bide his time and allow the little surprise he had planned to take place at its own pace.
Memories are a strange thing. They can come in glimpses or flood one’s mind in full detail. The figure, still standing at the top of “Grand Central”, had relived those eight years in a few seconds. He felt every emotion, every pain, as if it were fresh and still raw. A person cannot run from all the events that formed them. Those things are all still there waiting to be revisited. They will not be buried. Even if you choose to ignore them, they will visit you, force their way into your very being. They will drive your actions, make you resolve issues you wanted to forget. 
 
*****
Barney Doyle was confused. A state in which he seemed to be finding himself far too often. He was standing in the road that ran from the gate to the sanatorium. He had to have been standing there for some time; his body was frozen. He could not feel his feet or his hands because of the cold. He attempted to walk, but he was stiff from the cold and apparently standing in one place for too long. He hobbled to his apartment at the end of the east wing. Once inside, he stood by the heat vent to thaw out. 
As he stood, he tried to recreate the last ten hours. How did he end up standing out there like that? The last thing he recalled was leaving the Lockwood after checking the building one last time before the Paranormal Posse took over and started their investigation. That was shortly before 6:00 pm the evening before. It was 3:00 am. Had he been standing out there ever since? 
He had warmed up enough to walk around his apartment. He went into his 
bathroom to wash his face and hands. His stomach was letting him know he had not eaten in quite a while. He looked at himself in the mirror and was shocked to see blood on the front of the sweatshirt he was wearing. Whose blood was it? He pulled it over his head and checked himself for wounds. Finding none, he became even more concerned. How had the blood gotten on him? What had he done? 
The whole situation produced flashbacks to the day his wife left him. He was never really certain he hadn’t hurt his ex-wife. That fear was now at the front of mind. He had been losing time recently, and each time he had experienced something that was chipping away at his sanity. He couldn’t believe it was happening again. He had experienced this before. Back when his wife left him, he had no idea what had happened to her. She was there, and then when she decided to leave him, she just disappeared. Her family thought he had done something to her, but he had no memory of harming her. There was no evidence that he had done anything wrong, just like when his brother was hit by the semi.  When she walked out on him, it hit him very hard. He started to drink and then take pain medications.
The self-medication was not a good recipe for solving any of his issues.  For a while, he was checked into a psychiatric facility to get help. It had taken close to a year for him to get straightened out. He carried the memory of that time every day. The professionals had determined it was a nervous breakdown. He was suffering from Dissociative Post-Traumatic Stress Disorders. The therapy that followed his rehab helped him accept the loss, but he never really got over it. The loneliness lingered and reminded him daily that he had been rejected again. As he had been by so many in his life. 
Death and rejection were the milestones of Barney’s life. Family members died in unimaginable ways, leaving him alone. Pets he dearly loved were taken from him and destroyed. It seemed every attempt at friendship was thwarted by circumstances that made getting close impossible. When he felt he had found that one person with whom he could build a life, they pulled away like everyone else and left him more alone than ever. He felt doomed to wallow in the mire of solitude until he, too, left the mortal plain. In the Lockwood, he had found acceptance. It wanted him. He felt destined to become one of its army of wandering souls.     
As Barney stood there reminiscing, he realized he was feeling sorry for himself. If he had hurt someone, he hardly deserved self-pity. He needed to find out what he had done. If he couldn’t remember, then he needed to go see it. He threw on his outdoor clothes and headed back outside to look around. He would check around the building and then inside. 
The snow crunched under his feet as he walked. The darkness of the moonless night created a black cocoon that surrounded him as he walked with his flashlight, his only means of seeing. He was walking very slowly, fearing what he was about to discover. The blood had come from somewhere, or rather someone. The amount of blood seemed to hint that he was not going to find someone injured. They were more likely beyond assistance. 
The Paranormal Posse’s RV was dark up ahead. He thought that was strange because Nick should be inside, and as Barney understood it, would be staying up all night in support of the trio inside. That should mean the lights would be on. As he neared the large vehicle, his flashlight fell upon what looked like a pile of clothes on the ground by the door of the RV. Standing over the pile, he realized it was a person. He reached down to shake them.
 “Hey, what is going on? Did you fall? Are you alright?”
There was no answer, and as Barney rolled the body over, he was presented with a ghastly sight. Beneath the body of Nick was a huge puddle of freezing blood. The front of Nick's clothes was soaked with blood. The true horror was the frozen scream of the man’s face, which was covered in blood and bore gaping holes where the eyes had been.
“Oh my god!”
Barney fell back into the snow and, in a swift scurry, crab walked away from the body. Tears filled his eyes as he wondered if he had done such a terrible thing. The image of the raccoon from a few weeks earlier filled his brain. It was the same eyes, the black, bloody eyes, gouged from the head. He stared at the gruesome image and searched his memory. Surely if he had done such a thing, there would be a memory of it somewhere in his mind. He didn’t know exactly what to do. He shouldn’t be around people if he had the capacity to do something like that. 
How could he stop himself if he didn’t know when he was doing these things? He needed a group of people around him, if for no other reason than to keep him from doing more harm. The Paranormal Posse was inside. He should get them to help. There were three of them. That should make it safe.  
He quickly went to the front door, only to find it chained shut and locked. He recognized the lock, but did not have the key. He had left the key in the lock where he stored it in the utility building behind the Lockwood. He needed to get to the secret entrance on the west end. That was an old door that had rotted away, so he covered it with corrugated metal. 
The snow was deeper around that side of the building. It faced the west and caught the full brunt of the wind as the drifts piled up. He trudged through the fluffy white dunes. The walking was slowed by the frigid piles. When he rounded the corner, he wondered where the tire tracks had come from. There shouldn’t be any vehicles back there. He held his light up to look further ahead and caught the glint of a reflector on the back of a van. 
“What the heck is that doing here? The back door is open.”
He walked up and opened it all the way. The floor of the vehicle was bloody. It had racks on either side filled with equipment and vials of what appeared to be medicine. He leaned back, closing the door so he could see the side of the van. It was Doc Blanchard’s veterinarian van. 
Barney’s mind exploded with questions. What is this doing here? Is Doc somewhere inside? Did someone steal it and drive it here? Whose blood is on the floor? Was Nick somehow mutilated here and then dropped outside the RV? Why would someone do that? What advantage would that be? Or, has someone else lost blood here? How many people have been killed? This was getting way out of hand. He needed to find Musgrave and the others and then call the police.
Barney walked over to the corrugated metal blocking the entrance. The fact that there was a set of footprints going into the building through that doorway already really bothered him. Had he done that? The snow obscured the prints enough to make it impossible to judge that, based on the size of the track. There was nothing to do but go in and see what was going on.
The metal made that thunder sound as he moved it. Walking into the lower level of the Lockwood was like walking into an electric storm. The energy was swirling all around Barney. He felt like he was being embraced by the building itself. The pressure all around him was nearly suffocating. He pushed on into the stairwell and up to the first floor.
“Hello, Mr. Musgrave, are you on this floor?”
There was no answer. Barney knew that sound didn’t carry well through the building. The space between floors had been insulated when they made a portion of it into an insane asylum. That kept the noise down, and should one of these patients scream, it did not disturb the other residents.
 Barney continued up the stairs to the second floor. He stepped into the hallway and aimed his light down the hall.
“Hello, Mr. Musgrave, are you on this floor? It’s me, Barney Doyle.”
There was still no answer. This time, Barney opted to walk down the hallway to “Grand Central”. If he was going to get any vocal advantage, it was in the large central area. As he walked, he had the feeling he was not alone. He could feel entities all around him. It was like the hallway was crowded with the souls of the departed. Out of the corners of his eyes, he caught glimpses of them. Nurses and patients went about their routines as they had repeatedly since they had passed on from their mortal lives. They were stuck in a constant rewind of their last days at the Lockwood. Barney was close to them, but not part of them. At least, not yet.
At the end of the hallway, there seemed to be something lying on the floor at the bottom of the stairs that led to the third floor. He crept closer, fearing the lump was another victim. Standing over the form, he recognized it as Don Musgrave. It looked like he had fallen down the stairs. He hoped he was only dazed from the fall.
“D … Don? Mr. Musgrave, are you okay? Have you been … ”
His words were cut short as he rolled him over; it was apparent he was dead. He was in much the same condition as Nick had been. 
“Oh no! Not you as well. How, why … what’s happening?”
Barney dropped to his knees. This was all too much. His life had been sad, full of disappointment and hurtful actions by others, but nothing he had experienced warranted doing something like this. If he was doing these things, that darkness he carried had to be responsible. It had to be much stronger than he realized. It would have to be. He could never do this to a person. 
Whoever was doing this had a specific manner in which they were killing. The throat was slit, and the eyes gouged out. The killer was especially cruel in their manner of dispatching their victims. 
Barney searched his mind for a reason he would do such a sick thing. He could not think straight at this point. He was ready to join the ranks of the marooned souls trapped at the Lockwood, but that was the easy way out. He had to face the penalty for his actions. The muddle that was growing in his brain forced him to stop and take a breath. He had to hold it together. He needed to find the other two and explain the situation to them, if he could do that. 
He walked down to the first floor. Entering the Posse’s control room he looked at the monitors. The stationary cameras were pointed down the three floors above ground, but there were no feeds from the tunnels below. He could not see any movement on the camera feeds from the hallways above or the first floor. That seemed odd. Why was Don so far away from the other two? Did they wander around individually the whole time they were investigating a location? That seemed unsafe. Then he considered that thought and how ridiculous it was since half of the people on Don’s team were already dead. 
Barney made his way down the steps to the main building's basement. From there, he could enter the tunnels. He never liked the casement of the central building. It was the oldest part of the whole complex. It was where the darkest of the spirits hung out. It was as though they were more comfortable in the creepiest place in the building.
Barney could hear the whispers of the building’s inhabitants. They seemed to be extremely agitated by the events of the evening. For a fleeting moment, Barney considered whether they might have killed his partners. He dismissed the thought as he assumed he was just hoping there was someone other than himself to blame. He walked on. He wanted to call out, but forgot the names of the other two members of the Paranormal Posse. 
“Once again, I have forgotten. Did I hear their names? I must have. My mind is so muddled I’m not sure what I know and don’t know. I’m a mess, I guess I always have been. Just a broken individual with a broken mind. My grandmother would be so disappointed. She was the only person, other than maybe Sally, who ever really cared about me. I wish Grammy were here now, singing her nursery rhymes and rubbing my head. I lost her when I was so young. From that point on, I guess I have been on my own. My life is messed up because I messed it up. I can try to blame it all on the way people have treated me my whole life, but it is how one reacts to what life gives them that is important. I haven’t carried myself above that treatment. I have stuffed the pain of it into the baggage I carry every day.”
Barney made his way into the tunnel and tripped over something. He was afraid to look down. He paused for a moment to collect himself and aimed the light at his feet. Lying there was the guy who was part of Don’s troop. Boo, something. When he bent down to see him more easily, he was met with the same horrific mutilation of throat and eyes. This one was not done as cleanly; one of the eyes was still dangling from its socket.  
Barney backed up and placed a hand on the wall to steady himself as his stomach churned, and then he vomited. His mind was blank as if he had been electrically shocked to the point of fritzing out. He shook his head and tried to reconnect to what was actually happening all around him. He was there alone with the four people of the Posse team. Who else could have done the terrible things? It had to be his fault. There was one more member of the Posse, and he feared what he would find when he came across her.  
“Mary! That was the woman’s name. I don’t know why that just popped into my head. At least I can call her name. Please god let her answer. I can’t handle another death tonight.”
Barney left the tunnels. It made sense that Mary was either on the first or third floors, based on the location of the other two. It looked like they had decided to investigate floors as individuals. 
On the first floor, Barney walked down the east wing hallway and found nothing. When he made it back to ”Grand Central”, he had to decide if he should go down the center hallway or the west wing hall. He chose the center hallway. It took ten minutes to go to the end and back. There was no sign of her. Barney stood in the middle of “Grand Central” and yelled.
“Mary, are you down there? Mary, please answer. I can tell you some of what you don’t know about this place. Just answer me.”
The thought crossed his mind that she was afraid to answer. Maybe she had seen that both of her colleagues were dead. She wanted to wait that long? At what point might the spirits begin to play tricks on her? Perhaps they wouldn’t do that because they were not looking to have her stay there the way they wanted him to remain. He decided he needed to walk down to the end of the west wing hallway. It was silly to assume she was hiding. Maybe she had headphones on and couldn’t hear him calling.
“I don’t want to startle you. I am walking down the west wing hallway. If you are there, please be aware that I am coming.”
It took about five minutes to work his way down the hall. He had to check every room as he walked past them. That took time. He was nearing the far end of the hall when a noise caught his attention. It came from back at “Grand Central”. He looked back to try to see what had made the bang. There in the center of the hallway stood the shadow figure.
“Who are you? What do you want from me? Did you do these things?”
There was no answer, but the figure moved out of the center of the hallway into the shadows, effectively disappearing.
“Where did you go? Are you trying to scare me? It is too late for that. I don’t care about my life. I just want to save Mary if I can. Leave me alone. I’ll be joining this circus soon enough.”
Barney stopped talking and stared down the hallway. There was no movement and only silence. He opted to continue his search for Mary. He was nearing the end of the west wing hallway when his light caught the form of a lump on the floor.
“Oh no, no, no! Not Mary. That poor woman. She can’t … no one would be that cruel. She can’t be … ”
Barney knelt and rolled her toward himself so he could get a good look at her face. 
“Why is this happening? I couldn’t, I wouldn’t … but I don’t know what I did. I was standing outside. I have no idea what I was doing for so many hours. I had the time to do this, and I had blood on my sweatshirt. I can’t understand why or how I could have done this, but I think I must have. Oh god, how could I, how could I … ”
Barney dissolved into tears. His life was over; he knew that. But why would he do these things? He had no reason … Then it hit him. The building did not want to be exploited. He had felt the Posse was going to stir up so much traffic. It was his job to protect the Lockwood. Was that what he thought he was doing? Did he even think about it, or was it just a reaction? If all he was doing was protecting the building, he might have killed them without realizing it, but why mutilate them? Why had the raccoon been subjected to the same treatment before them? He hadn’t known the Posse was coming when that happened. 
“Maybe the raccoon had been mutilated by someone else, and that somehow that was in my head when I killed the Posse members. That’s possible. In my therapy, they talked about re-enactment. That was when a cruel treatment an individual has experienced becomes part of their treatment of others. Was that what was going on in my screwed-up head?” 
Barney didn’t realize he was talking out loud until he heard the laughing.
“Ha ha ha ha.”
“Who is laughing? Is that the shadow? What is so funny? You think death is funny, don’t you? I’ll show you funny, I’ll show you!”
Barney was done with the whole thing. His life no longer mattered; he was not going to do anything more for the Lockwood. He had done too much as it was. He grabbed a length of rope from the scaffold by the stairwell and headed for “Grand Central” to end the problem. 
________________


Chapter 10
                                     
        Sally was at her wits' end. She believed that Crystal was now dead and Dorian had done it or was involved. She had to find the boy before he did anymore harm. She believed he was at the Lockwood. Her anxiety would be through the roof were it not for how weary she was. 
        Deputy Thomas walked from her vehicle over to where Deputies Hart and Stussie were parked. Standing by the car was Deputy Bernedette Hart. Sally had known her since before she joined the Sheriff’s Department. If anyone was going to be willing to go along with her, Sally felt Bernie was the one. 
        “Bernie, can I talk with you for a minute?”
        “Okay.”
        Bernie followed Sally as she walked toward the Brendon home away from the other officers on site.
        “What’s up?”
        “I need your help. I believe Dorian Brendon is over at the Lockwood. I think he is on the roof trying to summon a demon. I obviously don’t think he can do that, but he does. We already know Crystal Osborn is probably the victim of the car wreck. The only way I think she ended up behind that cabin is that Dorian pulled the car there. Why would he do that if he didn’t have something to do with her death?”
        “Okay, I agree with everything you said. What kind of help do you need from me?”
        “I need backup when I go to the Lockwood. I need you to go along with me.”
        “I think we will all be moving over there when it gets light.”
        “That could be too late. We need to go there now. I think if we wait, we will just find bodies.”
        “That sounds a little dramatic, don’t you think?”
        “I’ve seen two deputies slaughtered, a missing girl killed and burned, and discovered kids are going to try to summon a demon today. Are you really saying I’m being dramatic?”
        “Good point, sorry. I am just not sure we should head over there until we are in full force.”
        “I have been working for twenty-four hours on this case. Every step of the way, I have been behind. I am done doing that. If you won’t come with me, I’ll just go on my own.”
Sally started to walk to her vehicle. Bernie watched for about a minute and then realized she could not allow her to do that.
“Hold on, hold on. I can’t let you go alone. If you’re going to be that stubborn, I'd better go along to keep you out of trouble. Let me go tell Hank what’s going on.” 
“Thanks, Bernie. I’ll be in the vehicle.”
“I’ll be right there.”
Sally climbed into her cruiser to wait for Bernie. She pulled out her notes and tried to read through them to keep herself going. She feared that if she just sat there for a few minutes, she would be sound asleep.
Bernie walked back to where Hank was leaning on their vehicle, watching the wrecked car being analyzed by the forensic team. Most of the deputies on site were waiting for the court order that would allow them to legally search the whole estate. Even though Sally had already done so in what most were concerned was an illegal fashion. 
“Hank, I’m going to be leaving with Sally. She wants to do a walk-through of the Lockwood. She thinks the Brendon kid is over there with his occult stuff.”
“I don’t think you should be following her leads right now. I’m afraid she is way out of the box right now. The missing kids had her on edge, and now with the deputies brutally murdered under her watch and the Osborn girl dead, she is not thinking straight.”
“Unless we take her into custody, she is going with me or without. I can’t let her walk into something she can’t handle alone.”
“Can you talk her into waiting until we all go? It should only be a couple of more hours.”
“That’s the problem. She feels she has been one step behind all along, waiting is what she won’t do.”
“Okay, I will do my best to get released here, and I’ll be over as soon as I can. You be careful. She is in no condition to be a reliable backup.”
“I got it. See you over there when you can. Sooner if she is right.”
“Okay.”
Bernie started walking to Sally’s vehicle. She was a little concerned because Hank was right, Sally wasn’t herself. She was too tired and emotionally a wreck. If the stuff hit the fan, Bernie wasn’t sure how Sally would react.
It took about fifteen minutes for Sally and Bernie to get to the Lockwood. Bernie wanted to get a better idea of how messed up Sally was.
“What are you thinking we are walking into over there?”
“I don’t know. The Brendon kid’s friends said he intended to get on the roof and perform some kind of ritual that he learned from a book of the occult.”
“You think this kid really believes he can summon a demon?”
“Yes, I think he is deranged and dangerous. I believe he did something to cause the wreck that killed Crystal Osborn. Bobbie and Dan, the kids who were his friends, told me that Dorian sent the other Osborn kid, Ben, somewhere the night they were practicing the ritual. That was the last time anyone saw him. It could have been a trap Dorian had set up to take Ben. I think he's smart, has resources, and is psychopathic.”
“That is a lot for a kid, who is what, seventeen or eighteen?”
“That is plenty old enough to kill. It is not like that doesn’t happen every day somewhere. It just happens to be in sleepy Eau Claire County this time.”
“I suppose that is true.”
Bernie went quiet. Sally wasn’t wrong, but it seemed like such a leap for a boy who had done some small-time mischief to become a cold-blooded killer. Was he really someone who was able to create such intricate schemes?
She looked at Sally. Her face was drawn, her eyes seemed sunken in and dark. It was obvious the woman was running on fumes at this point. Bernie thought she should just be driving her colleague home, not walking into god knows what at an abandoned monstrosity like the Lockwood.
As the cruiser neared the gate of the property, Bernie asked Sally to stop.
“I think we need to have a plan of action that we can agree upon.”
“Fine, but every minute counts here.”
Sally was in no mood to take her time. She had been anxious to get over here for sometime and wasting time now was just frustrating.
“Listen, I don’t want to go in half-cocked and end up in trouble right away. We need to stick together and cover the place as a team. If you can’t agree to that, I’m out right now. We are here to protect the public, but our safety is important too.”
Sally understood Bernie’s concern. She didn’t like the limitations. In her mind, they needed to move quickly, but that may be very risky. Risk was just not concerning her. She wanted some answers, and she wanted them now! But arguing was just going to take more time.
“Fine, let's go. Okay?”
“Okay.”
Bernie looked at Sally. She was not sure how much agreement they really had. She was going to have to be extra careful; she was not sure she could trust Sally.
The gate was locked, so Bernie stepped out and retrieved a bolt cutter from the back of the vehicle. That quickly snapped the chain holding the gate closed. She swung the gate open and reentered the cruiser.
“Let’s go.”
“I’ll pull up over there. Barney should be here somewhere. I hope he is in his apartment here at the end of the east wing.”
“Barney? You mean the guy who is the caretaker out here? Barney D… o… y… l …e?”
Bernie had spelled the last name to make the point that his last name started with DO, the letters Fred had written in the dirt before his death.
“Yes.”        
“You still don’t think he could be involved? All of these things are happening within fifteen minutes of this place. It is like all roads lead here, and the only person that is here is your man Barney. I know you don’t want to think he could do it, but there was his brother and then his wife.”
“Nothing was ever proved about either one of them. He was never even charged. You’re making too much out of the bad luck he has had in his life. The man I know would never do anything to harm someone.”
“That does not sound like Sally the sheriff’s deputy; that sounds like Sally the potential girlfriend.”
“That’s not fair. I’ve been working on this case from the start. I should know who is a suspect and who is not.”
“You should, but I think you are blinded by other factors.”
“We’re here. That RV seems to be parked right in front of the door. I don’t know what that is all about.”
Sally tried to jump out of the vehicle, but Bernie grabbed her arm.
“Hey, slow down, this is what I’m talking about. We need to go as a team!”
“Well, get out, let’s go.”
“Fine, but we do this together.”
“Fine, fine, come on.”
The two deputies exited the vehicle. Bernie rushed around to the driver's side, and the two of them walked toward the door to Barney’s apartment. Sally knocked on the door. There was no answer. Bernie peeked in the side window.
“There are lights on in there.”
Sally knocked again louder.
“Barney, are you in there? It is me, Sally, Deputy Sally Thomas.”
Sally used her full name and title for Bernie’s benefit. She knew her colleague already suspected she was biased about how improbable Barney was involved in anything that had happened.
“It doesn’t look like he is in there, Sally. Now what?”
“I guess we check the building.”
“You intend to go inside that place, now, just the two of us?”
“Yes, that is what we do; we’re trained for this, right?”
“Not for ghost hunting, we’re not.”
“Don’t be ridiculous. You’re not saying you believe in all that stuff, are you?”
“Let’s just say, I have a healthy respect for the things I don’t know about.”
“Come on, it’s the living that I have fear of. We’ll head up to the main entrance. We need to check that RV anyway.”
The two of them headed toward the RV with their flashlights directing their path. As they rounded the front of the RV Sally stopped abruptly and placed her arm in front of Bernie to stop her as well.
        “What is it?”
“A body! There in front of the RV door.”
“Who is it?”
“I don’t know.”
They walked closer and saw the remains of Nick, the RV driver and assistant to the Paranormal Posse. 
“He has been …”
“Yes, Bernie, I can see. Do you still think this was a waste of time?”
“I never said it was a waste of time. I was just concerned you were not seeing all of the suspects clearly, and we should wait until more of us could get here. I still think that would be wise. This is now a murder scene.”
“I agree it is a murder scene. I don’t think we can wait. If the Brendon boy is here, he is either a suspect or in danger.”
“Are you saying you still want to go to that place after seeing what someone did to that guy?”
“Whoever did that was the same person who killed Fred, Larry, and from what forensic said, Dober as well. That is four bodies within twenty-four hours. What makes you think they are done? From the looks of the RV, I think this was a group here investigating the haunted sanatorium. That means there are more people inside who may need help. I also think that boy is in there. Crazy or not, he is our responsibility too.”
“Okay, but I’m calling this in and naming all of the possible suspects. That includes Barney, like or not.”
“Fine, just hurry up, I’ll check the front door.”
Bernie headed to the cruiser and grabbed the radio.
“Hart to Dispatch.”
“Go for Dispatch.”
“We have another body at the Lockwood Sanatorium. It is the same m.o. As the victims at Grumby’s. I’m requesting backup.”
“Another one? I’ll get backup on the way. We are pretty stretched right now. I will be calling in help from the State Patrol and the Dunn County Sheriff. Backup may take a while. Hold until we get someone there.”
Bernie knew there was no holding Sally back, but did not want to reveal her concerns. She didn’t want to get her colleague into any more difficulty than she had already gotten herself.
“We believe there are people in imminent danger. We may need to enter before backup arrives.”
“You’re with Thomas, right?”
“Yes.”
“I think I know what you’re dealing with. Don’t let her get you too deep. Wait for backup.”
“Message received. Out.”
“Dispatch, clear.”
“Sally, dispatch says to hold for backup.”
“I’m not waiting; there are people in there that need our help.”
“How did I know you were going to say that? What are you getting?”
“The door is chained and locked. I need the bolt cutter.”
“So far, we have broken into the property at the gate. That can be covered by the fact that we found the body. Now we are going to break into the building after being told to wait for backup. We are out of bounds here, and I mean all the way.”
“I’m sorry, Bernie, I cannot stand by while more people are subjected to that kind of brutality. I’m going in, alone if necessary.”
“I figured so. Okay, well, I enjoyed this job as long as it lasted. Let’s go if we’re going.”
Sally moved as quickly as her exhausted body could. At the door, she attempted to snap the chain but struggled.
“Here, let’s both work the handles.”
Bernie grabbed hold of the long handles, and the two of them squeezed until the loud clink sounded the break in the chain link.
“Okay, now, if I’m going in, I’m leading. I know how long you have been going today. I don’t trust how quick your reaction time is right now. We will go in with sidearms drawn and stacked. You got it?”
Sally nodded.
“I need verbal confirmation.”
“Yes, I got it.”
“Okay on three. One … two … THREE!”
The two of them burst through the door into the grand entry. Each of them pointed their flashlights around the space, scanning the dark to make sure no one was lurking in the shadows to jump them. Once they were sure the space was clear behind them, they walked in tandem to the central area under the stairwell, where the first floor halls converged at the main building. 
They stood and pointed their lights down each of the hallways, searching for any signs of movement. At that point, they discussed whether they would announce their presence in the building.
“Sally, we should announce who we are and see if we get any responses.”
“I think we should hold off until we see movement somewhere. Otherwise, we may be allowing the killer to escape. We want to catch this guy.”
“Are we going to walk the length of each of these halls? That is going to take too much time.”
“No, I say we check each floor just like we did this one and then decide if we are going to go hallway by hallway.”
“Okay, so let’s hit the second floor.”
“We need to go back to the open staircase on either side of the grand entry.”
“Okay.”
Bernie took a deep breath. It was then she realized that the place was musty and had the scent of death wafting through the air. Creepy didn’t begin to describe the place.
It was uncomfortably cold and darker than the moonless night outside. The shadows were disconcerting. They seemed to all be moving as if inhabited by some long-past souls who were lingering just out of sight. It made the hair on the back of her neck stand on end.


*****


Ben was now standing about six feet behind Dorian. The whispering voice was talking to him.
“He is there in front of you, the one who caused your pain. You can end the pain and take him with you. Do it. Do it now!”
Ben could hear Dorian mumbling. He could not see him as he stood on the roof of the Lockwood between the twin gargoyles that stood vigil above the front entrance, but he could hear him talking.
“I must have the power. I did everything right … I’m immortal …” 
Dorian’s mumbling changed to speaking and then loudly announcing.
“I can do anything! I am the right hand of Balsadore! I FEAR NO MORTAL FOR I AM CLOAKED IN THE POWER OF MY DARK LORD!”
The sound of Dorian’s self glorification sent a shiver up Ben’s back. He squinted the traumatized muscles of his eyes and let out a guttural scream as he charged toward the sound of Dorian’s voice.
Dorian looked up upon hearing the scream. He had no chance to react before Ben hit him with full force. The two teens were carried by Ben’s momentum to the cap stones of the parapet at the roof’s edge. They rocked out over it, and for a moment seemed to hang teetering on the ledge. Ben fell first, plummeting to the steps below. His life ebbed from the shattered body lying crumpled on the steps. Dorian hung, clinging by one hand from the ridge of the parapet. The shadow figure of the Lockwood hovered above him.
“Please, Lord Balsadore, help me.”
The shadow loomed and consolidated into the form of a man. He wasn’t solid. The translucence of the figure betrayed its ghostly being. He was clothed in midcentury surgeon’s scrubs. He stood looking at the boy pleading for assistance, and began to laugh.   
“Ha ha ha. You fool! You might as well let go. I am not going to help you. I will welcome you as a new resident of the Lockwood. I shall be happy to keep you here under my control forever!”
“Ha ha ha.”
“No, I summoned you. You owe me.”
Dorian's fingers were starting to cramp. He could not hold on with one hand any longer. He attempted to swing his other hand up to catch a second hold, but the pressure that was placed on his grip forced it to give way. He fell with a loud swack onto the concrete below. He died staring up at the evil grin of Doctor Zuwiki.

        “Sally, did you hear something by the front doors?”
        “No, why?”
        “It’s probably just this place. I keep hearing things.”
        “That’s not surprising, the place is old and full of creaks and groans.”
        “It is creepy. We need to get this done and get out of here.”
        The two deputies ascended the stairs. Each step seemed to creak louder than the last, sounding like tiny screams directly from the building. When they reached the top of the steps, they were presented with the grisly sight of another body. 
        “Oh my god, Sally, he is dead. He’s been cut up just like the other one!”
        Sally couldn’t look. Her first thought was that the man on the floor had to be Barney.
        “Can you tell who it is?”
        “No, I’ve never seen him before. He must be one of the investigators who came in the RV.”
        “Okay, that … that  … is terrible, just terrible.”
        Sally had been certain they had found Barney in the same condition as the last two victims. Her chest was nearly exploding as her anxiety had climbed, and her heart rate went into overdrive. Her breathing became difficult as she neared hyperventilation. She had to step back and lean on the wall while she attempted to regain some sort of control. Her state of exhaustion had weakened. The burst of adrenaline when she saw the body and thought it could be Barney was more than she could handle. Sally was a little embarrassed that she was relieved to see a dead body that wasn’t Barney. When she spoke, she was still pretty shaky.
        “The … the killer has gone … scorched earth here. I … I’m afraid this will not be the last … body.”
        “Sally, I know you were afraid this was Barney, but we have now found two more victims, and Barney is nowhere to be found. I think you have to face the fact that he is a prime suspect.”
        Sally’s immediate reaction to Bernie’s words was to lash out at her colleague. She stifled that feeling; she knew Bernie was just using her investigative training and connecting the dots. She just did not understand that the dots were not as clear as she thought. She replied in a measured tone
        “I don’t want to talk about it! If we find evidence that proves that, I will do what I have to do. Until then, I am assuming he is either looking for whoever did this or is hiding from them.”
Bernie looked at Sally and just shook her head. She hoped Barney knew how much faith Sally had in him. He was a lucky guy. She hoped upon hope Sally was right for her own sake.
“So what do we do now?”
“We keep going up after we check the hallways here.”
The two of them pointed their lights down the east wing. They didn’t see anything there. They moved on to the center hallway and did the same. Again, there was nothing to see. When they pointed down the west wing at first, there was no movement. As they were about to abandon that floor and move up to the third, they saw a door move.
“Wait, what was that?”
“Where?”
“About three-quarters of the way down the hall. A door swung open.”
“Did you see anything else?”
“No, just the door.”
“Could it have been the wind?”
“I suppose, but I haven’t felt any wind in here, Bernie. I think we should go down there and check it out.”
“Okay, but let’s do this the smart way. You take the left side, and I’ll take the right. We need to check each room as we move down the hall so that no one can jump out from behind us as we move along. You check a room, and I will watch the hall, then I check a room on my side, and you watch the hall. Do you have that?”
“Yes, I got it. Let’s go.”
The two deputies moved into the hall with Sally on the left and Bernie on the right. When they reached the first room on the left, Sally entered as Bernie acted as a lookout and covered her from the hall. They reversed roles when Bernie checked the first room on the right. They continued in this manner as they moved down the hallway.
They were coming close to the spot where the door had swung open. Bernie was being especially careful as they moved within a few feet of the spot. She whispered to Sally.
“That is the door I saw move. I will move to your side, and we will get on either side of the door.”
The two moved into position, and Bernie spoke.
        “This is the Sheriff’s Department. Come out with your hands in the air, now!”
        There was no sound and no movement. Bernie repeated her order.
“This is the Sheriff’s Department. Come out with your hands in the air, now!”
Once again, there was no response.
Bernie motioned for Sally to follow her into the room. Both deputies had their sidearms drawn and their flashlights in their opposite hands as they stacked for entry. Bernie nodded, and they took a step into the room. A figure moved quickly into the doorway, shoving Bernie to the side. She lost her balance and hit the floor. As Sally tried to fire her weapon, she felt the sting of a knife cut deeply across her left shoulder. She spun around and fired her weapon as the figure ran for the end of the west wing hallway and disappeared into the stairwell. 
Sally pursued into the stairwell, but could not tell if the figure had gone up or down. Not wanting to leave her partner behind, she returned as Bernie was dusting herself off. 
“Did you get a good look at them?”
“No, they came out of the darkness to the left of the door. They were on me before I could react, and then I was on the floor. Did you see them clearly?”
“Not really, they were dressed all in black, I think. They had a hood, maybe a hooded sweatshirt, I couldn’t tell.”
“Did you hit him with your shot?”
“I don’t know. He didn’t slow down or react as if I did, but who knows?”
“Well, we at least know our killer is human and not some kind of spectre.”
